H_{g_{\mathrm{round}}}\equiv 0
.

(b) Standard conformal covariance of
(L^{\circ},B^{\circ})
.

(c) The explicit form (
2.21
) is obtained by setting
u=v
in the Green identity (
2.22
). The lower bound
\|u\|_{E,g}^{2}\geq c\|u\|_{H^{1}(M)}^{2}
is the coercivity assumption (Y
{}^{+}_{\partial}
). The upper bound follows from the explicit form, the boundedness of
\mathrm{Scal}_{g}
and
H_{g}
under (BG
2+
), and the trace inequality.
∎

Lemma 2.21

(Local
H^{1}
control from Dirichlet energy on
\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+}
)
.

Assume
n\geq 3
. For every
R\geq 1
and every
w\in\dot{H}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+})
,

\|w\|_{H^{1}(B_{R}^{+})}^{2}\ \leq\ C(n)\,\big(1+R^{2}\big)\,\|\nabla w\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+})}^{2}.

Proof.

Clearly
\|\nabla w\|_{L^{2}(B_{R}^{+})}\leq\|\nabla w\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+})}
. For the
L^{2}
term, use the Sobolev embedding
\dot{H}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+})\hookrightarrow L^{2^{\ast}}(\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+})
with
2^{\ast}=\frac{2n}{n-2}
and Hölder on
B_{R}^{+}
:

\|w\|_{L^{2}(B_{R}^{+})}\ \leq\ |B_{R}^{+}|^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{2^{\ast}}}\,\|w\|_{L^{2^{\ast}}(\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+})}\ \lesssim\ R\,\|\nabla w\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+})}.

Combining the two bounds yields the desired inequality.
∎

Corollary 2.22

(Local Escobar stability on
\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+}
(covariant))
.

There exist
\delta_{0}>0
and
c_{\mathrm{tr}}>0
such that, if
f\in\dot{H}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+})
obeys

\mathrm{Def}^{\mathrm{Esc}}_{\mathrm{flat}}(f)\leq\delta_{0}
, then for its best-fit
v^{\sharp}
on the optimizer manifold,

\mathrm{Def}^{\mathrm{Esc}}_{\mathrm{flat}}(f)\ \geq\ c_{\mathrm{tr}}\,\|f-v^{\sharp}\|_{E,\mathrm{flat}}^{2},

where here
\|\cdot\|_{E,\mathrm{flat}}
is the covariant graph norm for the flat metric; since
R_{\mathrm{flat}}\equiv 0
and
H_{\mathrm{flat}}\equiv 0
, it reduces to the Dirichlet norm, i.e.

\|\cdot\|_{E,\mathrm{flat}}^{2}=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+}}|\nabla(\cdot)|^{2}
. Moreover, for every
R\geq 1
,

\|f-v^{\sharp}\|_{H^{1}(B_{R}^{+})}^{2}\ \leq\ C(n,R)\,\mathrm{Def}^{\mathrm{Esc}}_{\mathrm{flat}}(f).

Proof.

The first inequality is the conformal transfer of Theorem
2.19
via Lemma
2.20
(b). The local
H^{1}
bound then follows from Lemma
2.21
applied to
w=f-v^{\sharp}\in\dot{H}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+})
.
∎

2.4.
Intrinsic coercivity and stability on
(M,g)

Theorem 2.23

(Intrinsic single–bubble coercivity on
(M,g)
)
.

Assume
(Y
{}^{+}_{\partial}
)
and
(BG
2+
)
. Equip
H^{1}(M)
with the covariant weak energy pairing

\langle u,v\rangle_{E,g}:=\int_{M}\!\Big(\nabla u\!\cdot\!\nabla v+\tfrac{1}{a_{n}}\mathrm{Scal}_{g}\,uv\Big)\,dV_{g}+\tfrac{n-2}{2}\int_{\partial M}\!H_{g}\,uv\,d\sigma_{g},\qquad\|u\|_{E,g}^{2}:=\langle u,u\rangle_{E,g}.

Then there exist
\varepsilon_{0}>0
and
c_{\ast}>0
(depending only on
n
and the
(BG
2+
)
,
(Y
{}^{+}_{\partial}
)
data of
(M,g)
) such that for every
x\in\partial M
, every
\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_{0}]
, and every
w\in H^{1}(M)
obeying the
constrained orthogonality

(2.23)

\langle w,\partial_{\varepsilon}U_{+,x,\varepsilon}\rangle_{E,g}=\langle w,\nabla_{x}U_{+,x,\varepsilon}\rangle_{E,g}=0,\qquad\int_{\partial M}(U_{+,x,\varepsilon})^{q-1}w\,d\sigma_{g}=0,\quad q=\tfrac{2(n-1)}{n-2},

(one orthogonality condition in the weak pairing for each modulation direction and one boundary constraint fixing the amplitude) one has

(2.24)

\big\langle D^{2}\mathcal{J}_{g}[U_{+,x,\varepsilon}]\,w,\ w\big\rangle\ \geq\ c_{\ast}\,\|w\|_{E,g}^{2}\ \simeq\ \|w\|_{H^{1}(M)}^{2}.

Proof (via blow-up and variational convergence).

We argue by contradiction. Suppose there exist
x_{k}\in\partial M
,
\varepsilon_{k}\downarrow 0
, and
w_{k}\in H^{1}(M)
such that

(2.25)

\|w_{k}\|_{E,g}=1,\qquad\eqref{eq:esc-orthog-gauge-final}\ \text{ holds with }(x,\varepsilon)=(x_{k},\varepsilon_{k}),\qquad\big\langle D^{2}\mathcal{J}_{g}[U_{+,x_{k},\varepsilon_{k}}]\,w_{k},\ w_{k}\big\rangle\longrightarrow 0.

Step 1 (Localization in a Fermi ball around the bubble).

Fix once and for all a smooth tangentially radial cutoff
\chi\in C_{c}^{\infty}(B_{2}^{+})
with
\chi\equiv 1
on
B_{1}^{+}
. For
R\geq 1
and each
k
, define

\chi_{k,R}(z):=\chi\!\left(\frac{\Phi_{x_{k}}^{-1}(z)}{R\varepsilon_{k}}\right),

supported in the Fermi ball
\Phi_{x_{k}}(R\varepsilon_{k}B_{2}^{+})
and equal to
1
on
\Phi_{x_{k}}(R\varepsilon_{k}B_{1}^{+})
. Decompose

w_{k}^{\mathrm{loc}}:=\chi_{k,R}w_{k},\qquad w_{k}^{\mathrm{far}}:=(1-\chi_{k,R})w_{k}.

A direct computation of the second variation of
\mathcal{J}_{g}
at
U_{+,x_{k},\varepsilon_{k}}
shows that, on the constraint slice

\int_{\partial M}(U_{+,x_{k},\varepsilon_{k}})^{q-1}h\,d\sigma_{g}=0
, one can write

\big\langle D^{2}\mathcal{J}_{g}[U_{+,x_{k},\varepsilon_{k}}]\,h,\ h\big\rangle=c_{0}\,\mathcal{N}_{g}^{\circ}(h,h)\;-\;C_{0}\int_{\partial M}(U_{+,x_{k},\varepsilon_{k}})^{q-2}h^{2}\,d\sigma_{g},

with constants
c_{0},C_{0}>0
depending only on
n
and the (BG
2+
) data (in particular, independent of
k
). Here the first term is strictly coercive by (Y
{}^{+}_{\partial}
), i.e.

\mathcal{N}_{g}^{\circ}(h,h)\ \geq\ c_{Y}\,\|h\|_{H^{1}(M)}^{2}\ \simeq\ \|h\|_{E,g}^{2}.

Up to projecting
w_{k}^{\mathrm{far}}
onto the constraint slice
\int_{\partial M}(U_{+,x_{k},\varepsilon_{k}})^{q-1}h\,d\sigma_{g}=0
(which changes it by
o_{R}(1)\|w_{k}^{\mathrm{far}}\|_{E,g}
since
U_{+}(\cdot,0)\in L^{q}(\mathbb{R}^{n-1})
on the far region), we may apply this representation with
h=w_{k}^{\mathrm{far}}
. On the support of
w_{k}^{\mathrm{far}}
in the Fermi collar of
x_{k}
, the boundary weight
(U_{+,x_{k},\varepsilon_{k}})^{q-2}
is small in
L^{n-1}
once
R
is large. More precisely, by the Fermi change of variables on
\partial M
and the definition of the boundary bubble
U_{+,x_{k},\varepsilon_{k}}=\mathcal{T}_{x_{k},\varepsilon_{k}}U_{+}
,

\int_{\partial M\cap\{\mathrm{dist}_{g}(\cdot,x_{k})\geq R\varepsilon_{k}\}}\bigl|(U_{+,x_{k},\varepsilon_{k}})^{q-2}\bigr|^{n-1}\,d\sigma_{g}\;\simeq\;\int_{\{|y^{\prime}|\geq R\}}U_{+}(\cdot,0)^{q}\,dy^{\prime},

and the right-hand side tends to
0
as
R\to\infty
because
U_{+}(\cdot,0)\in L^{q}(\mathbb{R}^{n-1})
. In particular,

\big\|(U_{+,x_{k},\varepsilon_{k}})^{q-2}\,\mathbf{1}_{\{\mathrm{dist}_{g}(\cdot,x_{k})\geq R\varepsilon_{k}\}}\big\|_{L^{n-1}(\partial M)}\ \leq\ \eta(R),

with
\eta(R)\to 0
as
R\to\infty
, uniformly in
k
.

Using Hölder on the boundary and the critical trace inequality

\|h(\cdot)\|_{L^{q}(\partial M)}\lesssim\|h\|_{H^{1}(M)}
(Lemma
2.2
), we obtain, for the far piece
h=w_{k}^{\mathrm{far}}
,

\int_{\partial M}(U_{+,x_{k},\varepsilon_{k}})^{q-2}h^{2}\ \leq\ \eta(R)\,\|h(\cdot)\|_{L^{q}(\partial M)}^{2}\ \lesssim\ \eta(R)\,\|h\|_{H^{1}(M)}^{2}\ \lesssim\ \eta(R)\,\|h\|_{E,g}^{2}.

Combining this with the coercivity of
\mathcal{N}_{g}^{\circ}
yields

(2.26)

\big\langle D^{2}\mathcal{J}_{g}[U_{+,x_{k},\varepsilon_{k}}]\,w_{k}^{\mathrm{far}},\ w_{k}^{\mathrm{far}}\big\rangle\ \geq\ \bigl(c_{Y}-C_{0}\eta(R)\bigr)\,\|w_{k}^{\mathrm{far}}\|_{E,g}^{2}.

Choosing
R
sufficiently large so that
C_{0}\eta(R)\leq c_{Y}/2
, we obtain

\big\langle D^{2}\mathcal{J}_{g}[U_{+,x_{k},\varepsilon_{k}}]\,w_{k}^{\mathrm{far}},\ w_{k}^{\mathrm{far}}\big\rangle\ \geq\ \tfrac{c_{Y}}{2}\,\|w_{k}^{\mathrm{far}}\|_{E,g}^{2}.

The mixed terms involving
w_{k}^{\mathrm{loc}}
and
w_{k}^{\mathrm{far}}
are supported in the thin annulus where
\nabla\chi_{k,R}
is nonzero; standard estimates using the boundedness of the coefficients in
D^{2}\mathcal{J}_{g}
and the small measure of this annulus (of order
R^{n-1}\varepsilon_{k}^{n-1}
) show that their contribution can be made arbitrarily small as
k\to\infty
, uniformly in
R
. Thus, if
\limsup_{k}\|w_{k}^{\mathrm{far}}\|_{E,g}>0
, then for large
R
and all
k
large enough the far part alone would give a strictly positive lower bound on
\langle D^{2}\mathcal{J}_{g}[U_{+,x_{k},\varepsilon_{k}}]\,w_{k},w_{k}\rangle
, contradicting (
2.25
). Hence, after fixing a sufficiently large
R
once and for all, we may assume (up to extracting a subsequence) that

\|w_{k}^{\mathrm{far}}\|_{E,g}\longrightarrow 0,\qquad\|w_{k}^{\mathrm{loc}}\|_{E,g}\longrightarrow 1.

Replacing
w_{k}
by
w_{k}^{\mathrm{loc}}
and renormalizing by a factor
1+o(1)
(which preserves (
2.25
) and keeps the orthogonality constraints (
2.23
) up to
o(1)
errors), we may and do assume that each
w_{k}
is supported in a fixed Fermi ball
\Phi_{x_{k}}(R\varepsilon_{k}B_{2}^{+})
and still satisfies

\|w_{k}\|_{E,g}=1,\qquad\big\langle D^{2}\mathcal{J}_{g}[U_{+,x_{k},\varepsilon_{k}}]\,w_{k},\ w_{k}\big\rangle\longrightarrow 0,

together with the orthogonality conditions (
2.23
) up to
o(1)
errors.

Step 2 (Rescaling to the half-space and flat bounds).

Fix the Fermi charts
\Phi_{x_{k}}
provided by (BG
2+
) on a fixed collar. For
y=(y^{\prime},y_{n})\in\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+}
set

W_{k}(y)\ :=\ \varepsilon_{k}^{\frac{n-2}{2}}\ w_{k}\big(\Phi_{x_{k}}(\varepsilon_{k}y)\big),\qquad G_{k}(y)\ :=\ \Phi_{x_{k}}^{\ast}g\,(\varepsilon_{k}y).

Since
w_{k}
is supported in
\Phi_{x_{k}}(R\varepsilon_{k}B_{2}^{+})
, the rescaled functions
W_{k}
are supported in
B_{2R}^{+}\subset\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+}
, uniformly in
k
. By the
C^{2}
Fermi expansions (Lemma
2.9
and Lemma
2.8
) and (BG
2+
), the metrics
G_{k}
converge to the Euclidean metric in
C^{2}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\overline{\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+}})
, and the scalar and mean curvatures satisfy
R_{G_{k}}\to 0
,
H_{G_{k}}\to 0
locally.

Using the explicit graph norm formula (Lemma
2.20
(c)) together with (BG
2+
), the support property of
w_{k}
, and a change of variables as in Lemma
2.9
, one finds constants
0<c_{0}\leq C_{0}<\infty
, independent of
k
, such that

(2.27)

c_{0}\ \leq\ \|W_{k}\|_{E,\mathrm{flat}}^{2}=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+}}|\nabla W_{k}|^{2}\,dy\ \leq\ C_{0}.

In particular,
(W_{k})
is bounded in
\dot{H}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+})
and hence in
H^{1}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\overline{\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+}})
by Lemma
2.21
.

The rescaled bubble profiles

U^{\mathrm{res}}_{k}(y):=\varepsilon_{k}^{\frac{n-2}{2}}\,U_{+,x_{k},\varepsilon_{k}}\big(\Phi_{x_{k}}(\varepsilon_{k}y)\big)

satisfy

U^{\mathrm{res}}_{k}(y)=U_{+}(y)\quad\text{for all }y\in B_{2R}^{+},

by the definition of the Fermi pushforward
U_{+,x,\varepsilon}=\mathcal{T}_{x,\varepsilon}U_{+}
. The orthogonality conditions (
2.23
) transfer, via the same change of variables and the
C^{2}
Fermi expansions, to approximate flat orthogonality: for each tangent mode
Z\in E_{\flat}:=T_{U_{+}}\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{Esc,flat}}
,

(2.28)

\langle W_{k},Z\rangle_{E,\mathrm{flat}}=o(1),\qquad\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n-1}}U_{+}^{\,q-1}(\cdot,0)\,W_{k}(\cdot,0)\,dy^{\prime}=o(1),

where
o(1)\to 0
as
k\to\infty
. Thus
W_{k}
is asymptotically orthogonal to
E_{\flat}
in the graph pairing and to the amplitude direction in the constraint.

Step 3 (Variational convergence of the Hessians and stability of the spectral gap).

Let
\mathcal{Q}_{\flat}
denote the constrained second variation at
U_{+}
for the flat Escobar functional, i.e. the quadratic form

\mathcal{Q}_{\flat}[\psi]:=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+}}|\nabla\psi|^{2}\,dy-(q-1)\lambda_{\ast}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n-1}}U_{+}^{\,q-2}(\cdot,0)\,\psi(\cdot,0)^{2}\,dy^{\prime},

where
\lambda_{\ast}=S_{\ast}
and the constraint is
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n-1}}U_{+}^{q-1}\psi(\cdot,0)=0
(cf. Lemma
2.17
, Proposition
2.18
, and Lemma
2.20
(b)). The weight
U_{+}^{\,q-2}(\cdot,0)
decays like
|y^{\prime}|^{-2}
at infinity, so
U_{+}^{\,q-2}\in L^{n-1}(\mathbb{R}^{n-1})
, and the trace inequality
\|\psi(\cdot,0)\|_{L^{q}(\mathbb{R}^{n-1})}\lesssim\|\psi\|_{H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+})}
with
q=\frac{2(n-1)}{n-2}
shows that
\mathcal{Q}_{\flat}
is a bounded quadratic form on
H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+})
.

Let
\mathcal{Q}_{k}
denote the quadratic forms obtained by pulling back the constrained second variation at
U_{+,x_{k},\varepsilon_{k}}
to the rescaled variables
y
with the metric
G_{k}
, and then restricting to functions supported in
B_{2R}^{+}
. Using the Fermi expansions and (BG
2+
), one checks that

(2.29)

\mathcal{Q}_{k}[\psi]\ =\ \mathcal{Q}_{\flat}[\psi]\ +\ \mathcal{R}_{k}[\psi],\qquad\psi\in H^{1}(B_{2R}^{+}),

where
\mathcal{R}_{k}
is a quadratic form satisfying, for some
\eta_{k}\to 0
,

(2.30)

|\mathcal{R}_{k}[\psi]|\ \leq\ \eta_{k}\,\|\psi\|_{E,\mathrm{flat}}^{2}\qquad\forall\,\psi\in H^{1}(B_{2R}^{+}).

This uses the facts that
G_{k}\to\mathrm{Id}
in
C^{2}_{\mathrm{loc}}
,
R_{G_{k}},H_{G_{k}}\to 0
locally, that
U_{k}^{\mathrm{res}}=U_{+}
exactly, and that
\lambda_{k}:=\mathcal{J}_{g}[U_{+,x_{k},\varepsilon_{k}}]
satisfies
\lambda_{k}\to\lambda_{\ast}
by the one-bubble first-order expansion (uniformly in
x
under (BG
2+
)).

In particular, the forms
\mathcal{Q}_{k}

Mosco-converge
to the restriction of
\mathcal{Q}_{\flat}
on
H^{1}(B_{2R}^{+})
: if
\psi_{k}\rightharpoonup\psi
weakly in
H^{1}(B_{2R}^{+})
, then

\liminf_{k\to\infty}\mathcal{Q}_{k}[\psi_{k}]\ \geq\ \mathcal{Q}_{\flat}[\psi],

and for every fixed
\psi\in H^{1}(B_{2R}^{+})
the constant sequence
\psi_{k}\equiv\psi
satisfies

\limsup_{k\to\infty}\mathcal{Q}_{k}[\psi_{k}]\ \leq\ \mathcal{Q}_{\flat}[\psi],

thanks to (
2.29
)–(
2.30
) and the continuity of the trace map and of the
U_{+}^{q-2}
-weighted boundary term.

Let
E_{k}
be the pulled-back tangent spaces at
U_{+,x_{k},\varepsilon_{k}}
in the
y
-variables, restricted to
B_{2R}^{+}
. By the
C^{2}_{\mathrm{loc}}
convergence of the metrics and of the optimizer manifold, the finite-dimensional spaces
E_{k}
converge to
E_{\flat}
in the gap topology. The constraint slices in the rescaled variables have the form

T_{k}:=\Big\{\psi\in H^{1}(B_{2R}^{+}):\ \int_{\mathbb{R}^{n-1}}U_{+}^{\,q-1}(\cdot,0)\,\psi(\cdot,0)\,(1+\theta_{k}(y^{\prime}))\,dy^{\prime}=0\Big\},

where
\theta_{k}(y^{\prime})\to 0
uniformly on compact sets, coming from the boundary Jacobian expansion (Lemma
2.10
). Thus
T_{k}
converges to

T_{\flat}:=\Big\{\psi\in H^{1}(B_{2R}^{+}):\ \int_{\mathbb{R}^{n-1}}U_{+}^{\,q-1}(\cdot,0)\,\psi(\cdot,0)\,dy^{\prime}=0\Big\}

in the gap topology as
k\to\infty
.

By Proposition
2.18
(the Robin spectral gap on the hemisphere, transferred to the half-space via Lemma
2.20
(b)), there exists
\gamma_{0}>0
such that

\mathcal{Q}_{\flat}[\psi]\ \geq\ \gamma_{0}\,\|\psi\|_{E,\mathrm{flat}}^{2}\quad\forall\ \psi\in T_{\flat}\cap E_{\flat}^{\perp}.

Since this inequality holds for
all
such
\psi
, it remains valid in particular for those supported in
B_{2R}^{+}
. The Mosco convergence of
\mathcal{Q}_{k}
to
\mathcal{Q}_{\flat}
on
H^{1}(B_{2R}^{+})
, together with the gap convergence
E_{k}\to E_{\flat}
,
T_{k}\to T_{\flat}
, implies (by the minimax principle for quadratic forms) that there exists
k_{0}
such that, for all
k\geq k_{0}
,

(2.31)

\mathcal{Q}_{k}[\psi]\ \geq\ \tfrac{\gamma_{0}}{2}\,\|\psi\|_{E,\mathrm{flat}}^{2}\qquad\text{for all }\ \psi\in T_{k}\cap E_{k}^{\perp}.

Step 4 (Projection onto the constrained orthogonal complement and contradiction).

By (
2.28
) and the convergence of
E_{k}
,
T_{k}
to
E_{\flat}
,
T_{\flat}
, the defects of
W_{k}
with respect to the constraint and symmetry spaces are
o(1)
in the
\|\cdot\|_{E,\mathrm{flat}}
-topology. Let
W_{k}^{\perp}
denote the orthogonal projection of
W_{k}
onto
T_{k}\cap E_{k}^{\perp}
with respect to the flat graph pairing
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle_{E,\mathrm{flat}}=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+}}\nabla\cdot\nabla
. Then

(2.32)

\|W_{k}^{\perp}-W_{k}\|_{E,\mathrm{flat}}=o(1),\qquad\|W_{k}^{\perp}\|_{E,\mathrm{flat}}^{2}\ \geq\ \tfrac{c_{0}}{2}

for all sufficiently large
k
, where
c_{0}
is as in (
2.27
). The lower bound follows from

\|W_{k}^{\perp}\|_{E,\mathrm{flat}}\ \geq\ \|W_{k}\|_{E,\mathrm{flat}}-\|W_{k}^{\perp}-W_{k}\|_{E,\mathrm{flat}}\ \geq\ c_{0}^{1/2}-o(1).

By construction of
\mathcal{Q}_{k}
as the pulled-back constrained Hessian, we have

\mathcal{Q}_{k}[W_{k}]\ =\ \big\langle D^{2}\mathcal{J}_{g}[U_{+,x_{k},\varepsilon_{k}}]\,w_{k},\ w_{k}\big\rangle.

Since
\mathcal{Q}_{k}
is continuous with respect to
\|\cdot\|_{E,\mathrm{flat}}
, the projection error in (
2.32
) yields

(2.33)

\big|\mathcal{Q}_{k}[W_{k}]-\mathcal{Q}_{k}[W_{k}^{\perp}]\big|\ \lesssim\ \|W_{k}-W_{k}^{\perp}\|_{E,\mathrm{flat}}\big(\|W_{k}\|_{E,\mathrm{flat}}+\|W_{k}^{\perp}\|_{E,\mathrm{flat}}\big)\ =\ o(1).

Combining (
2.31
), (
2.32
) and (
2.33
) gives, for all large
k
,

\mathcal{Q}_{k}[W_{k}]\ \geq\ \tfrac{\gamma_{0}}{2}\,\|W_{k}^{\perp}\|_{E,\mathrm{flat}}^{2}-o(1)\ \geq\ \tfrac{\gamma_{0}}{4}\,c_{0}-o(1),

which contradicts (
2.25
) (since
\mathcal{Q}_{k}[W_{k}]=\langle D^{2}\mathcal{J}_{g}[U_{+,x_{k},\varepsilon_{k}}]w_{k},w_{k}\rangle\to 0
by assumption).

This contradiction shows that (
2.24
) holds with some
c_{\ast}>0
for all sufficiently small
\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_{0}]
, uniformly in
x\in\partial M
. Finally, the equivalence between
\|\cdot\|_{E,g}
and
\|\cdot\|_{H^{1}(M)}
under (BG
2+
) and (Y
{}^{+}_{\partial}
) (Lemma
2.20
(c)) yields the last comparison in (
2.24
).
∎

Theorem 2.24

(Quantitative Escobar stability on
(M,g)
, covariant)
.

We use the covariant weak graph norm

\langle\phi,\psi\rangle_{E,g}:=\mathcal{N}_{g}^{\circ}(\phi,\psi)=\!\!\int_{M}\!\Big(\nabla\phi\cdot\nabla\psi+\tfrac{1}{a_{n}}\mathrm{Scal}_{g}\,\phi\psi\Big)\,dV_{g}+\tfrac{n-2}{2}\!\int_{\partial M}\!H_{g}\,\phi\psi\,d\sigma_{g},\,\,\|\phi\|_{E,g}^{2}\!:=\!\langle\phi,\phi\rangle_{E,g}.

Assume
(Y
{}^{+}_{\partial}
)
and
(BG
2+
)
. There exist
\delta_{0}>0
,
\varepsilon_{0}>0
,
C>0
, and
C_{\mathrm{geo}}>0
(depending only on
(M,g,n)
) with the following property.
If
u\in H^{1}(M)
satisfies
\|u\|_{L^{q}(\partial M)}=1
with
q=2^{*}_{\partial}=\tfrac{2(n-1)}{n-2}
,
\mathrm{Def}^{\mathrm{Esc}}_{g}(u)\leq\delta_{0}
, and its best–fit
scaled
boundary bubble

v^{\sharp}=a^{\sharp}\,U_{+,x^{\sharp},\varepsilon^{\sharp}}
(given by Lemma
2.16

with the
covariant weak
graph inner product and the boundary
L^{q}
-slice, so that

a^{\sharp}>0
and
(x^{\sharp},\varepsilon^{\sharp})
are uniquely determined) has scale
\varepsilon^{\sharp}\in(0,\varepsilon_{0})
, then

(2.34)

\|u-v^{\sharp}\|_{E,g}^{2}\ \leq\ C\,\mathrm{Def}^{\mathrm{Esc}}_{g}(u)\ +\ C_{\mathrm{geo}}\,(\varepsilon^{\sharp})^{2},

and hence, by graph/H
1
norm equivalence on bounded geometry,

\|u-v^{\sharp}\|_{H^{1}(M)}^{2}\ \lesssim\ \mathrm{Def}^{\mathrm{Esc}}_{g}(u)\ +\ C_{\mathrm{geo}}\,(\varepsilon^{\sharp})^{2}.

Proof.

We work throughout with the
covariant
Escobar quotient and the weak graph inner product

\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle_{E,g}
.

By Lemma
2.16
(applied with the inner product
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle_{E,g}
and the boundary
L^{q}
constraint), for
u
in the small–deficit regime we may write

u=v^{\sharp}+w,\qquad w\ \perp\ \mathrm{span}\{\partial_{\varepsilon}U_{+,x^{\sharp},\varepsilon^{\sharp}},\ \nabla_{x}U_{+,x^{\sharp},\varepsilon^{\sharp}}\}\ \text{ in }\ \langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle_{E,g},\qquad\int_{\partial M}(v^{\sharp})^{q-1}w\,d\sigma_{g}=0,

where
q=2^{*}_{\partial}=\tfrac{2(n-1)}{n-2}
and
v^{\sharp}=a^{\sharp}\,U_{+,x^{\sharp},\varepsilon^{\sharp}}
.

Step 1 (Taylor expansion on the constraint slice).

Set

\mathcal{J}_{g}[F]:=\frac{\mathcal{N}_{g}^{\circ}(F)}{\|F\|_{L^{q}(\partial M)}^{2}},\qquad q=\tfrac{2(n-1)}{n-2},

and define the constraint slice at
v^{\sharp}
by

\mathcal{S}_{v^{\sharp}}:=\Big\{F\in H^{1}(M):\ \|F\|_{L^{q}(\partial M)}=\|v^{\sharp}\|_{L^{q}(\partial M)}\Big\}.

By the variation of the
L^{q}
norm,

\frac{d}{dt}\Big|_{t=0}\|v^{\sharp}+tw\|_{L^{q}(\partial M)}^{q}=q\int_{\partial M}(v^{\sharp})^{q-1}w\,d\sigma_{g},

so the amplitude constraint
\int_{\partial M}(v^{\sharp})^{q-1}w=0
says exactly that
w
is tangent to
\mathcal{S}_{v^{\sharp}}
at
v^{\sharp}
.

Under (BG
2+
) (bounded geometry and the global trace embedding), the functional
\mathcal{J}_{g}
is
C^{2}
near
v^{\sharp}
. Hence a Taylor expansion of
\mathcal{J}_{g}
at
v^{\sharp}
yields

(2.35)

\mathcal{J}_{g}[u]-\mathcal{J}_{g}[v^{\sharp}]=\underbrace{\langle D\mathcal{J}_{g}[v^{\sharp}],\,w\rangle_{E,g}}_{=:L[v^{\sharp}](w)}+\tfrac{1}{2}\,\underbrace{\langle D^{2}\mathcal{J}_{g}[v^{\sharp}]\,w,\,w\rangle_{E,g}}_{=:Q_{v^{\sharp}}[w]}+\mathcal{R}(v^{\sharp};w),

where the remainder satisfies

(2.36)

|\mathcal{R}(v^{\sharp};w)|\ \leq\ C\,\|w\|_{E,g}^{\,p},\qquad p:=\min\{3,q\}

whenever
\|w\|_{E,g}
is sufficiently small (with
C
depending only on the geometry and on
n
).

Step 2 (Coercivity of the quadratic term).

By Theorem
2.23
(the intrinsic single–bubble coercivity, stated precisely for the constrained Hessian of
\mathcal{J}_{g}
in the covariant gauge), there exist
c_{\ast}>0
and
\varepsilon_{0}>0
such that, whenever
\varepsilon^{\sharp}\in(0,\varepsilon_{0}]
,

(2.37)

Q_{v^{\sharp}}[w]\ \geq\ c_{\ast}\,\|w\|_{E,g}^{2},

for every
w\in H^{1}(M)
satisfying the orthogonality conditions of Lemma
2.16
(i.e. those in (
2.23
) with
(x,\varepsilon)=(x^{\sharp},\varepsilon^{\sharp})
). The constants
c_{\ast}
and
\varepsilon_{0}
depend only on the (BG
2+
), (Y
{}^{+}_{\partial}
) data and on
n
.

Step 3 (First–order drift is
O(\varepsilon^{\sharp})
along the slice).

We claim that there exists
C_{\mathrm{geo}}>0
(depending only on
(M,g,n)
) such that

(2.38)

\big|L[v^{\sharp}](\psi)\big|\ \leq\ C_{\mathrm{geo}}\,\varepsilon^{\sharp}\,\|\psi\|_{E,g}\quad\text{for all }\ \psi\in T_{v^{\sharp}}\mathcal{S}_{v^{\sharp}},

whenever
\varepsilon^{\sharp}\in(0,\varepsilon_{0}]
.

This is precisely Lemma
2.25
below: the bubble
v^{\sharp}
is an exact constrained critical point of the covariant Escobar quotient for the flat model, and the geometric perturbation of
(L_{g}^{\circ},B_{g}^{\circ})
relative to the flat pair
(L_{\mathrm{flat}}^{\circ},B_{\mathrm{flat}}^{\circ})
produces only an
O(\varepsilon^{\sharp})
error in the dual
E,g
–norm. We refer to Lemma
2.25
for the detailed argument.

Step 4 (From the expansion to a quantitative lower bound).

Combining (
2.35
), (
2.37
), (
2.36
), and (
2.38
) gives

\mathcal{J}_{g}[u]-\mathcal{J}_{g}[v^{\sharp}]\ \geq\ -\,C_{\mathrm{geo}}\,\varepsilon^{\sharp}\,\|w\|_{E,g}\ +\ \tfrac{1}{2}c_{\ast}\,\|w\|_{E,g}^{2}\ -\ C\,\|w\|_{E,g}^{\,p}.

We now shrink
\delta_{0}>0
(the deficit threshold) so that in the small–deficit regime the modulation lemma guarantees
\|w\|_{E,g}\leq\eta
with
\eta
so small that the cubic term can be dominated:

C\,\|w\|_{E,g}^{\,p}\ \leq\ \tfrac{c_{\ast}}{4}\,\|w\|_{E,g}^{2}.

Then

\mathcal{J}_{g}[u]-\mathcal{J}_{g}[v^{\sharp}]\ \geq\ -\,C_{\mathrm{geo}}\,\varepsilon^{\sharp}\,\|w\|_{E,g}\ +\ \tfrac{c_{\ast}}{4}\,\|w\|_{E,g}^{2}.

To absorb the linear term in
\|w\|_{E,g}
, use Young’s inequality with a parameter
\alpha>0
:

C_{\mathrm{geo}}\varepsilon^{\sharp}\,\|w\|_{E,g}\ \leq\ \frac{\alpha}{2}\,\|w\|_{E,g}^{2}\ +\ \frac{C_{\mathrm{geo}}^{2}}{2\alpha}\,(\varepsilon^{\sharp})^{2}.

Choosing
\alpha:=\tfrac{c_{\ast}}{4}
, we obtain

C_{\mathrm{geo}}\varepsilon^{\sharp}\,\|w\|_{E,g}\ \leq\ \tfrac{c_{\ast}}{8}\,\|w\|_{E,g}^{2}\ +\ C^{\prime}_{\mathrm{geo}}\,(\varepsilon^{\sharp})^{2}

for some
C^{\prime}_{\mathrm{geo}}
depending only on
C_{\mathrm{geo}}
and
c_{\ast}
. Therefore

(2.39)

\mathcal{J}_{g}[u]-\mathcal{J}_{g}[v^{\sharp}]\ \geq\ \Big(\tfrac{c_{\ast}}{4}-\tfrac{c_{\ast}}{8}\Big)\|w\|_{E,g}^{2}\ -\ C^{\prime}_{\mathrm{geo}}\,(\varepsilon^{\sharp})^{2}\ =\ \tfrac{c_{\ast}}{8}\,\|w\|_{E,g}^{2}\ -\ C^{\prime}_{\mathrm{geo}}\,(\varepsilon^{\sharp})^{2}.

Step 5 (Comparison to the deficit).

By definition,
C^{\ast}_{\mathrm{Esc}}(M,g)
is the
infimum
of
\mathcal{J}_{g}
over
H^{1}(M)\setminus\{0\}
, so in particular

\mathcal{J}_{g}[v^{\sharp}]-C^{\ast}_{\mathrm{Esc}}(M,g)\ \geq\ 0.

Thus

\mathrm{Def}^{\mathrm{Esc}}_{g}(u)=\mathcal{J}_{g}[u]-C^{\ast}_{\mathrm{Esc}}(M,g)\ \geq\ \mathcal{J}_{g}[u]-\mathcal{J}_{g}[v^{\sharp}].

Combining this with (
2.39
) yields

\mathrm{Def}^{\mathrm{Esc}}_{g}(u)\ \geq\ \tfrac{c_{\ast}}{8}\,\|w\|_{E,g}^{2}\ -\ C^{\prime}_{\mathrm{geo}}\,(\varepsilon^{\sharp})^{2},

or equivalently

\|w\|_{E,g}^{2}\ \leq\ \frac{8}{c_{\ast}}\,\mathrm{Def}^{\mathrm{Esc}}_{g}(u)\ +\ \frac{8C^{\prime}_{\mathrm{geo}}}{c_{\ast}}\,(\varepsilon^{\sharp})^{2}.

This is (
2.34
) with
C=8/c_{\ast}
and
C_{\mathrm{geo}}=8C^{\prime}_{\mathrm{geo}}/c_{\ast}
. Since
\|\cdot\|_{E,g}
and
\|\cdot\|_{H^{1}(M)}
are equivalent norms under (BG
2+
) and (Y
{}^{+}_{\partial}
) (Lemma
2.20
(c)), the stated
H^{1}
bound follows.
∎

Lemma 2.25

(First-order drift estimate)
.

Assume
(BG
2+
)
and
(Y
{}^{+}_{\partial}
)
. There exist
\varepsilon_{0}>0
and
C_{\mathrm{geo}}>0
(depending only on
n
and the
(BG
2+
)
,
(Y
{}^{+}_{\partial}
)
data) such that the following holds.

For every
x\in\partial M
, every
\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_{0}]
, and every amplitude
a>0
, set

v_{x,\varepsilon}:=a\,U_{+,x,\varepsilon},

where
U_{+,x,\varepsilon}=\mathcal{T}_{x,\varepsilon}(\chi_{R}U_{+})
is the (truncated) boundary bubble
of Definition
2.6
, with the context-dependent cutoff convention of
Remark
2.7
.
Then the first variation of the covariant Escobar quotient at
v_{x,\varepsilon}
satisfies

\big|L[v_{x,\varepsilon}](\psi)\big|:=\big|\langle D\mathcal{J}_{g}[v_{x,\varepsilon}],\psi\rangle_{E,g}\big|\ \leq\ C_{\mathrm{geo}}\,\varepsilon\,a^{-1}\,\|\psi\|_{E,g}

for all
\psi\in T_{v_{x,\varepsilon}}\mathcal{S}_{v_{x,\varepsilon}}
, where

\mathcal{S}_{v_{x,\varepsilon}}:=\Big\{F\in H^{1}(M):\ \|F\|_{L^{q}(\partial M)}=\|v_{x,\varepsilon}\|_{L^{q}(\partial M)}\Big\},\qquad q=2^{*}_{\partial}.

Proof.

Write
v=v_{x,\varepsilon}
and
q=2^{*}_{\partial}
. On the constraint slice
\mathcal{S}_{v}
we have

\mathcal{J}_{g}[F]=\frac{\mathcal{N}_{g}^{\circ}(F)}{\|v\|_{L^{q}(\partial M)}^{2}},

so its first variation at
v
along a tangent vector
\psi\in T_{v}\mathcal{S}_{v}
is

(2.40)

L[v](\psi)=\frac{d}{dt}\Big|_{t=0}\mathcal{J}_{g}[v+t\psi]=\frac{2}{\|v\|_{L^{q}(\partial M)}^{2}}\,\mathcal{N}_{g}^{\circ}(v,\psi),

because the derivative of the denominator vanishes on
T_{v}\mathcal{S}_{v}
. Thus it suffices to bound
\mathcal{N}_{g}^{\circ}(v,\psi)
.

By the Green identity for the canonical pair
(L_{g}^{\circ},B_{g}^{\circ})
(cf. (
2.22
)),

(2.41)

\mathcal{N}_{g}^{\circ}(v,\psi)=\int_{M}\psi\,L_{g}^{\circ}v\,dV_{g}+\int_{\partial M}\psi\,B_{g}^{\circ}v\,d\sigma_{g}.

We now use the fact that
v
is obtained from the flat optimizer
U_{+}
by the Fermi pushforward at scale
\varepsilon
and compare
(L_{g}^{\circ},B_{g}^{\circ})
with the flat canonical pair
(L_{\mathrm{flat}}^{\circ},B_{\mathrm{flat}}^{\circ})
in local Fermi coordinates.

(a) Decompose into flat part and geometric error.

Let
\Phi_{x}
be a Fermi chart at
x
with scale
\varepsilon
as in Lemma
2.9
, and write
y=\Phi_{x}^{-1}(z)/\varepsilon
for
z
near
x
. By construction,

v(z)=a\,\varepsilon^{-\frac{n-2}{2}}\,(\chi_{R}U_{+})(y),\qquad U_{R}:=\chi_{R}U_{+},

where
U_{+}
is the fixed half-space optimizer on
\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+}
. Denote by
(L_{\mathrm{flat}}^{\circ},B_{\mathrm{flat}}^{\circ})
the canonical pair for the flat metric on
\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+}
(pulled back to the chart).

The untruncated optimizer
U_{+}
solves the flat Euler–Lagrange system

L_{\mathrm{flat}}^{\circ}U_{+}=0\ \text{ in }\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+},\qquad B_{\mathrm{flat}}^{\circ}U_{+}=S_{\ast}\,U_{+}^{\,q-1}\ \text{ on }\partial\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+}.

For the truncated profile
U_{R}=\chi_{R}U_{+}
we have the flat residuals

L_{\mathrm{flat}}^{\circ}U_{R}=F_{R},\qquad B_{\mathrm{flat}}^{\circ}U_{R}=S_{\ast}\,U_{R}^{\,q-1}+G_{R},

where

F_{R}=-2\nabla\chi_{R}\!\cdot\!\nabla U_{+}-U_{+}\,\Delta\chi_{R}\quad\text{in }\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+},\qquad G_{R}=S_{\ast}\,(\chi_{R}-\chi_{R}^{\,q-1})\,U_{+}^{\,q-1}(\cdot,0)\quad\text{on }\mathbb{R}^{n-1},

and
F_{R},G_{R}
are supported where
\nabla\chi_{R}\neq 0
(i.e. in the annulus
R\lesssim|y|\lesssim 2R
).

For the scaled profile
v=a\,\mathcal{T}_{x,\varepsilon}U_{R}
the corresponding flat boundary multiplier is

\lambda_{v}:=S_{\ast}\,a^{2-q},\qquad\text{so that}\qquad\lambda_{v}\,v^{q-1}=S_{\ast}\,a\,(\mathcal{T}_{x,\varepsilon}U_{R})^{q-1}.

Comparing
(L_{g}^{\circ},B_{g}^{\circ})
with the flat pair in Fermi coordinates, we may write in the collar of
x

L_{g}^{\circ}v=E_{\mathrm{int}},\qquad B_{g}^{\circ}v=\lambda_{v}\,v^{q-1}+E_{\mathrm{bd}},

where
E_{\mathrm{int}},E_{\mathrm{bd}}
now collect
both
the geometric perturbation terms
and the transported flat truncation residuals coming from
F_{R},G_{R}
.

Substituting into (
2.41
) and using the amplitude constraint
\int_{\partial M}v^{q-1}\psi\,d\sigma_{g}=0
(since
\psi\in T_{v}\mathcal{S}_{v}
), we obtain

(2.42)

\mathcal{N}_{g}^{\circ}(v,\psi)=\int_{M}\psi\,E_{\mathrm{int}}\,dV_{g}+\int_{\partial M}\psi\,E_{\mathrm{bd}}\,d\sigma_{g}.

(b) Estimate the error terms in dual norms.

We claim that, under (BG
2+
), there exist
C>0
and a cutoff defect function
\omega(R)\downarrow 0

as
R\to\infty
such that

(2.43)

\|E_{\mathrm{int}}\|_{H^{-1}(M)}+\|E_{\mathrm{bd}}\|_{H^{-1/2}(\partial M)}\ \leq\ C\,\varepsilon\ +\ \omega(R),

uniformly in
x\in\partial M
and
\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_{0}]
.
In the single–bubble (diagonal) convention of Remark
2.7
(a), we choose
R=R(\varepsilon)\to\infty

so that
\omega(R(\varepsilon))\leq\varepsilon
, hence the right-hand side is
O(\varepsilon)
. Granting this for a moment, (
2.42
) and Cauchy–Schwarz in the dual pairings yield

|\mathcal{N}_{g}^{\circ}(v,\psi)|\ \leq\ \big(\|E_{\mathrm{int}}\|_{H^{-1}}+\|E_{\mathrm{bd}}\|_{H^{-1/2}}\big)\,\|\psi\|_{H^{1}(M)}\ \leq\ C\,\varepsilon\,\|\psi\|_{H^{1}(M)}.

Using the equivalence
\|\psi\|_{H^{1}(M)}\simeq\|\psi\|_{E,g}
from Lemma
2.20
(c) and the fact that
\|v\|_{L^{q}(\partial M)}
is fixed (depending only on
n
and the normalization of
U_{+}
), (
2.40
) implies (
2.38
).

It remains to justify (
2.43
). In boundary Fermi coordinates
(y^{\prime},y_{n})
scaled by
\varepsilon
, the metric coefficients
g_{ij}(\varepsilon y)
satisfy the expansions from Lemma
2.9
:

g_{ij}(\varepsilon y)=\delta_{ij}+O(\varepsilon|y|)+O(\varepsilon^{2}|y|^{2}),\quad\partial_{k}g_{ij}(\varepsilon y)=O(\varepsilon)+O(\varepsilon^{2}|y|),

uniformly for
|y|\leq R
with
R
fixed (recall we only use a fixed Fermi radius). Differentiating the expressions for
L_{g}^{\circ}
and
B_{g}^{\circ}
in terms of
g_{ij}
and their derivatives (see, e.g., the standard formulas in boundary normal coordinates), one sees that in these scaled variables

(L_{g}^{\circ}-L_{\mathrm{flat}}^{\circ})U_{+}=\varepsilon\,\mathcal{E}_{1}(y)+\varepsilon^{2}\,\mathcal{E}_{2}(y),\qquad(B_{g}^{\circ}-B_{\mathrm{flat}}^{\circ})U_{+}=\varepsilon\,\mathcal{F}_{1}(y^{\prime})+\varepsilon^{2}\,\mathcal{F}_{2}(y^{\prime}),

with
\mathcal{E}_{j},\mathcal{F}_{j}
depending on
U_{+}
and its first and second derivatives and controlled using the standard decay
|\nabla^{k}U_{+}(y)|\lesssim(1+|y|)^{-n+2-k}
for
k\leq 2
.

A routine computation, using Hölder and the Sobolev trace embedding
H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+})\hookrightarrow L^{2^{\ast}}(\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+})
and
H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+})\to L^{q}(\partial\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+})
, shows that

\|(L_{g}^{\circ}-L_{\mathrm{flat}}^{\circ})U_{+}\|_{H^{-1}(\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+})}+\|(B_{g}^{\circ}-B_{\mathrm{flat}}^{\circ})U_{+}\|_{H^{-1/2}(\partial\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+})}\ \leq\ C\,\varepsilon,

with
C
depending only on
n
and the (BG
2+
) bounds (the polynomial
y
-growth from the coefficients is compensated by the decay of
U_{+}
and its derivatives in the dual norms; in particular, no weighted moments beyond those already used in Lemma
2.11
are needed). Pulling back these estimates to
(M,g)
via the Fermi chart and using the uniform equivalence of Sobolev norms in Fermi coordinates yields (
2.43
).

This establishes (
2.43
), and hence (
2.38
) via (
2.40
). The constants are uniform in
x\in\partial M
and
\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_{0}]
by the uniformity in the (BG
2+
) data. This completes the proof.
∎

Remark 2.26

(On the exponent of the geometric remainder)
.

The estimate (
2.34
) is stated with a remainder
C_{\mathrm{geo}}(\varepsilon^{\sharp})^{2}
.
If desired, one may relax it to
C^{\prime}_{\mathrm{geo}}\varepsilon^{\sharp}
by enlarging the constant, using
\varepsilon^{\sharp}\leq\varepsilon_{0}\leq 1
. The proof above shows that the first-order drift is
O(\varepsilon^{\sharp})
in the dual
E,g
-norm; the passage from an
O(\varepsilon^{\sharp})
linear term in
\|u-v^{\sharp}\|_{E,g}
to the
(\varepsilon^{\sharp})^{2}
contribution in (
2.34
) comes from the Young-inequality absorption in Step 4 of Theorem
2.24
, which is slightly stronger than what is strictly needed.

Bubble cones (including amplitude).

For stability statements on the
L^{q}(\partial M)
–slice it is convenient to include the
amplitude parameter explicitly. We set

\mathcal{C}_{\partial}:=\{a\,U_{+,x,\varepsilon}:a>0,\ x\in\partial M,\ \varepsilon>0\},\qquad\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{int}}:=\{a\,U^{\mathrm{int}}_{x,\varepsilon}:a>0,\ x\in M^{\circ},\ \varepsilon>0\}.

Lemma 2.27

(Interior channel excluded at small Escobar deficit)
.

Assume
(Y
{}^{+}_{\partial}
)
and
(BG
2+
)
. There exists
\delta_{\mathrm{sep}}>0
(depending only on
(M,g,n)
) such that if
u\in H^{1}(M)
satisfies

\|u\|_{L^{2^{*}_{\partial}}(\partial M)}=1
and
\mathrm{Def}^{\mathrm{Esc}}_{g}(u)\leq\delta_{\mathrm{sep}}
, then
every best–fit to
u
in
\mathcal{C}_{\partial}\cup\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{int}}
(with respect to
\|\cdot\|_{E,g}
) lies in
\mathcal{C}_{\partial}
; in particular, the interior best–fit cannot occur in the small–deficit regime. Here
\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{int}}
refers to genuinely interior bubbles (Definition
2.6
), i.e. interior Yamabe bubbles whose centers stay a fixed positive distance from
\partial M
(equivalently, their support remains disjoint from
\partial M
).

Proof sketch.

For
W\in\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{int}}
(genuinely interior bubbles), one has
W|_{\partial M}\equiv 0

since
\supp W\cap\partial M=\emptyset
by Definition
2.6
. Hence for any
u
with

\|u\|_{L^{q}(\partial M)}=1
,

\|u-W\|_{H^{1}(M)}\ \geq\ C_{\mathrm{tr}}^{-1}\,\|u-W\|_{L^{q}(\partial M)}\ =\ C_{\mathrm{tr}}^{-1},\qquad q=2^{*}_{\partial},

by the global trace inequality (Lemma
2.2
). In the small–deficit regime,
Theorem
2.24
yields a boundary bubble
v^{\sharp}_{\partial}\in\mathcal{C}_{\partial}

with
\|u-v^{\sharp}_{\partial}\|_{H^{1}(M)}
arbitrarily small. Choosing
\delta_{\mathrm{sep}}
so that

\|u-v^{\sharp}_{\partial}\|_{H^{1}(M)}<\tfrac{1}{2}C_{\mathrm{tr}}^{-1}
excludes the interior channel.
(No quantitative value of
\delta_{\mathrm{sep}}
is used below.)∎

Theorem 2.28

(Bi–modal quantitative stability with geometric remainder)
.

Assume
(Y
{}^{+}_{\partial}
)
and
(BG
2+
)
. There exist
\delta_{0},c,C_{\mathrm{geo}}>0
(depending only on
(M,g,n)
) such that

\delta_{0}\ \leq\ \min\big\{\delta_{\mathrm{sep}},\,\delta_{\mathrm{quant}}\big\},

where
\delta_{\mathrm{sep}}
is as in Lemma
2.27
and
\delta_{\mathrm{quant}}
is the small-deficit constant from Theorem
2.24
.
Then for every
u\in H^{1}(M)
with
\|u\|_{L^{2^{*}_{\partial}}(\partial M)}=1
and
\mathrm{Def}^{\mathrm{Esc}}_{g}(u)\leq\delta_{0}
, let
v^{\sharp}_{\partial}\in\mathcal{C}_{\partial}
and
v^{\sharp}_{\mathrm{int}}\in\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{int}}
be boundary and interior best–fits to
u

(in the covariant weak inner product), and let
\varepsilon^{\sharp}(u)
be the scale parameter of
v^{\sharp}_{\partial}
. If
\varepsilon^{\sharp}(u)\in(0,\varepsilon_{0})
(with
\varepsilon_{0}
as in Theorem
2.24
), then

(2.44)

\mathrm{Def}^{\mathrm{Esc}}_{g}(u)\ +\ C_{\mathrm{geo}}\;\varepsilon^{\sharp}(u)^{2}\ \geq\ c\ \min\Big\{\,\mathrm{dist}_{H^{1}(M)}\big(u,\mathcal{C}_{\partial}\big)^{2}\ ,\ \mathrm{dist}_{H^{1}(M)}\big(u,\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{int}}\big)^{2}\,\Big\}.

Proof.

Let
v^{\sharp}_{\partial}
and
v^{\sharp}_{\mathrm{int}}
be the boundary and interior best–fits defined above. Note that since
\mathrm{Def}^{\mathrm{Esc}}_{g}(u)\leq\delta_{0}\leq\delta_{\mathrm{sep}}
, Lemma
2.27
ensures that any
global
best–fit lies in
\mathcal{C}_{\partial}
, although we will not use this directly in the estimate.

Boundary channel.
If
\varepsilon^{\sharp}(u)\in(0,\varepsilon_{0})
, Theorem
2.24
yields

\|u-v^{\sharp}_{\partial}\|_{E,g}^{2}\ \leq\ C\,\mathrm{Def}^{\mathrm{Esc}}_{g}(u)+C_{\mathrm{geo}}\,\varepsilon^{\sharp}(u)^{2}.

By graph/
H^{1}
norm equivalence on bounded geometry (Lemma
2.20
(c)),

\|u-v^{\sharp}_{\partial}\|_{H^{1}(M)}^{2}\ \lesssim\ \mathrm{Def}^{\mathrm{Esc}}_{g}(u)+C_{\mathrm{geo}}\,\varepsilon^{\sharp}(u)^{2}.

Since
\mathrm{dist}_{H^{1}}(u,\mathcal{C}_{\partial})\leq\|u-v^{\sharp}_{\partial}\|_{H^{1}(M)}
, we obtain

\mathrm{Def}^{\mathrm{Esc}}_{g}(u)+C_{\mathrm{geo}}\,\varepsilon^{\sharp}(u)^{2}\ \gtrsim\ \mathrm{dist}_{H^{1}(M)}\big(u,\mathcal{C}_{\partial}\big)^{2}.

Bi–modal estimate.
For any
u
,

\min\Big\{\mathrm{dist}_{H^{1}(M)}\big(u,\mathcal{C}_{\partial}\big),\ \mathrm{dist}_{H^{1}(M)}\big(u,\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{int}}\big)\Big\}\ \leq\ \mathrm{dist}_{H^{1}(M)}\big(u,\mathcal{C}_{\partial}\big)\leq\|u-v^{\sharp}_{\partial}\|_{H^{1}(M)}.

Thus the bound from the boundary channel implies

\mathrm{Def}^{\mathrm{Esc}}_{g}(u)+C_{\mathrm{geo}}\,\varepsilon^{\sharp}(u)^{2}\ \gtrsim\ \min\Big\{\mathrm{dist}_{H^{1}(M)}\big(u,\mathcal{C}_{\partial}\big)^{2},\ \mathrm{dist}_{H^{1}(M)}\big(u,\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{int}}\big)^{2}\Big\},

which is precisely (
2.44
) after renaming constants.
∎

Remark 2.29

(When the geometric remainder can be removed)
.

The remainder
C_{\mathrm{geo}}\varepsilon^{\sharp}(u)^{2}
reflects that on
(M,g)
a boundary bubble is only an
approximate
critical point; curvature induces a first-order drift of size
O(\varepsilon^{\sharp})
in the Euler-Lagrange equations.
If, in addition, one has a
scale-deficit control

\varepsilon^{\sharp}(u)^{2}\ \leq\ K\,\mathrm{Def}^{\mathrm{Esc}}_{g}(u)

(for instance, from a first-order scale law at a boundary point with
|S_{\ast}\rho_{n}^{\mathrm{conf}}H_{g}(x^{\sharp})|\geq h_{0}>0
), then (
2.44
) improves to the pure coercivity

\mathrm{Def}^{\mathrm{Esc}}_{g}(u)\ \gtrsim\ \min\Big\{\,\mathrm{dist}_{H^{1}(M)}(u,\mathcal{C}_{\partial})^{2},\ \mathrm{dist}_{H^{1}(M)}(u,\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{int}})^{2}\Big\}.

Establishing such a scale-deficit bound requires additional geometric input and is not used in the theorem as stated.

2.5.
Boundary localization by deficit (anchors)

Definition 2.30

(Anchored center via nearest-point projection)
.

Fix a
C^{2}
isometric embedding
X:\partial M\hookrightarrow\mathbb{R}^{N}
and let
\Pi:\mathrm{Tub}_{\delta}\big(X(\partial M)\big)\to X(\partial M)
be the nearest-point projection for some
\delta>0
.
For
u\in H^{1}(M)
with
\|u\|_{L^{2^{*}_{\partial}}(\partial M)}=1
(where
2^{*}_{\partial}=\tfrac{2(n-1)}{n-2}
), define the (unnormalized) barycenter

\bar{z}(u):=\int_{\partial M}X(x)\,|u(x)|^{2^{*}_{\partial}}\,d\sigma_{g}(x)\in\mathbb{R}^{N}.

Since
\|u\|_{L^{2^{*}_{\partial}}(\partial M)}=1
, this is the barycenter with respect to the probability measure
|u|^{2^{*}_{\partial}}\,d\sigma_{g}
on
\partial M
.
The
anchored center
is

x_{\mathcal{A}}(u)\ :=\ X^{-1}\big(\Pi(\bar{z}(u))\big)\ \in\ \partial M.

For
u
in a small
H^{1}
-neighborhood of the boundary bubble manifold
\mathcal{B}_{\partial}
,
\bar{z}(u)\in\mathrm{Tub}_{\delta}
and
x_{\mathcal{A}}(u)
is uniquely defined.

Remark 2.31

(Favored boundary set determined by the first-order law)
.

Let
\rho_{n}^{\mathrm{conf}}
be the (dimension-dependent) normalized coefficient from the first-order expansion (see Lemmas
2.9
,
2.10
, and (
2.18
)). Define the
favored set

\mathcal{C}_{\ast}\ :=\ {\arg\min}_{x\in\partial M}\ \big(\operatorname{sgn}\rho_{n}^{\mathrm{conf}}\big)\,H_{g}(x).

Equivalently,
\mathcal{C}_{\ast}=\arg\min H_{g}
if
\rho_{n}^{\mathrm{conf}}>0
and
\mathcal{C}_{\ast}=\arg\max H_{g}
if
\rho_{n}^{\mathrm{conf}}<0
(the case
\rho_{n}^{\mathrm{conf}}=0
is first-order degenerate). This convention matches the first-order law for the Escobar quotient at a boundary bubble:

\mathcal{J}_{g}[U_{+,x,\varepsilon}]=S_{\ast}\big(1+\rho_{n}^{\mathrm{conf}}H_{g}(x)\,\varepsilon\big)+o(\varepsilon).

Lemma 2.32

(Barycenter is well posed and close to the best-fit center)
.

Assume
(BG
2+
)
. There exist
\varepsilon_{0},\delta_{0},C>0
such that the following holds.
Let
u\in H^{1}(M)
with
\|u\|_{L^{2^{*}_{\partial}}(\partial M)}=1
,
\mathrm{Def}^{\mathrm{Esc}}_{g}(u)\leq\delta_{0}
, and let

v^{\sharp}=U_{+,x^{\sharp},\varepsilon^{\sharp}}\in\mathcal{B}_{\partial}
be a best-fit boundary bubble for
u
in the
covariant weak
graph inner product (Lemma
2.16
), with
\varepsilon^{\sharp}\leq\varepsilon_{0}
.
Then
x_{\mathcal{A}}(u)
is well defined and

(2.45)

d_{\partial M}\big(x_{\mathcal{A}}(u),x^{\sharp}\big)\ \leq\ C\,\varepsilon^{\sharp}\Big(\,\|u-v^{\sharp}\|_{H^{1}(M)}\ +\ \varepsilon^{\sharp}\Big).

Proof sketch.

Work in tangential geodesic/Fermi coordinates at
x^{\sharp}
.

Taylor-expand
X
:
X(\Phi_{x^{\sharp}}(\varepsilon^{\sharp}y^{\prime},0))=X(x^{\sharp})+dX_{x^{\sharp}}[\varepsilon^{\sharp}y^{\prime}]+O((\varepsilon^{\sharp})^{2}|y^{\prime}|^{2})
. For
v^{\sharp}
the tangential profile is radial, hence
\int y^{\prime}\,|v^{\sharp}(\cdot,0)|^{2^{*}_{\partial}}=0
and the
O(\varepsilon^{\sharp})
term cancels, yielding
\bar{z}(v^{\sharp})=X(x^{\sharp})+O((\varepsilon^{\sharp})^{2})
. Write
w:=u-v^{\sharp}
. By the modulation orthogonality (Lemma
2.16
), the first tangential moment of
w
is small; using the boundary trace estimate and Lemma
2.10
, we obtain

\big\|\;|u|^{2^{*}_{\partial}}-|v^{\sharp}|^{2^{*}_{\partial}}\;\big\|_{W^{-1,1}(\partial M)}\ \lesssim\ \varepsilon^{\sharp}\,\|w\|_{H^{1}(M)},

where
W^{-1,1}(\partial M)
denotes the dual of
W^{1,\infty}(\partial M)
. Hence

|\bar{z}(u)-\bar{z}(v^{\sharp})|\ \lesssim\ \varepsilon^{\sharp}\,\|w\|_{H^{1}(M)}+(\varepsilon^{\sharp})^{2}
.
Since
\Pi
is
C^{1}
with uniformly bounded derivative on
\mathrm{Tub}_{\delta}
, the nearest-point projection is Lipschitz; therefore

d_{\partial M}(x_{\mathcal{A}}(u),x^{\sharp})\ \lesssim\ \varepsilon^{\sharp}\,\|w\|_{H^{1}(M)}+(\varepsilon^{\sharp})^{2}
,
which is (
2.45
).∎

Lemma 2.33

(Localization toward the favored set)
.

Assume
(BG
2+
)
and
\rho_{n}^{\mathrm{conf}}\neq 0
. There exist
\varepsilon_{0},\delta_{0},C>0
and a modulus
\omega(\cdot)
with
\omega(t)\to 0
as
t\downarrow 0
such that: if
u\in H^{1}(M)
satisfies
\|u\|_{L^{2^{*}_{\partial}}(\partial M)}=1
,
\mathrm{Def}^{\mathrm{Esc}}_{g}(u)\leq\delta_{0}
, and its boundary best-fit satisfies
\varepsilon^{\sharp}(u)\leq\varepsilon_{0}
, then

(2.46)

\mathrm{dist}\big(x_{\mathcal{A}}(u),\ \mathcal{C}_{\ast}\big)\ \leq\ C\,\varepsilon^{\sharp}(u)\ +\ \omega\big(\mathrm{Def}^{\mathrm{Esc}}_{g}(u)\big).

Proof sketch.

Compare the Escobar
quotient
at boundary centers
x
using the first-order expansion provided by Lemmas
2.9
,
2.10
, and (
2.18
). The first-order law selects
\mathcal{C}_{\ast}
up to an
O(\varepsilon^{\sharp})
ambiguity; the additional
\omega(\mathrm{Def}^{\mathrm{Esc}}_{g}(u))
term comes from the controlled deviation of
u
from its best-fit bubble and compactness of the center parameter. No assumption of a scale-deficit law is used here: localization is conditional on both small deficit and small boundary scale.∎

Theorem 2.34

(Anchored quantitative stability with a fixed center)
.

Assume
(Y
{}^{+}_{\partial}
)
and
(BG
2+
)
. There exist
\varepsilon_{0},\delta_{0}>0
and
C,C_{\mathrm{geo}}>0
such that the following holds.
Let
u\in H^{1}(M)
with
\|u\|_{L^{2^{*}_{\partial}}(\partial M)}=1
and
\mathrm{Def}^{\mathrm{Esc}}_{g}(u)\leq\delta_{0}
.
Define the anchored center
x_{\mathcal{A}}(u)
by Definition
2.30
, and choose an anchored scale

\varepsilon(u)\ \in\ {\arg\min}_{\varepsilon>0}\ \big\|\,u-U_{+,x_{\mathcal{A}}(u),\varepsilon}\,\big\|_{H^{1}(M)}.

(For
\delta_{0}
and
\varepsilon_{0}
sufficiently small there is a unique minimizer, but uniqueness is not needed here.)
Let
\varepsilon^{\sharp}(u)
be the boundary best-fit scale from Lemma
2.16
. If
\varepsilon^{\sharp}(u)\leq\varepsilon_{0}
, then

(2.47)

\big\|\,u-U_{+,x_{\mathcal{A}}(u),\varepsilon(u)}\,\big\|_{H^{1}(M)}^{2}\ \leq\ C\,\mathrm{Def}^{\mathrm{Esc}}_{g}(u)\ +\ C_{\mathrm{geo}}\,(\varepsilon^{\sharp}(u))^{2}.

Proof sketch.

Let
v^{\sharp}=U_{+,x^{\sharp},\varepsilon^{\sharp}}
be the (unanchored) best-fit from Lemma
2.16
(in the covariant weak graph pairing) with
\varepsilon^{\sharp}\leq\varepsilon_{0}
(the boundary channel). If the interior channel were the best-fit, this is excluded in the small-deficit regime by Lemma
2.27
; thus we may assume the boundary channel throughout.
By the quantitative boundary stability with geometric remainder (Theorem
2.24
),

\|u-v^{\sharp}\|_{H^{1}(M)}^{2}\ \leq\ C\,\mathrm{Def}^{\mathrm{Esc}}_{g}(u)+C_{\mathrm{geo}}\,(\varepsilon^{\sharp})^{2}.

Next, by Lemma
2.32
,

d_{\partial M}(x_{\mathcal{A}}(u),x^{\sharp})\ \leq\ C\,\varepsilon^{\sharp}\Big(\|u-v^{\sharp}\|_{H^{1}(M)}+\varepsilon^{\sharp}\Big).

The bubble manifold is
C^{1}
in parameters, and standard parameter-sensitivity estimates yield

\big\|\,U_{+,x_{\mathcal{A}}(u),\varepsilon^{\sharp}}-U_{+,x^{\sharp},\varepsilon^{\sharp}}\,\big\|_{H^{1}(M)}\ \lesssim\ \frac{d_{\partial M}(x_{\mathcal{A}}(u),x^{\sharp})}{\varepsilon^{\sharp}}\ \lesssim\ \|u-v^{\sharp}\|_{H^{1}(M)}+\varepsilon^{\sharp}.

Finally, by the definition of
\varepsilon(u)
(anchored best scale) and triangle inequality,

\|u-U_{+,x_{\mathcal{A}}(u),\varepsilon(u)}\|_{H^{1}}\leq\|u-U_{+,x_{\mathcal{A}}(u),\varepsilon^{\sharp}}\|_{H^{1}}\leq\|u-v^{\sharp}\|_{H^{1}}+\|v^{\sharp}-U_{+,x_{\mathcal{A}}(u),\varepsilon^{\sharp}}\|_{H^{1}}\lesssim\|u-v^{\sharp}\|_{H^{1}}+\varepsilon^{\sharp}.

Squaring and inserting the bound for
\|u-v^{\sharp}\|_{H^{1}}
gives (
2.47
) (with possibly a different constant
C_{\mathrm{geo}}
).
∎

Remark 2.35

(No contradiction with the geometric remainder)
.

Theorem
2.34

retains
the geometric
O(\varepsilon^{2})
remainder found in Theorem
2.24
; anchoring does not magically remove the dilation neutrality.
If, in addition, one has a
scale-deficit
bound
\varepsilon^{\sharp}(u)^{2}\leq K\,\mathrm{Def}^{\mathrm{Esc}}_{g}(u)
(available under extra geometric hypotheses or a nondegenerate first-order selection at a point in
\mathcal{C}_{\ast}
), then (
2.47
) improves to a pure coercivity estimate (up to constants) with no separate
\varepsilon
-term. We do
not
assume such a law here.

2.6.
First-order expansion and scale–deficit law

Notation update.

Throughout we identify the Fermi pushforward at scale
\varepsilon
with the operator
\mathcal{T}_{x,\varepsilon}
defined earlier (including the critical amplitude factor
\varepsilon^{-(n-2)/2}
). When we previously wrote
\Pi^{\mathrm{Fermi}}_{x,\varepsilon}
, we now use
\mathcal{T}_{x,\varepsilon}
.

Lemma 2.36

(Boundary scale–deficit for the covariant Escobar
quotient
)
.

Assume
(BG
2+
)
and
(Y
{}^{+}_{\partial}
)
. Let
U_{+}
be the half-space model optimizer (covariant setting) and
S_{\ast}=C^{*}_{\mathrm{Esc}}(S^{n}_{+})
. Let
v_{x,\varepsilon}:=\mathcal{T}_{x,\varepsilon}U_{+}

be its Fermi pushforward at a boundary point
x\in\partial M
with scale
\varepsilon\ll 1
. Then, uniformly in
x
,

\mathcal{J}_{g}[v_{x,\varepsilon}]-S_{\ast}\ =\ S_{\ast}\,\rho_{n}^{\mathrm{conf}}\,H_{g}(x)\,\varepsilon\ +\ o(\varepsilon),

for some dimension-dependent constant
\rho_{n}^{\mathrm{conf}}>0
determined by the model optimizer
U_{+}
and the chosen normalization. For
n\geq 4
,
\rho_{n}^{\mathrm{conf}}
is given explicitly by the moment formula (
2.18
), and for the harmonic half-space optimizer
U_{+}
one has

\rho_{n}^{\mathrm{conf}}=\frac{(n-2)^{2}}{2(n-1)}\,\Theta>0

(Lemma
2.42
). In dimension
n=3
,
\rho_{3}^{\mathrm{conf}}>0
is defined via the first-order expansion on the hemisphere model (see Remark
2.12
); we do not use an explicit moment representation in that case.

Consequently, there exist
\varepsilon_{0},\delta_{0}>0
(depending on
(M,g,n,h_{0})
and the bounded-geometry data) such that if
C^{*}_{\mathrm{Esc}}(M,g)=S_{\ast}
and

\operatorname{sgn}\!\big(\rho_{n}^{\mathrm{conf}}\big)\,H_{g}(x)\geq h_{0}>0
, then for any
u
with
\|u\|_{L^{2^{*}_{\partial}}(\partial M)}=1
and
\mathrm{Def}^{\mathrm{Esc}}_{g}(u)\leq\delta_{0}
whose best-fit
scaled
boundary bubble (as in Lemma
2.16
)
has the form
v^{\sharp}=a^{\sharp}U_{+,x,\varepsilon}
with center
x
and scale
\varepsilon\leq\varepsilon_{0}
one has the scale–deficit bound

\varepsilon\ \lesssim\ \mathrm{Def}^{\mathrm{Esc}}_{g}(u).

Proof of Lemma
2.36
.

Fix
x\in\partial M
and let
U_{+}
be a half-space optimizer for the
covariant
Escobar functional on
\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+}
. For
\varepsilon>0
small, set
v_{x,\varepsilon}:=\mathcal{T}_{x,\varepsilon}U_{+}
, implemented via a tangentially radial cutoff
\chi_{R}
as in Lemmas
2.9
–
2.10
and with
R=R(\varepsilon)\to\infty
chosen so that the cutoff error is absorbed into
o(\varepsilon)
. We compute
\mathcal{J}_{g}[v_{x,\varepsilon}]
to first order in
\varepsilon
.

Step 1 (Numerator expansion - first order).

Using
\mathcal{N}_{g}^{\circ}=\tfrac{1}{a_{n}}\mathcal{Q}_{g}
and Lemma
2.9
((
2.9
)) with
w=U_{+}
(tangentially radial),

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}^{\circ}(v_{x,\varepsilon})

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+}}|\nabla U_{+}|^{2}\,dy

\displaystyle\quad+\ \varepsilon\,H_{g}(x)\Bigg[\left(\frac{2}{n-1}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+}}y_{n}\,|\nabla_{\tan}U_{+}|^{2}\,dy\ -\ \int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+}}y_{n}\,|\nabla U_{+}|^{2}\,dy\right)

(2.48)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\frac{n-2}{2}\,\|U_{+}(\cdot,0)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n-1})}^{2}\Bigg]\ +\ o(\varepsilon).

Here
\tfrac{2(n-1)}{a_{n}}=\tfrac{n-2}{2}
was used for the boundary term. (In dimension
n=3
we do not use this moment representation;
\rho_{3}^{\mathrm{conf}}>0
is instead defined via the hemisphere model as in Remark
2.12
.)

Step 2 (Denominator expansion - no linear term).

By Lemma
2.10
,

(2.49)

\Big(\int_{\partial M}|v_{x,\varepsilon}|^{\,2^{*}_{\partial}}\,d\sigma_{g}\Big)^{2/2^{*}_{\partial}}\ =\ \|U_{+}(\cdot,0)\|_{L^{2^{*}_{\partial}}(\mathbb{R}^{n-1})}^{2}\ +\ o(\varepsilon),

uniformly in
x
.

Step 3 (Quotient expansion and the first-order coefficient).

Let
E_{0}=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+}}|\nabla U_{+}|^{2}
and
D_{0}=\|U_{+}(\cdot,0)\|_{L^{2^{*}_{\partial}}(\mathbb{R}^{n-1})}^{2}
. Introducing the dimensionless moments from §
2.1
, we obtain:

(2.50)

\mathcal{J}_{g}[v_{x,\varepsilon}]\ =\ \frac{E_{0}}{D_{0}}\ +\ \varepsilon\,H_{g}(x)\,\frac{E_{0}}{D_{0}}\,\rho_{n}^{\mathrm{conf}}\ +\ o(\varepsilon).

Since
S_{\ast}=E_{0}/D_{0}=C^{*}_{\mathrm{Esc}}(S^{n}_{+})
, we have

\mathcal{J}_{g}[v_{x,\varepsilon}]=S_{\ast}\big(1+\rho_{n}^{\mathrm{conf}}\,H_{g}(x)\,\varepsilon\big)+o(\varepsilon),

where
\rho_{n}^{\mathrm{conf}}
is defined by normalizing the bracket in (
2.48
):

\Bigg[\left(\frac{2}{n-1}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+}}y_{n}\,|\nabla_{\tan}U_{+}|^{2}\,dy\ -\ \int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+}}y_{n}\,|\nabla U_{+}|^{2}\,dy\right)+\frac{n-2}{2}\,\|U_{+}(\cdot,0)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n-1})}^{2}\Bigg]=E_{0}\,\rho_{n}^{\mathrm{conf}}.

For
n\geq 4
this agrees with the explicit moment combination in (
2.18
); for
n=3
we do not use this moment identity (Remark
2.12
).

Step 4 (From the quotient expansion to the deficit).

By definition
\mathrm{Def}^{\mathrm{Esc}}_{g}(w)=\mathcal{J}_{g}[w]-C^{*}_{\mathrm{Esc}}(g)
. Subtracting
C^{*}_{\mathrm{Esc}}(g)
from (
2.50
) yields

\mathrm{Def}^{\mathrm{Esc}}_{g}(v_{x,\varepsilon})\ =\ \big(S_{\ast}-C^{*}_{\mathrm{Esc}}(g)\big)\ +\ S_{\ast}\,\rho_{n}^{\mathrm{conf}}\,H_{g}(x)\,\varepsilon\ +\ o(\varepsilon).

If
C^{*}_{\mathrm{Esc}}(g)\neq S_{\ast}
, the constant gap
S_{\ast}-C^{*}_{\mathrm{Esc}}(g)>0
implies a boundary bubble cannot produce small Escobar deficit (for
\varepsilon\ll 1
). In what follows assume
C^{*}_{\mathrm{Esc}}(g)=S_{\ast}
. For the scale law also assume the nondegenerate favorable sign

\operatorname{sgn}\big(\rho_{n}^{\mathrm{conf}}\big)\,H_{g}(x)\ \geq\ h_{0}>0.

Step 5 (Best-fit scale).

Let
u\in H^{1}(M)
and let
v^{\sharp}=a^{\sharp}U_{+,x,\varepsilon}
be its best-fit
scaled

boundary bubble given by Lemma
2.16
(covariant weak pairing), with center
x
and scale
\varepsilon
.
Write
u=v^{\sharp}+w
, where
w
satisfies the modulation orthogonality and slice constraint:

\langle w,\partial_{\varepsilon}v^{\sharp}\rangle_{E,g}=0,\qquad\langle w,\nabla_{x}v^{\sharp}\rangle_{E,g}=0,\qquad\int_{\partial M}(v^{\sharp})^{q-1}w\,d\sigma_{g}=0.

A Taylor expansion of
\mathcal{J}_{g}
on the constraint slice at
v^{\sharp}
gives

\mathcal{J}_{g}[u]=\mathcal{J}_{g}[v^{\sharp}]+D\mathcal{J}_{g}[v^{\sharp}][w]+\tfrac{1}{2}\,\langle D^{2}\mathcal{J}_{g}[v^{\sharp}]w,w\rangle+O(\|w\|_{E,g}^{\,p}),\qquad p:=\min\{3,q\}.

By the
O(\varepsilon)
drift estimate (cf. (
2.38
)),

|D\mathcal{J}_{g}[v^{\sharp}][w]|\ \leq\ C\,\varepsilon\,(a^{\sharp})^{-1}\,\|w\|_{E,g}\ \leq\ C\,\varepsilon\,\|w\|_{E,g}.

By intrinsic single-bubble coercivity (Theorem
2.23
),

\langle D^{2}\mathcal{J}_{g}[v^{\sharp}]w,w\rangle\ \geq\ c_{0}\,\|w\|_{E,g}^{2}.

For
\|w\|_{E,g}
sufficiently small, the remainder term
O(\|w\|_{E,g}^{\,p})
(with
p>2
) can be absorbed into
\tfrac{1}{2}c_{0}\|w\|_{E,g}^{2}
. Thus

\mathcal{J}_{g}[u]\ \geq\ \mathcal{J}_{g}[v^{\sharp}]-C\,\varepsilon\,\|w\|_{E,g}+c_{1}\,\|w\|_{E,g}^{2}

for some
c_{1}>0
. Subtracting
C^{*}_{\mathrm{Esc}}(g)=S_{\ast}
from both sides yields

\mathrm{Def}^{\mathrm{Esc}}_{g}(u)\ \geq\ \mathrm{Def}^{\mathrm{Esc}}_{g}(v^{\sharp})\ -\ C\,\varepsilon\,\|w\|_{E,g}.

Using the first–order expansion of
\mathrm{Def}^{\mathrm{Esc}}_{g}(v^{\sharp})
(Step 4) and writing
o(\varepsilon)=\varepsilon\,\omega(\varepsilon)
with
\omega(\varepsilon)\to 0
,
we obtain

\mathrm{Def}^{\mathrm{Esc}}_{g}(u)\ \geq\ S_{\ast}\rho_{n}^{\mathrm{conf}}H_{g}(x)\,\varepsilon\ +\ \varepsilon\,\omega(\varepsilon)\ -\ C\,\varepsilon\,\|w\|_{E,g}.

By Theorem
2.24
,

\|w\|_{E,g}^{2}\ \lesssim\ \mathrm{Def}^{\mathrm{Esc}}_{g}(u)\ +\ C_{\mathrm{geo}}\,\varepsilon^{2}.

Hence,

\mathrm{Def}^{\mathrm{Esc}}_{g}(u)\ \geq\ S_{\ast}\rho_{n}^{\mathrm{conf}}H_{g}(x)\,\varepsilon\ +\ \varepsilon\,\omega(\varepsilon)\ -\ C\,\varepsilon\sqrt{\mathrm{Def}^{\mathrm{Esc}}_{g}(u)+\varepsilon^{2}}.

Step 6 (Young’s inequality and absorption).

Apply Young’s inequality to the mixed term

C\,\varepsilon\sqrt{\mathrm{Def}^{\mathrm{Esc}}_{g}(u)+\varepsilon^{2}}\ \leq\ \tfrac{1}{4}\,\mathrm{Def}^{\mathrm{Esc}}_{g}(u)\ +\ C^{\prime}\,\varepsilon^{2}

for a suitable
C^{\prime}>0
. This gives

\frac{5}{4}\,\mathrm{Def}^{\mathrm{Esc}}_{g}(u)\ \geq\ S_{\ast}\rho_{n}^{\mathrm{conf}}H_{g}(x)\,\varepsilon\ +\ \varepsilon\,\omega(\varepsilon)\ -\ C^{\prime\prime}\,\varepsilon^{2}.

If
\operatorname{sgn}(\rho_{n}^{\mathrm{conf}})H_{g}(x)\geq h_{0}>0
, then for all
0<\varepsilon\leq\varepsilon_{1}(h_{0},M,g)
,

S_{\ast}\,\rho_{n}^{\mathrm{conf}}\,H_{g}(x)\,\varepsilon\ -\ C^{\prime\prime}\,\varepsilon^{2}\ \geq\ \tfrac{1}{2}S_{\ast}\,|\rho_{n}^{\mathrm{conf}}|\,h_{0}\,\varepsilon,

so

\mathrm{Def}^{\mathrm{Esc}}_{g}(u)\ \gtrsim\ \varepsilon.

This yields the stated scale-deficit bound
\varepsilon\ \lesssim\ \mathrm{Def}^{\mathrm{Esc}}_{g}(u)
for
\varepsilon\leq\varepsilon_{0}
and
\mathrm{Def}^{\mathrm{Esc}}_{g}(u)\leq\delta_{0}
.
∎

Lemma 2.37

(Stratified one-bubble expansion at the hemisphere threshold)
.

Assume
n\geq 3
and
C^{*}_{\mathrm{Esc}}(M,g)=S_{\ast}
. Let
x\in\partial M
and let

v_{x,\varepsilon}:=U_{+,x,\varepsilon}=\mathcal{T}_{x,\varepsilon}(\chi_{R(\varepsilon)}U_{+}),\qquad R(\varepsilon)\to\infty,\ \varepsilon R(\varepsilon)\to 0,

be the boundary bubble at
x
with scale
\varepsilon
.

(i) Linear stratum (first order; (BG
2+
)).
If
\operatorname{sgn}(\rho_{n}^{\mathrm{conf}})\,H_{g}(x)\geq h_{0}>0
, then for all sufficiently small
\varepsilon
,

\mathrm{Def}^{\mathrm{Esc}}_{g}(v_{x,\varepsilon})\ \geq\ \tfrac{1}{2}\,S_{\ast}\,\rho_{n}^{\mathrm{conf}}\,H_{g}(x)\,\varepsilon.

Conversely, if
\operatorname{sgn}(\rho_{n}^{\mathrm{conf}})\,H_{g}(x)\leq-h_{0}<0
, then for
\varepsilon
small one has
\mathrm{Def}^{\mathrm{Esc}}_{g}(v_{x,\varepsilon})<0
, contradicting
C^{*}_{\mathrm{Esc}}(M,g)=S_{\ast}
; thus such centers cannot occur in the small-deficit regime.

(ii) Quadratic stratum (second order; (BG
3+
),
n\geq 4
).
If
n\geq 4
and
H_{g}(x)=0
, then the second-order one-bubble expansion at
x
is

\mathrm{Def}^{\mathrm{Esc}}_{g}(v_{x,\varepsilon})\ =\ S_{\ast}\,\mathfrak{R}_{g}(x)\,\varepsilon^{2}\ +\ O(\varepsilon^{3}),

uniformly for
x
in compact subsets of
\partial M
. In particular, if
\mathfrak{R}_{g}(x)>0
, there exist
c,C>0
(depending only on
(M,g,n)
and the local bounded-geometry data) such that, for
\varepsilon
small,

\mathrm{Def}^{\mathrm{Esc}}_{g}(v_{x,\varepsilon})\ \geq\ c\,S_{\ast}\,\mathfrak{R}_{g}(x)\,\varepsilon^{2}\ -\ C\,\varepsilon^{3}.

(iii) Cubic stratum (third order; (BG
4+
),
n\geq 4
).
If
n\geq 4
and
H_{g}(x)=\mathfrak{R}_{g}(x)=0
, then the third-order one-bubble expansion at
x
is

\mathrm{Def}^{\mathrm{Esc}}_{g}(v_{x,\varepsilon})\ =\ S_{\ast}\,\Theta_{g}(x)\,\varepsilon^{3}\ +\ O(\varepsilon^{4}),

again uniformly on compact subsets of
\partial M
. In particular, if
\Theta_{g}(x)>0
, there exist
c,C>0
such that, for
\varepsilon
small,

\mathrm{Def}^{\mathrm{Esc}}_{g}(v_{x,\varepsilon})\ \geq\ c\,S_{\ast}\,\Theta_{g}(x)\,\varepsilon^{3}\ -\ C\,\varepsilon^{4}.

In each case, the implicit constants depend only on
(M,g,n)
and the relevant bounded-geometry threshold (
(BG
2+
)
,
(BG
3+
)
, or
(BG
4+
)
).

Proof.

Let
m\in\{1,2,3\}
be the first index with nonvanishing normalized coefficient at
x
:

c_{1}(x)=S_{\ast}\,\rho_{n}^{\mathrm{conf}}H_{g}(x),\qquad c_{2}(x)=S_{\ast}\,\mathfrak{R}_{g}(x)\ \ (n\geq 4),\qquad c_{3}(x)=S_{\ast}\,\Theta_{g}(x)\ \ (n\geq 4).

For
m=1
, the first-order one-bubble expansion (Lemma
2.41
) gives

\mathcal{J}_{g}[v_{x,\varepsilon}]=S_{\ast}\bigl(1+\rho_{n}^{\mathrm{conf}}\,H_{g}(x)\,\varepsilon\bigr)+o(\varepsilon),

so

\mathrm{Def}^{\mathrm{Esc}}_{g}(v_{x,\varepsilon})=\mathcal{J}_{g}[v_{x,\varepsilon}]-C^{*}_{\mathrm{Esc}}(M,g)=S_{\ast}\,\rho_{n}^{\mathrm{conf}}H_{g}(x)\,\varepsilon+o(\varepsilon).

This yields the claimed lower bound in the favorable sign case and the exclusion in the unfavorable sign case, for
\varepsilon
sufficiently small (depending on
h_{0}
and the geometric data).

For
m=2
, assume
n\geq 4
and
H_{g}(x)=0
. By the second-order one-bubble expansion (Theorem
3.6
under (BG
3+
)),

\mathcal{J}_{g}[v_{x,\varepsilon}]=S_{\ast}\bigl(1+\mathfrak{R}_{g}(x)\,\varepsilon^{2}\bigr)+O(\varepsilon^{3}),

so

\mathrm{Def}^{\mathrm{Esc}}_{g}(v_{x,\varepsilon})=S_{\ast}\,\mathfrak{R}_{g}(x)\,\varepsilon^{2}+O(\varepsilon^{3}).

If
\mathfrak{R}_{g}(x)>0
, the stated inequality follows by shrinking
\varepsilon
so that the
O(\varepsilon^{3})
term is dominated by a fixed fraction of the leading
\varepsilon^{2}
term.

For
m=3
, assume
n\geq 4
and
H_{g}(x)=\mathfrak{R}_{g}(x)=0
. By the third-order expansion (Lemma
3.27
under (BG
4+
)),

\mathcal{J}_{g}[v_{x,\varepsilon}]=S_{\ast}\bigl(1+\Theta_{g}(x)\,\varepsilon^{3}\bigr)+O(\varepsilon^{4}),

and the same argument yields the cubic estimate when
\Theta_{g}(x)>0
.
∎

Remark 2.38

(Scale vs. deficit for general perturbations)
.

The lemma above is a
one-bubble
statement: it controls
\mathrm{Def}^{\mathrm{Esc}}_{g}(v_{x,\varepsilon})
directly in terms of
\varepsilon
in the three strata. For a general
u
with best-fit
scaled
boundary bubble
v^{\sharp}=a^{\sharp}U_{+,x,\varepsilon}
(as in Lemma
2.16
)
and remainder
w=u-v^{\sharp}
(so that
\int_{\partial M}(v^{\sharp})^{q-1}w=0
), one combines these expansions with the quantitative stability estimate (Theorem
2.24
) and the first-order drift bound (Lemma
2.25
).

In the
linear stratum
(where
\rho_{n}^{\mathrm{conf}}H_{g}(x)\neq 0
) this yields the sharp scale-deficit law

\varepsilon\ \lesssim\ \mathrm{Def}^{\mathrm{Esc}}_{g}(u),

as detailed in Lemma
2.36
. In the
quadratic
and
cubic
strata, the higher-order coefficients
\mathfrak{R}_{g}(x)
and
\Theta_{g}(x)
still play a decisive role in the finer selection and multiplicity arguments, but we will
not
need an explicit global
\varepsilon^{2}
- or
\varepsilon^{3}
-scale law for arbitrary perturbations
u
.

Corollary 2.39

(Boundary inverse selection: probing
H_{g}
and
\mathfrak{R}_{g}
)
.

Assume
n\geq 4
and
(BG
3+
)
. Fix a Fermi collar on
\partial M
and set
S_{\ast}=C^{*}_{\mathrm{Esc}}(S^{n}_{+})
. For
v_{x,\varepsilon}:=\mathcal{T}_{x,\varepsilon}U_{+}
define

\mathscr{M}_{\varepsilon}(x):=\frac{\mathcal{J}_{g}[v_{x,\varepsilon}]-S_{\ast}}{\varepsilon},\qquad\mathscr{N}_{\varepsilon}(x):=\frac{\mathcal{J}_{g}[v_{x,\varepsilon}]-S_{\ast}-S_{\ast}\rho_{n}^{\mathrm{conf}}H_{g}(x)\varepsilon}{\varepsilon^{2}}.

Then, uniformly on compact subsets of
\partial M
,

\lim_{\varepsilon\downarrow 0}\mathscr{M}_{\varepsilon}(x)=S_{\ast}\rho_{n}^{\mathrm{conf}}\,H_{g}(x),\qquad\mathscr{N}_{\varepsilon}(x)=S_{\ast}\mathfrak{R}_{g}(x)+O(\varepsilon).

In particular,
H_{g}
and
\mathfrak{R}_{g}
are recoverable from asymptotic Escobar measurements with quantitative stability.

Remark 2.40
.

In dimension
n=3
, the first-order quantity
\mathscr{M}_{\varepsilon}(x)
still detects
H_{g}(x)
via Lemma
2.41
, but we do not use a second-order inverse formula for
\mathfrak{R}_{g}
in this paper.

Lemma 2.41

(First-order Escobar quotient expansion)
.

Assume
(BG
2+
)
and
n\geq 3
. For
x\in\partial M
and
v_{x,\varepsilon}
the standard boundary bubble (Fermi chart at
x
, scale
\varepsilon
),

(2.51)

\mathcal{J}_{g}[v_{x,\varepsilon}]\;=\;C^{*}_{\mathrm{Esc}}(S^{n}_{+})\Big(1+\rho_{n}^{\mathrm{conf}}\,H_{g}(x)\,\varepsilon\Big)+o(\varepsilon)\qquad(\varepsilon\downarrow 0),

where
\rho_{n}^{\mathrm{conf}}
is the (dimension-dependent) first-order coefficient. For
n\geq 4
,
\rho_{n}^{\mathrm{conf}}
is given by the moment formula (
2.18
). In
n=3
the same expansion holds with some
\rho_{3}^{\mathrm{conf}}>0
, defined via the spherical model as in Remark
2.12
; we do not use an explicit
L^{2}
-moment identity for
\rho_{3}^{\mathrm{conf}}
.

Lemma 2.42

(Sign and value of the first–order conformal coefficient)
.

Let
U_{+}
be a half-space optimizer for the covariant Escobar functional.

(a)

For
n\geq 4
one has

(2.52)

\rho_{n}^{\mathrm{conf}}\;=\;\left(\frac{2}{n-1}\,\mathfrak{g}^{(1)}_{\tan}-\mathfrak{g}^{(1)}\right)\;+\;\frac{n-2}{2}\,\Theta\;=\;\frac{(n-2)^{2}}{2(n-1)}\,\Theta\;>\;0,

where
\mathfrak{g}^{(1)},\mathfrak{g}^{(1)}_{\tan},\Theta
are the dimensionless moments from §
2.1
, and
\rho_{n}^{\mathrm{conf}}
coincides with the coefficient recorded in (
2.18
).

(b)

In dimension
n=3
there exists a constant
\rho_{3}^{\mathrm{conf}}>0
(depending only on the choice of optimizer
U_{+}
) such that the first-order expansion (
2.51
) holds with this coefficient. In particular,
\rho_{3}^{\mathrm{conf}}>0
, although we do not use an
L^{2}
-trace representation of
\rho_{3}^{\mathrm{conf}}
.

Proof.

For
n\geq 4
the trace
U_{+}(\cdot,0)
lies in
L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n-1})
, so Lemma
2.11
applies and gives the identities

\mathfrak{g}^{(1)}=\tfrac{1}{2}\,\Theta,\qquad\mathfrak{g}^{(1)}_{\tan}=\tfrac{1}{4}\,\Theta.

Inserting these into (
2.18
) yields

\left(\frac{2}{n-1}\,\mathfrak{g}^{(1)}_{\tan}-\mathfrak{g}^{(1)}\right)+\frac{n-2}{2}\,\Theta=\left(\frac{1}{2(n-1)}-\frac{1}{2}\right)\Theta+\frac{n-2}{2}\,\Theta=\frac{(n-2)^{2}}{2(n-1)}\,\Theta,

which is strictly positive.

For
n=3
, the existence of a first-order expansion (
2.51
) with some finite coefficient
\rho_{3}^{\mathrm{conf}}
is ensured by the spherical model construction in Remark
2.12
applied at the level of the Escobar quotient. Its positivity is verified on the hemisphere model and then transferred by conformal covariance. No explicit
L^{2}
-moment formula is needed in the
n=3
case.
∎

Remark 2.43

(Selection direction)
.

With the inner normal convention, strictly convex Euclidean boundaries satisfy
H_{g}>0
(cf. Remark
2.14
). Since
\rho_{n}^{\mathrm{conf}}>0
, the first-order correction

\mathcal{J}_{g}[v_{x,\varepsilon}]=S_{\ast}\Big(1+\rho_{n}^{\mathrm{conf}}\,H_{g}(x)\,\varepsilon\Big)+o(\varepsilon)

is lower where
H_{g}(x)
is smaller and higher where
H_{g}(x)
is larger. Thus, near the threshold, boundary concentration (if any) selects points that
minimize

H_{g}
on
\partial M
(points of least positive curvature in the strictly convex case, or more generally points where
H_{g}
is most negative when concave regions are present).

2.7.
Boundary vs. interior: first-order competition

Theorem 2.44

(Boundary vs. interior competition: leading orders)
.

Assume
(BG
2+
)
and
C^{*}_{\mathrm{Esc}}(M,g)=C^{*}_{\mathrm{Esc}}(S^{n}_{+})=S_{\ast}
.
Let
(u_{k})
be a sequence on the Escobar constraint slice with
\mathrm{Def}^{\mathrm{Esc}}_{g}(u_{k})\to 0
.
Any blow-up (loss of compactness) at the level
S_{\ast}
concentrates at a boundary point

x_{\partial}\in\partial M
; the genuinely interior channel is excluded in the small–deficit
regime (cf. Lemma
2.27
).

For a boundary bubble competitor
v_{x,\varepsilon}=\mathcal{T}_{x,\varepsilon}(\chi_{R(\varepsilon)}U_{+})

in the diagonal single–bubble convention (Remark
2.7
(a)), with
\varepsilon\downarrow 0
, we have,
uniformly in
the center on compact boundary collars,

\mathcal{J}_{g}[v_{x,\varepsilon}]-S_{\ast}=\ S_{\ast}\,\rho_{n}^{\mathrm{conf}}\,H_{g}(x)\,\varepsilon\ +\ o(\varepsilon),

where
\rho_{n}^{\mathrm{conf}}>0
is the first-order coefficient from Lemma
2.42
.
In particular, among boundary centers, the first-order term favors points where
H_{g}
is smaller
(
i.e.
it selects
\arg\min_{\partial M}H_{g}
at the level of values).

If, moreover,
(u_{k})
is a Palais–Smale sequence (or a sequence of constrained critical points),
then boundary blow-up centers satisfy the additional stationarity condition

\nabla H_{g}(x_{k})=O(\varepsilon_{k})
, hence concentrate at
\mathrm{Crit}(H_{g})
.
(See Theorem
4.18
.)

2.8.
Compactness below the hemisphere

Corollary 2.45

(Compactness away from the hemisphere value)
.

Let
(u_{k})
be a minimizing sequence for
\mathcal{J}_{g}
on the Escobar constraint slice

\|u_{k}\|_{L^{2^{*}_{\partial}}(\partial M)}=1
, with
\mathrm{Def}^{\mathrm{Esc}}_{g}(u_{k})\to 0
.
If
C^{*}_{\mathrm{Esc}}(M,g)<C^{*}_{\mathrm{Esc}}(S^{n}_{+})
, then

(u_{k})
is precompact in
H^{1}(M)
; in particular, along a subsequence

u_{k}\to u_{\infty}
strongly in
H^{1}(M)
and
u_{\infty}
is an extremal.

Proof.

By concentration-compactness, any loss of compactness at the minimizing level would force bubbling at the hemisphere value. The strict gap
C^{*}_{\mathrm{Esc}}(M,g)<C^{*}_{\mathrm{Esc}}(S^{n}_{+})
rules this out, hence the sequence is precompact.
∎

Theorem 2.46

(Conditional compactness below the hemisphere)
.

Assume
(Y
{}^{+}_{\partial}
)
and
(BG
2+
)
. If

C^{*}_{\mathrm{Esc}}(M,g)\ <\ C^{*}_{\mathrm{Esc}}(S^{n}_{+}),

then every Palais–Smale sequence for the Escobar quotient
\mathcal{J}_{g}
on the constraint slice

\|u_{k}\|_{L^{2^{*}_{\partial}}(\partial M)}=1
at level
C^{*}_{\mathrm{Esc}}(M,g)
is precompact in
H^{1}(M)
; bubbling cannot occur. At the threshold
C^{*}_{\mathrm{Esc}}(M,g)=C^{*}_{\mathrm{Esc}}(S^{n}_{+})
, any
Palais–Smale
blow-up sequence (if it occurs)
concentrates at critical points of
H_{g}
, selected at first order by
S_{\ast}\rho_{n}^{\mathrm{conf}}H_{g}
(cf. Lemma
2.41
and Lemma
2.42
).
In dimensions
n\geq 4
, and under
(BG
3+
)
together with an appropriate nondegeneracy hypothesis, one has in addition a second-order refinement at
H_{g}=0
governed explicitly by Theorem
3.6
in terms of the renormalized mass
\mathfrak{R}_{g}
.

Proof sketch.

Write
q=2^{*}_{\partial}=\frac{2(n-1)}{n-2}
and work on the slice

\mathcal{S}=\Big\{u\in H^{1}(M):\ \|u\|_{L^{q}(\partial M)}=1\Big\}.

On
\mathcal{S}
one has
\mathcal{J}_{g}=\mathcal{E}_{g}=\langle u,u\rangle_{E,g}
.

Step 1 (
H^{1}
-boundedness at finite level).

Let
(u_{k})\subset\mathcal{S}
be a Palais–Smale sequence for
\mathcal{J}_{g}
at level
\Lambda<\infty
, i.e.

\mathcal{J}_{g}[u_{k}]\to\Lambda
and
\|\mathsf{grad}_{\mathcal{S}}\mathcal{J}_{g}(u_{k})\|_{H^{1}(M)}\to 0
.
By coercivity (Y
{}^{+}_{\partial}
) there exists
c_{0}>0
such that

c_{0}\,\|u\|_{H^{1}(M)}^{2}\ \leq\ \langle u,u\rangle_{E,g}=\mathcal{J}_{g}[u]\qquad\forall\,u\in\mathcal{S},

hence
\|u_{k}\|_{H^{1}(M)}=O(1)
.

Step 2 (Lagrange multipliers and convergence
\lambda_{k}\to\Lambda
).

The constrained PS condition on
\mathcal{S}
is equivalent to the existence of multipliers
\lambda_{k}\in\mathbb{R}
such that

(2.53)

L_{g}^{\circ}u_{k}\to 0\ \text{in }H^{-1}(M),\qquad B_{g}^{\circ}u_{k}-\lambda_{k}\,|u_{k}|^{q-2}u_{k}\to 0\ \text{in }H^{-1/2}(\partial M).

Testing the boundary residual in (
2.53
) against
u_{k}
and using
\|u_{k}\|_{L^{q}(\partial M)}=1
gives

\int_{\partial M}u_{k}\,B_{g}^{\circ}u_{k}\,d\sigma_{g}=\lambda_{k}+o(1).

Testing the interior residual against
u_{k}
gives
\int_{M}u_{k}\,L_{g}^{\circ}u_{k}\,dV_{g}=o(1)
, since
u_{k}
is bounded in
H^{1}
.
By the weak Green identity,

\mathcal{J}_{g}[u_{k}]=\langle u_{k},u_{k}\rangle_{E,g}=\int_{M}u_{k}\,L_{g}^{\circ}u_{k}\,dV_{g}+\int_{\partial M}u_{k}\,B_{g}^{\circ}u_{k}\,d\sigma_{g},

hence
\lambda_{k}=\mathcal{J}_{g}[u_{k}]+o(1)\to\lambda_{\infty}:=\Lambda
.

Step 3 (Concentration–compactness and extraction of half-space critical points).

Set
\mu_{k}:=|u_{k}|^{q}\,d\sigma_{g}
on
\partial M
. By concentration–compactness at the critical trace exponent, after passing
to a subsequence,

\mu_{k}\ \xrightharpoonup{\ast}\ |u_{\infty}|^{q}\,d\sigma_{g}+\sum_{j=1}^{J}m_{j}\,\delta_{p_{j}},\qquad m_{j}>0,\ p_{j}\in\partial M,

for some finite
J
, where
u_{k}\rightharpoonup u_{\infty}
in
H^{1}(M)
and
u_{k}\to u_{\infty}
in
L^{r}(\partial M)
for
1\leq r<q
.
In particular,

1=\int_{\partial M}|u_{\infty}|^{q}\,d\sigma_{g}+\sum_{j=1}^{J}m_{j}.

Around each atom
p_{j}
one performs the standard Fermi rescaling at a suitable scale
\varepsilon_{k}^{j}\downarrow 0
and uses the
PS residual (
2.53
) together with
\lambda_{k}\to\lambda_{\infty}
to obtain a nontrivial half-space limit

U^{(j)}\in\dot{H}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+})
solving

-\Delta U^{(j)}=0\ \text{in }\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+},\qquad-\partial_{y_{n}}U^{(j)}=\lambda_{\infty}\,|U^{(j)}|^{q-2}U^{(j)}\ \text{on }\partial\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+},

and carrying boundary mass
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n-1}}|U^{(j)}(\cdot,0)|^{q}\,dy^{\prime}=m_{j}
.

Step 4 (Bubble obstruction when
\Lambda<S_{\ast}
).

Testing the half-space equation against
U^{(j)}
gives

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+}}|\nabla U^{(j)}|^{2}\,dy=\lambda_{\infty}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n-1}}|U^{(j)}(\cdot,0)|^{q}\,dy^{\prime}=\lambda_{\infty}\,m_{j}.

By the sharp half-space trace inequality (whose sharp constant equals
S_{\ast}
by conformal invariance),

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+}}|\nabla U^{(j)}|^{2}\,dy\ \geq\ S_{\ast}\,m_{j}^{2/q}.

Combining yields

\lambda_{\infty}\,m_{j}\ \geq\ S_{\ast}\,m_{j}^{2/q}\qquad\Longleftrightarrow\qquad m_{j}^{\,1-\frac{2}{q}}\ \geq\ \frac{S_{\ast}}{\lambda_{\infty}}.

Since
1-\frac{2}{q}=\frac{1}{n-1}>0
, if
\Lambda=\lambda_{\infty}<S_{\ast}
then
\frac{S_{\ast}}{\lambda_{\infty}}>1
, hence
m_{j}>1
.
This contradicts
\sum_{j=1}^{J}m_{j}\leq 1
. Therefore
J=0
and no bubbling occurs.

Step 5 (Strong convergence at the minimizing level).

We now apply the above with
\Lambda=C^{*}_{\mathrm{Esc}}(M,g)<S_{\ast}
. Since
J=0
, we have

\int_{\partial M}|u_{\infty}|^{q}\,d\sigma_{g}=1
, so
u_{\infty}\in\mathcal{S}
.
By weak lower semicontinuity,

\mathcal{J}_{g}[u_{\infty}]=\langle u_{\infty},u_{\infty}\rangle_{E,g}\ \leq\ \liminf_{k\to\infty}\langle u_{k},u_{k}\rangle_{E,g}=\lim_{k\to\infty}\mathcal{J}_{g}[u_{k}]=C^{*}_{\mathrm{Esc}}(M,g).

Since
u_{\infty}\in\mathcal{S}
and
C^{*}_{\mathrm{Esc}}(M,g)
is the infimum of
\mathcal{J}_{g}
on
\mathcal{S}
, the reverse inequality holds,
hence
\mathcal{J}_{g}[u_{\infty}]=C^{*}_{\mathrm{Esc}}(M,g)
and
\|u_{k}\|_{E,g}^{2}\to\|u_{\infty}\|_{E,g}^{2}
.
Together with
u_{k}\rightharpoonup u_{\infty}
in
H^{1}(M)
(hence weakly in
\|\cdot\|_{E,g}
), this implies

u_{k}\to u_{\infty}
strongly in
\|\cdot\|_{E,g}
, and therefore strongly in
H^{1}(M)
by norm equivalence.
This proves precompactness below the hemisphere value.

Threshold remarks.

At the threshold
C^{*}_{\mathrm{Esc}}(M,g)=S_{\ast}
the above obstruction disappears; leading-order selection of blow-up centers by

\mathrm{Crit}(H_{g})
and refined selection by
\mathfrak{R}_{g}
(in
n\geq 5
under the refined regime) are addressed separately
in Theorems
4.18
and
4.25
.
∎

2.9.
Plain trace addendum: sharp constants, first/second order, and attainment

Scope (plain trace).

We now work with the
non-covariant
(plain) trace quotient on
(M,g)
.
Throughout this subsection we assume
n\geq 4
. (The flat trace inequality and the
Bianchi–Egnell stability result below hold for all
n\geq 3
, but our geometric
first/second-order expansions and trichotomy statements will only be used, and
are only asserted, for
n\geq 4
.)

(2.54)

T_{M}(u)\ :=\ \frac{\|u\|_{L^{2^{*}_{\partial}}(\partial M)}^{2^{*}_{\partial}}}{\Big(\displaystyle\int_{M}|\nabla u|_{g}^{2}\,dV_{g}\Big)^{\alpha}},\qquad\alpha=\frac{2^{*}_{\partial}}{2}=\frac{n-1}{n-2},

which depends only on the Dirichlet energy in the denominator. This functional is distinct from the covariant Escobar quotient and is included here for completeness; it is not used in the covariant thread. Here
2^{*}_{\partial}=\frac{2(n-1)}{n-2}
. We use
2^{*}_{\partial}
(not
2^{\sharp}
) throughout for the critical trace exponent.

Homogeneous gauge (to exclude constants).

Since the denominator in (
2.54
) is the pure Dirichlet energy, adding constants leaves the denominator unchanged; on a compact
M
this forces
S_{\mathrm{tr}}(M,g)=+\infty
unless one quotients out constants. We therefore work in the homogeneous gauge

H^{1}_{\#}(M)\;:=\;\Big\{u\in H^{1}(M):\ \int_{M}u\,dV_{g}=0\Big\},

equivalently
H^{1}(M)/\mathbb{R}
. All suprema for
T_{M}
below are taken over

H^{1}_{\#}(M)\setminus\{0\}
.

2.9.1.
Flat sharp trace, constants, and notation (plain trace)

On
\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+}=\{(y^{\prime},y_{n}):y_{n}>0\}
, the sharp trace inequality (Escobar
[
25
]
) reads

(2.55)

\|u(\cdot,0)\|_{L^{2^{*}_{\partial}}(\mathbb{R}^{n-1})}\ \leq\ C_{\mathrm{tr}}(\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+})\,\|\nabla u\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+})},\qquad u\in H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+})\cap L^{2^{*}_{\partial}}(\partial\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+}).

Set

S_{\mathrm{tr}}(\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+})\ :=\ \big(C_{\mathrm{tr}}(\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+})\big)^{2^{*}_{\partial}},

so that (
2.55
) is equivalent to

\|u(\cdot,0)\|_{L^{2^{*}_{\partial}}}^{2^{*}_{\partial}}\ \leq\ S_{\mathrm{tr}}(\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+})\,\|\nabla u\|_{L^{2}}^{2^{*}_{\partial}}.

Let
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{tr}}
denote the manifold of optimizers for (
2.55
) modulo translations/dilations (see
[
25
]
); fix a representative
U_{+}
and normalize
\|\nabla U_{+}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+})}=1
.

Theorem 2.47

(Bianchi–Egnell stability for the trace inequality
[
36
]
)
.

Let
n\geq 3
and write
q=2^{*}_{\partial}=\frac{2(n-1)}{n-2}
. Let
K>0
be the sharp constant in

\|\nabla\varphi\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+})}^{2}\ \geq\ K^{2}\,\|\varphi(\cdot,0)\|_{L^{q}(\mathbb{R}^{n-1})}^{2}\qquad\text{for all }\varphi\in H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+}),

equivalently
\|\varphi(\cdot,0)\|_{L^{q}}\leq C_{\mathrm{tr}}\|\nabla\varphi\|_{2}
with
K=C_{\mathrm{tr}}^{-1}
. Let
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{tr}}
be the set of all optimizers on
\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+}
. Then there exists
\alpha(n)>0
such that for all
\varphi\in H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+})

(2.56)

\|\nabla\varphi\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+})}^{2}\ -\ K^{2}\,\|\varphi(\cdot,0)\|_{L^{q}(\mathbb{R}^{n-1})}^{2}\ \geq\ \alpha(n)\,\mathrm{dist}_{\dot{H}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+})}\big(\varphi,\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{tr}}\big)^{2}.

Lemma 2.48

(Squared vs. critical-power deficit under
\|\nabla f\|_{2}=1
)
.

Let
S_{\mathrm{tr}}(\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+})=C_{\mathrm{tr}}^{\,q}
with
q=2^{*}_{\partial}
and assume
\|\nabla f\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+})}=1
. Then

S_{\mathrm{tr}}(\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+})-\|f(\cdot,0)\|_{L^{q}}^{q}\ \geq\ C_{\mathrm{tr}}^{\,q}\Big(1-K^{2}\,\|f(\cdot,0)\|_{L^{q}}^{2}\Big).

Consequently, by (
2.56
),

(2.57)

S_{\mathrm{tr}}(\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+})-\|f(\cdot,0)\|_{L^{q}}^{q}\ \geq\ c(n)\,\mathrm{dist}_{\dot{H}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+})}\big(f,\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{tr}}\big)^{2},

for some
c(n)>0
.

Proof.

Since
\|\nabla f\|_{2}=1
, we have
t:=K^{2}\|f(\cdot,0)\|_{L^{q}}^{2}\in[0,1]
. Then

S_{\mathrm{tr}}-\|f\|_{L^{q}}^{q}=C_{\mathrm{tr}}^{\,q}\big(1-t^{q/2}\big)\ \geq\ C_{\mathrm{tr}}^{\,q}(1-t)=C_{\mathrm{tr}}^{\,q}\Big(1-K^{2}\,\|f(\cdot,0)\|_{L^{q}}^{2}\Big),

since
t^{q/2}\leq t
on
[0,1]
for
q/2\geq 1
. Combining with (
2.56
) with
\|\nabla f\|_{2}=1
yields (
2.57
) with
c(n)=C_{\mathrm{tr}}^{\,q}\alpha(n)
.
∎

Lemma 2.49

(Plain-trace first-order coefficient and sign)
.

Assume
n\geq 4
. Let
U_{+}
be a flat optimizer for (
2.55
) on
\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+}
, normalized by

\|\nabla U_{+}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+})}=1
. For
(M,g)
satisfying
(BG
2+
)
and any boundary point

x\in\partial M
, define the boundary bubble in the diagonal single–bubble convention

v_{x,\varepsilon}:=\mathcal{T}_{x,\varepsilon}(\chi_{R(\varepsilon)}U_{+}),\qquad R(\varepsilon)\to\infty,\ \ \varepsilon R(\varepsilon)\to 0

(cf. Remark
2.7
(a)),
so that the cutoff defect is absorbed into the
o(\varepsilon)
remainder. Then, as
\varepsilon\downarrow 0
,

(2.58)

T_{M}(v_{x,\varepsilon})\ =\ S_{\mathrm{tr}}(\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+})\,\Big(1+\rho_{n}\,H_{g}(x)\,\varepsilon+o(\varepsilon)\Big),\qquad\rho_{n}:=-\,\frac{2^{*}_{\partial}}{2}\Big(\tfrac{2}{n-1}\,\mathfrak{g}^{(1)}_{\mathrm{tan}}-\mathfrak{g}^{(1)}\Big).

Here
\mathfrak{g}^{(1)}
and
\mathfrak{g}^{(1)}_{\mathrm{tan}}
are the first
bulk and tangential moments of
U_{+}
. Moreover,
U_{+}
is harmonic in
\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+}
(Euler-Lagrange), so the identities of Lemma
2.11
apply:

\mathfrak{g}^{(1)}=\tfrac{1}{2}\Theta
and
\mathfrak{g}^{(1)}_{\mathrm{tan}}=\tfrac{1}{4}\Theta
, which imply

\tfrac{2}{n-1}\mathfrak{g}^{(1)}_{\mathrm{tan}}-\mathfrak{g}^{(1)}=-\tfrac{n-2}{2(n-1)}\Theta<0
and hence
\rho_{n}=\tfrac{1}{2}\,\Theta>0
. Therefore, at first order,
T_{M}(v_{x,\varepsilon})

increases with the mean curvature
H_{g}(x)
.

Remark 2.50

(Second order for the plain trace)
.

Assume
n\geq 4
and (BG
3+
). Carrying the Fermi expansions for the Dirichlet energy and for the boundary Jacobian to
O(\varepsilon^{2})
yields

T_{M}(v_{x,\varepsilon})\;=\;S_{\mathrm{tr}}(\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+})\Big(1+\rho_{n}\,H_{g}(x)\,\varepsilon\;+\;\varepsilon^{2}\,\mathfrak{R}^{\mathrm{tr}}_{g}(x)\;+\;o(\varepsilon^{2})\Big),

with a
plain-trace renormalized mass

\mathfrak{R}^{\mathrm{tr}}_{g}
that is a fixed linear combination (with dimension-dependent coefficients) of

\operatorname{Ric}_{g}(\nu,\nu)(x),\qquad\mathrm{Scal}_{g|\partial M}(x),\qquad|\mathring{\mathrm{II}}(x)|^{2}.

We do not need the explicit constants below; only the existence of this expansion and the continuity of
\mathfrak{R}^{\mathrm{tr}}_{g}
on
\partial M
. We do not attempt to renormalize or use the
n=3
case here.

Mean-zero gauge (well-posedness on compact
M
).

Since adding constants leaves the Dirichlet-energy denominator unchanged while it changes the boundary norm,
S_{\mathrm{tr}}(M,g)=\sup T_{M}
would be infinite on a compact manifold.
Accordingly, throughout the plain-trace addendum we work on the mean-zero slice

H^{1}_{\#}(M):=\Big\{u\in H^{1}(M):\ \int_{M}u\,dV_{g}=0\Big\},

and all suprema/maximizing sequences for
T_{M}
are understood over
H^{1}_{\#}(M)\setminus\{0\}
.

Theorem 2.51

(Plain trace: threshold vs. super-Euclidean alternative)
.

Assume
n\geq 4
and work in the homogeneous gauge
H^{1}_{\#}(M)
introduced above. Let

S_{\mathrm{tr}}(M,g):=\sup_{u\in H^{1}_{\#}(M)\setminus\{0\}}T_{M}(u),\qquad S_{\mathrm{tr}}(\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+})\ \text{the half-space sharp constant.}

Then the following hold.

(a)

(
Universal lower bound
) One has
S_{\mathrm{tr}}(M,g)\geq S_{\mathrm{tr}}(\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+})
.

(b)

(
Strict super-Euclidean
\Rightarrow
attainment
) If
S_{\mathrm{tr}}(M,g)>S_{\mathrm{tr}}(\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+})
,
then every maximizing sequence is precompact in
H^{1}(M)
and
T_{M}
attains its supremum on
H^{1}_{\#}(M)
.

(c)

(
Positive first order
\Rightarrow
strict super-Euclidean
