0=\rho_{n}^{\mathrm{conf}}H_{g}(x_{\ell})+2\mathfrak{R}_{g}(x_{\ell})\varepsilon_{\ell}+o(\varepsilon_{\ell}).

(If
n\geq 6
, the remainder improves to
O(\varepsilon_{\ell}^{2})
.)

Step 3 (Centers approach
\mathrm{Crit}(H_{g})
).

By Theorem
4.18
with
k_{\ast}=1
,

\|\nabla H_{g}(x_{\ell})\|\ \leq\ C\,\varepsilon_{\ell}.

Since
H_{g}
is Morse, this implies
x_{\ell}\to p\in\mathrm{Crit}(H_{g})
and
d_{\partial}(x_{\ell},p)=O(\varepsilon_{\ell})
.
Taylor expansion at
p
gives

(4.35)

H_{g}(x_{\ell})=H_{g}(p)\ +\ O(\varepsilon_{\ell}^{2}),\qquad\mathfrak{R}_{g}(x_{\ell})=\mathfrak{R}_{g}(p)\ +\ O(\varepsilon_{\ell}).

Step 4 (Eliminate the case
H_{g}(p)\neq 0
).

Substituting (
4.35
) into (
4.34
) and letting
\ell\to\infty
yields

0=\rho_{n}^{\mathrm{conf}}\,H_{g}(p)\ +\ o(1),

so
H_{g}(p)=0
(since
\rho_{n}^{\mathrm{conf}}\neq 0
).

Step 5 (Positive mass yields a contradiction).

With
H_{g}(p)=0
and
d_{\partial}(x_{\ell},p)=O(\varepsilon_{\ell})
, (
4.35
) implies

H_{g}(x_{\ell})=O(\varepsilon_{\ell}^{2})
and
\mathfrak{R}_{g}(x_{\ell})=\mathfrak{R}_{g}(p)+O(\varepsilon_{\ell})
.
Plugging into (
4.34
) gives

0=2\,\mathfrak{R}_{g}(p)\,\varepsilon_{\ell}\ +\ O(\varepsilon_{\ell}^{2}),

hence
\mathfrak{R}_{g}(p)=0
, contradicting

\min_{x\in\mathrm{Crit}(H_{g})}\mathfrak{R}_{g}(x)\ >\ 0.

Thus no blow-up can occur;
(u_{\ell})
is precompact in
H^{1}(M)
.
∎

Remark 4.30
.

If
H_{g}\equiv 0
in a boundary collar and
\min_{\partial M}\mathfrak{R}_{g}>0
, Step 4 is void and the same argument applies
(with
p
arbitrary in the collar).

Theorem 4.31

(Generic nondegeneracy at boundary critical points; conditional threshold compactness)
.

Assume
n\geq 5
and fix an integer
m\gg 1
.
Let
\mathrm{Met}^{m}(M)
be the Banach manifold of
C^{m}
Riemannian metrics on
M
, and let

\mathrm{Met}^{m}_{Y,BG}(M):=\Big\{g\in\mathrm{Met}^{m}(M):\ g\ \text{satisfies {(Y${}^{+}_{\partial}$)} and {(BG${}^{3+}$)}}\Big\}.

(Recall this is a
C^{m}
–open subset.)
Then there exists a
C^{m}
–open dense subset

\mathcal{G}\ \subset\ \mathrm{Met}^{m}_{Y,BG}(M)

such that for every
g\in\mathcal{G}
the following hold:

(a)

H_{g}
is a Morse function on
\partial M
; in particular
\mathrm{Crit}(H_{g})
is finite.

(b)

The renormalized mass does not vanish at boundary critical points:

\mathfrak{R}_{g}(x)\ \neq\ 0\qquad\forall\,x\in\mathrm{Crit}(H_{g}).

Equivalently,

\big\{x\in\partial M:\ \nabla_{\partial}H_{g}(x)=0\ \text{and}\ \mathfrak{R}_{g}(x)=0\big\}=\varnothing.

Consequently, if in addition
g\in\mathcal{G}
satisfies the hemisphere threshold

C^{*}_{\mathrm{Esc}}(M,g)=C^{*}_{\mathrm{Esc}}(S^{n}_{+})=S_{\ast},

and moreover

\min_{x\in\mathrm{Crit}(H_{g})}\ \mathfrak{R}_{g}(x)\ >\ 0,

Then every sequence of
positive
constrained critical points

u_{\ell}\in\mathcal{S}
of
\mathcal{J}_{g}
on
\mathcal{S}
(equivalently of
\mathcal{E}_{g}
on
\mathcal{S}
) with
\mathcal{E}_{g}[u_{\ell}]\to S_{\ast}
is precompact in
H^{1}(M)
.

Proof.

Set
\mathcal{M}:=\mathrm{Met}^{m}_{Y,BG}(M)
, which is a separable
C^{m}
Banach manifold.
Under (BG
3+
) (and
n\geq 5
), the boundary mean curvature

H_{g}\in C^{m-1}(\partial M)
and the renormalized mass
\mathfrak{R}_{g}\in C^{m-3}(\partial M)

are well-defined and depend
C^{m-3}
–smoothly on
g
(in particular continuously in the
C^{m}
topology).
We prove (a) and (b) by parametric transversality (Sard–Smale) applied to universal sections.

Step 1 (Generic Morse property of
H_{g}
).

Consider the universal section

\mathcal{H}:\ \mathcal{M}\times\partial M\longrightarrow T\partial M,\qquad\mathcal{H}(g,x):=\nabla_{\partial}H_{g}(x),

which is
C^{m-2}
.
We claim that
\mathcal{H}
is a submersion. Fix
(g,x_{0})\in\mathcal{M}\times\partial M
.
Choose boundary Fermi coordinates
(x^{\prime},x_{n})
centered at
x_{0}
so that
\partial M=\{x_{n}=0\}
and
x^{\prime}=0
corresponds to
x_{0}
.
In such coordinates one has the standard identity

H_{g}=-\tfrac{1}{2}\,g^{ab}\,\partial_{x_{n}}g_{ab}\quad\text{on }\{x_{n}=0\},\qquad a,b\in\{1,\dots,n-1\}.

Let
\psi\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{R}^{n-1})
and define a collar-supported symmetric
2
–tensor
h
by

h_{ab}(x^{\prime},x_{n}):=2x_{n}\,\psi(x^{\prime})\,g_{ab}(x^{\prime},0),\qquad h_{an}=h_{nn}=0.

For
|t|\ll 1
,
g_{t}:=g+th
remains a
C^{m}
metric and (since
\mathcal{M}
is open) lies in
\mathcal{M}
.
Differentiating the above formula for
H_{g_{t}}
at
t=0
yields

\left.\frac{d}{dt}\right|_{t=0}\nabla_{\partial}H_{g_{t}}(x_{0})=-(n-1)\,\nabla_{x^{\prime}}\psi(0).

Since
\nabla_{x^{\prime}}\psi(0)
can be prescribed arbitrarily, the partial differential

D_{g}\mathcal{H}_{(g,x_{0})}:T_{g}\mathcal{M}\to T_{x_{0}}\partial M
is surjective. Hence
\mathcal{H}
is a submersion.

By the Sard–Smale parametric transversality theorem, the set

\mathcal{G}_{1}:=\Big\{g\in\mathcal{M}:\ x\mapsto\nabla_{\partial}H_{g}(x)\ \text{is transverse to the zero section of }T\partial M\Big\}

is residual (hence dense) in
\mathcal{M}
. Transversality of a section is an open condition in the
C^{m}
topology,
so
\mathcal{G}_{1}
is also
C^{m}
–open. For
g\in\mathcal{G}_{1}
, the critical points of
H_{g}
are nondegenerate,
i.e.
H_{g}
is Morse. This proves (a).

Step 2 (Generic nonvanishing of
\mathfrak{R}_{g}
at critical points of
H_{g}
).

Let
E:=T\partial M\oplus\mathbb{R}
and consider the universal section

\mathcal{F}:\ \mathcal{M}\times\partial M\longrightarrow E,\qquad\mathcal{F}(g,x):=\big(\nabla_{\partial}H_{g}(x),\,\mathfrak{R}_{g}(x)\big),

which is
C^{m-3}
.
We claim that
\mathcal{F}
is a submersion at every point
(g,x_{0})
with
\mathcal{F}(g,x_{0})=0
.

Surjectivity onto the
T_{x_{0}}\partial M
component is provided by the same collar-supported variations as in Step 1.
To obtain surjectivity onto the
\mathbb{R}
component while keeping the
T\partial M
component fixed at
x_{0}
,
we exploit the explicit curvature decomposition of
\mathfrak{R}_{g}
(Definition
3.5
and
Theorem
3.15
):

\mathfrak{R}_{g}=\kappa_{1}(n)\,\Ric_{g}(\nu,\nu)+\kappa_{2}(n)\,\mathrm{Scal}_{g|\partial M}+\kappa_{3}(n)\,|\mathring{\II}|^{2},\qquad\kappa_{2}(n)>0.

Choose a conformal variation
g_{t}:=e^{2t\phi}g
with
\phi\in C^{\infty}_{c}
supported in a small collar neighborhood of
x_{0}

and satisfying, at
x_{0}
,

\phi(x_{0})=0,\qquad\nabla_{\partial}\phi(x_{0})=0,\qquad\partial_{\nu}\phi=0\ \text{on }\partial M\ \text{near }x_{0},\qquad\Delta_{\partial}\phi(x_{0})\neq 0.

Since
\mathcal{F}(g,x_{0})=0
implies
\nabla_{\partial}H_{g}(x_{0})=0
, the first-order variation of the gradient is

\left.\frac{d}{dt}\right|_{t=0}\nabla_{\partial}H_{g_{t}}(x_{0})=\nabla_{\partial}\!\left(\left.\frac{d}{dt}\right|_{t=0}H_{g_{t}}\right)(x_{0}),

because the term coming from the variation of the boundary metric multiplies
dH_{g}(x_{0})=0
and thus vanishes.
By the conformal transformation law

H_{e^{2\phi}g}=e^{-\phi}(H_{g}+(n-1)\partial_{\nu}\phi)
, our conditions
\phi(x_{0})=0
,
\nabla_{\partial}\phi(x_{0})=0
,
and
\partial_{\nu}\phi=0
near
x_{0}
imply

\left.\frac{d}{dt}\right|_{t=0}\nabla_{\partial}H_{g_{t}}(x_{0})=0.

On the other hand, the induced boundary metric transforms as
\bar{g}_{t}=e^{2t\phi|_{\partial M}}\bar{g}
, and in boundary dimension

n-1
the conformal scalar-curvature variation satisfies

\left.\frac{d}{dt}\right|_{t=0}\mathrm{Scal}_{\bar{g}_{t}}(x_{0})=-2(n-2)\,\Delta_{\partial}\phi(x_{0}).

Thus
\left.\frac{d}{dt}\right|_{t=0}\mathrm{Scal}_{g_{t}|\partial M}(x_{0})\neq 0
, and since
\kappa_{2}(n)>0
it follows that

\left.\frac{d}{dt}\right|_{t=0}\mathfrak{R}_{g_{t}}(x_{0})\neq 0,\qquad\text{while}\qquad\left.\frac{d}{dt}\right|_{t=0}\nabla_{\partial}H_{g_{t}}(x_{0})=0.

Therefore the image of
D_{g}\mathcal{F}_{(g,x_{0})}
contains
(0,1)\in T_{x_{0}}\partial M\oplus\mathbb{R}
, and together with
Step 1 we conclude
D_{g}\mathcal{F}_{(g,x_{0})}
is surjective onto
E_{x_{0}}
. This proves the submersion claim.

Applying Sard–Smale to
\mathcal{F}
, we obtain that the set

\mathcal{G}_{2}:=\Big\{g\in\mathcal{M}:\ x\mapsto(\nabla_{\partial}H_{g}(x),\mathfrak{R}_{g}(x))\ \text{is transverse to the zero section of }E\Big\}

is residual (hence dense) in
\mathcal{M}
. Since
\mathrm{rank}(E)=n
whereas
\dim(\partial M)=n-1
,
transversality forces the zero set to be empty:

\{x\in\partial M:\ \nabla_{\partial}H_{g}(x)=0\ \text{and}\ \mathfrak{R}_{g}(x)=0\}=\varnothing\qquad\text{for all }g\in\mathcal{G}_{2}.

Because
\partial M
is compact, emptiness implies a uniform separation from
0
:

\min_{\partial M}\big(|\nabla_{\partial}H_{g}|+|\mathfrak{R}_{g}|\big)>0
, hence this property is stable under small
C^{m}
perturbations.
Thus
\mathcal{G}_{2}
is
C^{m}
–open as well. This proves (b).

Step 3 (Intersection and conditional threshold compactness).

Let
\mathcal{G}:=\mathcal{G}_{1}\cap\mathcal{G}_{2}
. Then
\mathcal{G}
is
C^{m}
–open and dense in
\mathcal{M}
and satisfies
(a)–(b). The final compactness statement under

C^{*}_{\mathrm{Esc}}(M,g)=S_{\ast}
and
\min_{\mathrm{Crit}(H_{g})}\mathfrak{R}_{g}>0
is exactly
Theorem
4.29
.
∎

Remark 4.32

(On density of
\mathfrak{R}_{g}>0
versus
\mathfrak{R}_{g}\neq 0
)
.

The condition
\mathfrak{R}_{g}(x)\neq 0
at boundary critical points is a natural generic nondegeneracy condition.
By contrast, the one-sided sign condition
\mathfrak{R}_{g}(x)>0
is open but need not be dense without additional geometric
restrictions. In particular, in dimensions
n\geq 5
the local formula for the renormalized mass contains a Willmore-type term

\kappa_{3}\,|\mathring{\mathrm{II}}|^{2}
with
\kappa_{3}<0
(cf. Section
3
), so sufficiently anisotropic
boundary geometry can force
\mathfrak{R}_{g}<0
at a given point, and this sign is stable under small
C^{m}
perturbations when the
negative contribution is quantitatively large.

Finally, if
H_{g}(p)=0
and
\mathfrak{R}_{g}(p)<0
at some boundary point
p
, then the one-bubble expansion produces test functions
with Escobar quotient strictly below
S_{\ast}
for small scales, hence
C^{*}_{\mathrm{Esc}}(M,g)<S_{\ast}
. In particular, the hemisphere-threshold
hypothesis
C^{*}_{\mathrm{Esc}}(M,g)=S_{\ast}
rules out this instability.

4.5.
Existence/Morse counting; LS/flow tools

Theorem 4.33

(Quantitative reduced–gradient principle near
\mathcal{B}_{\partial,k}
)
.

Assume
(Y
{}^{+}_{\partial}
)
and
(BG
3+
)
and work on the Escobar constraint slice

\mathcal{S}:=\Big\{u\in H^{1}(M):\ \|u\|_{L^{q}(\partial M)}=1\Big\},\qquad q=2^{*}_{\partial}=\frac{2(n-1)}{n-2}.

Fix an integer
k\geq 1
and let
\mathcal{B}_{\partial,k}\cap\mathcal{S}
denote the
(slice–normalized)
k
–bubble manifold

\mathcal{B}_{\partial,k}\cap\mathcal{S}:=\Big\{\ \mathcal{U}_{\mathbf{x},\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}=t(\mathbf{x},\boldsymbol{\varepsilon})\sum_{i=1}^{k}U_{+,x_{i},\varepsilon_{i}}\ \in\mathcal{S}\ :\ (\mathbf{x},\boldsymbol{\varepsilon})\ \text{satisfy \eqref{eq:separation}}\ \Big\},

where
\mathbf{x}=(x_{1},\dots,x_{k})\in\mathrm{Conf}_{k}(\partial M)
,
\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}=(\varepsilon_{1},\dots,\varepsilon_{k})
,
and
t(\mathbf{x},\boldsymbol{\varepsilon})>0
is the slice-normalizing factor.

Equip
H^{1}(M)
with the Escobar graph inner product
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle_{E,g}
, let

\mathcal{R}_{E,g}:H^{1}(M)\to(H^{1}(M))^{\ast},\qquad\mathcal{R}_{E,g}(v)(\phi):=\langle v,\phi\rangle_{E,g},

and define the constrained gradient on
\mathcal{S}
by

\mathsf{grad}_{\mathcal{S}}\mathcal{J}_{g}(u)\ :=\ \mathcal{R}_{E,g}^{-1}\Big(D\mathcal{J}_{g}[u]\big|_{T_{u}\mathcal{S}}\Big)\in H^{1}(M),\quad T_{u}\mathcal{S}=\Big\{\phi\in H^{1}(M):\ \int_{\partial M}|u|^{q-2}u\,\phi\,d\sigma_{g}=0\Big\}.

(Here
D\mathcal{J}_{g}[u]
denotes the constrained first variation on
\mathcal{S}
in the convention
(
4.1
).)

There exist constants
\delta>0
,
\varepsilon_{0}>0
,
\Lambda_{0}\gg 1
, and
C\geq 1
(depending on
k
and
(M,g)
) such that
for every
u\in\mathcal{S}
with
u|_{\partial M}>0
and

\mathrm{dist}_{H^{1}(M)}\big(u,\mathcal{B}_{\partial,k}\big)\ <\ \delta,

the multi-bubble modulation lemma yields parameters
(\mathbf{x},\boldsymbol{\varepsilon})
with

\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}\in(0,\varepsilon_{0})^{k}
and
\Lambda(\mathbf{x},\boldsymbol{\varepsilon})\geq\Lambda_{0}
,
together with a remainder
w\in H^{1}(M)
such that

(4.36)

u=\mathcal{U}_{\mathbf{x},\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}+w,\qquad w\in\mathcal{K}_{\mathbf{x},\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}^{\perp_{E}},\qquad\int_{\partial M}\mathcal{U}_{\mathbf{x},\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}^{\,q-1}w\,d\sigma_{g}=0.

Here
\mathcal{K}_{\mathbf{x},\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}
is the
k
–bubble tangent space

\mathcal{K}_{\mathbf{x},\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}:=\mathrm{span}\Big\{\partial_{\varepsilon_{i}}\mathcal{U}_{\mathbf{x},\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}},\ \partial_{(x_{i})^{\alpha}}\mathcal{U}_{\mathbf{x},\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}:\ 1\leq i\leq k,\ 1\leq\alpha\leq n-1\Big\},

\perp_{E}
denotes orthogonality with respect to
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle_{E,g}
, and

\mathcal{U}_{\mathbf{x},\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}\in\mathcal{S}
is as above.

Define the normalized tangent vectors

Z_{i,0}:=\varepsilon_{i}\,\partial_{\varepsilon_{i}}\mathcal{U}_{\mathbf{x},\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}},\qquad Z_{i,\alpha}:=\varepsilon_{i}\,\partial_{(x_{i})^{\alpha}}\mathcal{U}_{\mathbf{x},\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}\qquad(1\leq i\leq k,\ 1\leq\alpha\leq n-1),

and their slice–tangent corrections

Z_{i,\beta}^{\top}:=Z_{i,\beta}-\Big(\int_{\partial M}|u|^{q-2}u\,Z_{i,\beta}\,d\sigma_{g}\Big)\,u\qquad(1\leq i\leq k,\ \beta=0,\dots,n-1),

so that
Z_{i,\beta}^{\top}\in T_{u}\mathcal{S}
(since
\|u\|_{L^{q}(\partial M)}=1
).
Set the reduced coordinates

\Gamma_{i,\beta}(u):=D\mathcal{J}_{g}[u]\,[Z_{i,\beta}^{\top}]\qquad(1\leq i\leq k,\ \beta=0,\dots,n-1),

where
D\mathcal{J}_{g}[u]
is the constrained first variation on
\mathcal{S}

(equivalently, the restriction of
D\mathcal{J}_{g}[u]
to
T_{u}\mathcal{S}
via (
4.1
)).

Finally, define the intrinsic
multi-bubble drift scale

(4.37)

\mathsf{Drift}_{k}(\mathbf{x},\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}):=\sum_{i=1}^{k}\varepsilon_{i}\;+\;\sum_{i\neq j}(\varepsilon_{i}\varepsilon_{j})^{\frac{n-2}{2}}\Big(\Lambda(\mathbf{x},\boldsymbol{\varepsilon})^{-1}+\frac{\varepsilon_{i}+\varepsilon_{j}}{d_{g}(x_{i},x_{j})}\Big).

(Note: In the refined regime where
H_{g}\approx 0
and
n\geq 5
, the self-drift term
\varepsilon_{i}
improves to
\varepsilon_{i}^{2}
.)

Then the constrained gradient satisfies the two-sided estimates

(4.38)

\|\mathsf{grad}_{\mathcal{S}}\mathcal{J}_{g}(u)\|_{H^{1}(M)}\ \leq\ C\Big(\|w\|_{H^{1}(M)}+\sum_{i=1}^{k}\sum_{\beta=0}^{n-1}|\Gamma_{i,\beta}(u)|+\mathsf{Drift}_{k}(\mathbf{x},\boldsymbol{\varepsilon})\Big),

and

(4.39)

\|w\|_{H^{1}(M)}+\sum_{i=1}^{k}\sum_{\beta=0}^{n-1}|\Gamma_{i,\beta}(u)|\ \leq\ C\Big(\|\mathsf{grad}_{\mathcal{S}}\mathcal{J}_{g}(u)\|_{H^{1}(M)}+\mathsf{Drift}_{k}(\mathbf{x},\boldsymbol{\varepsilon})\Big).

Moreover,

|\Gamma_{i,\beta}(u)-\Gamma_{i,\beta}(\mathcal{U}_{\mathbf{x},\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}})|\ \leq\ C\,\|w\|_{H^{1}(M)}\qquad(1\leq i\leq k,\ \beta=0,\dots,n-1).

Along the
k
–bubble manifold (i.e. for
u=\mathcal{U}_{\mathbf{x},\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}\in\mathcal{B}_{\partial,k}\cap\mathcal{S}
) one has

\Gamma_{i,0}(\mathcal{U}_{\mathbf{x},\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}})=\varepsilon_{i}\,\partial_{\varepsilon_{i}}\big(\mathcal{J}_{g}(\mathcal{U}_{\mathbf{x},\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}})\big),\qquad\big(\Gamma_{i,1},\dots,\Gamma_{i,n-1}\big)(\mathcal{U}_{\mathbf{x},\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}})=\varepsilon_{i}\,\nabla_{x_{i}}\big(\mathcal{J}_{g}(\mathcal{U}_{\mathbf{x},\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}})\big).

Proof.

We fix
k
and work throughout in the regime provided by the modulation lemma, so that
(
4.36
) holds with
\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}\in(0,\varepsilon_{0})^{k}
and

\Lambda(\mathbf{x},\boldsymbol{\varepsilon})\geq\Lambda_{0}\gg 1
.

Step 1 (Uniform linear algebra on the tangent space).

Let

\mathcal{K}^{\top}(u):=\mathrm{span}\{Z_{i,\beta}^{\top}:\ 1\leq i\leq k,\ \beta=0,\dots,n-1\}\ \subset\ T_{u}\mathcal{S}.

Since
\mathcal{U}_{\mathbf{x},\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}\in\mathcal{S}
depends
C^{1}
-smoothly on
(\mathbf{x},\boldsymbol{\varepsilon})

in the separation regime, the vectors
Z_{i,\beta}
are well-defined and satisfy uniform bounds

\|Z_{i,\beta}\|_{H^{1}(M)}\ \simeq\ 1\qquad\text{for all }(\mathbf{x},\boldsymbol{\varepsilon})\text{ with }\max_{i}\varepsilon_{i}\leq\varepsilon_{0},\ \Lambda\geq\Lambda_{0},

where the implicit constants depend only on
(M,g)
and
k
.
Moreover, by standard overlap estimates under separation,

(4.40)

\big|\langle Z_{i,\beta},Z_{j,\gamma}\rangle_{E,g}\big|\ \lesssim\ (\varepsilon_{i}\varepsilon_{j})^{\frac{n-2}{2}}\Big(\Lambda^{-1}+\frac{\varepsilon_{i}+\varepsilon_{j}}{d_{g}(x_{i},x_{j})}\Big)\qquad(i\neq j),

while the diagonal block Gram matrices

\big(\langle Z_{i,\beta},Z_{i,\gamma}\rangle_{E,g}\big)_{\beta,\gamma}

converge (after rescaling in Fermi coordinates) to the corresponding Euclidean half-space Gram matrix,
hence are uniformly invertible for
\varepsilon_{i}\leq\varepsilon_{0}
.
By (
4.40
) and
\Lambda_{0}\gg 1
, the full Gram matrix

\big(\langle Z_{i,\beta},Z_{j,\gamma}\rangle_{E,g}\big)_{(i,\beta),(j,\gamma)}

is a small perturbation of a block diagonal uniformly invertible matrix; therefore it is uniformly invertible.
Since
Z_{i,\beta}^{\top}=Z_{i,\beta}+O(\|w\|_{H^{1}})
in
H^{1}
(because

|\int_{\partial M}u^{q-1}Z_{i,\beta}|\lesssim\|u-\mathcal{U}_{\mathbf{x},\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}\|_{H^{1}}\lesssim\|w\|_{H^{1}}
),
the same conclusion holds for the Gram matrix of
\{Z_{i,\beta}^{\top}\}
after shrinking
\delta
if needed.
Consequently:

•

\mathcal{K}^{\top}(u)
has dimension
k\,n
and is complemented in
T_{u}\mathcal{S}
;

•

all norms on
\mathcal{K}^{\top}(u)
are uniformly equivalent;

•

the projection
P_{\mathcal{K}^{\top}(u)}:T_{u}\mathcal{S}\to\mathcal{K}^{\top}(u)
has operator norm bounded by
C
,
uniformly in the regime.

Step 2 (Multi-bubble coercivity on the orthogonal complement).

Let

\mathcal{K}^{\top}(u)^{\perp_{E}}:=\Big\{v\in T_{u}\mathcal{S}:\ \langle v,Z_{i,\beta}^{\top}\rangle_{E,g}=0\ \text{ for all }i,\beta\Big\}.

We claim there exists
c_{0}>0
such that, uniformly in the separation regime,

(4.41)

\|v\|_{H^{1}(M)}\ \leq\ c_{0}^{-1}\,\Big\|D(\mathsf{grad}_{\mathcal{S}}\mathcal{J}_{g})(\mathcal{U}_{\mathbf{x},\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}})[v]\Big\|_{H^{1}(M)}\qquad\forall\,v\in\mathcal{K}^{\top}(\mathcal{U}_{\mathbf{x},\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}})^{\perp_{E}}.

This is the standard
k
–bubble analogue of the single-bubble spectral gap (Theorem
2.23
),
transported through fixed-cutoff Fermi coordinates and stabilized by separation.

Step 3 (Control of
w
by the complement component of the constrained gradient).

Let
G(u):=\mathsf{grad}_{\mathcal{S}}\mathcal{J}_{g}(u)\in H^{1}(M)
.
Since
\mathcal{J}_{g}
is
C^{2}
on
\mathcal{S}
in a sign-preserving neighborhood, Taylor expansion in
H^{1}
gives

G(\mathcal{U}+w)=G(\mathcal{U})+DG(\mathcal{U})[w]\ +\ \mathcal{N}(w),\qquad\|\mathcal{N}(w)\|_{H^{1}(M)}\ \leq\ C\,\|w\|_{H^{1}(M)}^{2},

where
\mathcal{U}=\mathcal{U}_{\mathbf{x},\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}
and
C
is uniform in the regime.
Projecting onto
\mathcal{K}^{\top}(\mathcal{U})^{\perp_{E}}
and using
w\in\mathcal{K}(\mathcal{U})^{\perp_{E}}
from
(
4.36
), the spectral gap (
4.41
) yields

\|w\|_{H^{1}}\ \lesssim\ \|G(u)\|_{H^{1}}+\|G(\mathcal{U})\|_{H^{1}}+\|w\|_{H^{1}}^{2}.

Shrinking
\delta
ensures
\|w\|_{H^{1}}
is small so that the quadratic term is absorbed, yielding

(4.42)

\|w\|_{H^{1}}\ \lesssim\ \|G(u)\|_{H^{1}}+\|G(\mathcal{U})\|_{H^{1}}.

As in the Lyapunov–Schmidt residual estimates (Lemma
4.4
), one has the drift bound

(4.43)

\|G(\mathcal{U}_{\mathbf{x},\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}})\|_{H^{1}(M)}\ \lesssim\ \mathsf{Drift}_{k}(\mathbf{x},\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}),

which inserted into (
4.42
) gives the
w
–part of (
4.39
).

Step 4 (Control of the
\Gamma
–coordinates and Lipschitz dependence).

For each
(i,\beta)
,

|\Gamma_{i,\beta}(u)|=|D\mathcal{J}_{g}[u][Z_{i,\beta}^{\top}]|\ \leq\ \|G(u)\|_{H^{1}(M)}\,\|Z_{i,\beta}^{\top}\|_{H^{1}(M)}\ \lesssim\ \|G(u)\|_{H^{1}(M)},

since
\|Z_{i,\beta}^{\top}\|_{H^{1}}\simeq 1
uniformly.
Summing over
(i,\beta)
yields the
\Gamma
–part of (
4.39
).
Moreover, by
C^{1}
-smoothness of
D\mathcal{J}_{g}
and the map
u\mapsto Z_{i,\beta}^{\top}
in the modulation regime,

|\Gamma_{i,\beta}(u)-\Gamma_{i,\beta}(\mathcal{U})|\ \lesssim\ \|u-\mathcal{U}\|_{H^{1}}\ =\ \|w\|_{H^{1}},

which is the claimed Lipschitz estimate.

Step 5 (Upper bound (
4.38
)).

Decompose
T_{u}\mathcal{S}=\mathcal{K}^{\top}(u)\oplus\mathcal{K}^{\top}(u)^{\perp_{E}}
.
On
\mathcal{K}^{\top}(u)^{\perp_{E}}
, Steps 3–4 (together with (
4.43
)) give

\|G(u)\|_{H^{1};\,\mathcal{K}^{\top}(u)^{\perp_{E}}}\ \lesssim\ \|w\|_{H^{1}}+\mathsf{Drift}_{k}(\mathbf{x},\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}).

On the finite-dimensional space
\mathcal{K}^{\top}(u)
, Step 1 implies that the restriction of
G(u)
is controlled by
the coordinate vector
\big(\Gamma_{i,\beta}(u)\big)_{(i,\beta)}
, hence

\|G(u)\|_{H^{1};\,\mathcal{K}^{\top}(u)}\ \lesssim\ \sum_{i=1}^{k}\sum_{\beta=0}^{n-1}|\Gamma_{i,\beta}(u)|.

Combining the two bounds yields (
4.38
).

Step 6 (Identities along the manifold).

If
u=\mathcal{U}_{\mathbf{x},\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}\in\mathcal{B}_{\partial,k}\cap\mathcal{S}
, then the parameter derivatives
lie in
T_{u}\mathcal{S}
, hence
Z_{i,\beta}^{\top}=Z_{i,\beta}
.
By the chain rule,

\partial_{\varepsilon_{i}}\big(\mathcal{J}_{g}(\mathcal{U}_{\mathbf{x},\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}})\big)=D\mathcal{J}_{g}[u]\,[\partial_{\varepsilon_{i}}u]=D\mathcal{J}_{g}[u]\,[\varepsilon_{i}^{-1}Z_{i,0}],

and multiplying by
\varepsilon_{i}
gives
\Gamma_{i,0}(u)=\varepsilon_{i}\partial_{\varepsilon_{i}}\mathcal{J}_{g}(u)
.
The center-derivative identity is identical, using
\partial_{(x_{i})^{\alpha}}u
and assembling into
\nabla_{x_{i}}
.
∎

Corollary 4.34

(Explicit reduced–gradient expansion along a single bubble)
.

Assume
n\geq 7
,
(Y
{}^{+}_{\partial}
)
and
(BG
4+
)
.
For
u=\mathcal{U}_{x,\varepsilon}:=t(x,\varepsilon)\,U_{+,x,\varepsilon}\in\mathcal{B}_{\partial}\cap\mathcal{S}

(the case
k=1
),
Lemma
3.27
implies, uniformly in
x
on compact collars,

\Gamma_{0}(\mathcal{U}_{x,\varepsilon})=S_{\ast}\Big(\rho_{n}^{\mathrm{conf}}\,H_{g}(x)\,\varepsilon+2\,\mathfrak{R}_{g}(x)\,\varepsilon^{2}+3\,\Theta_{g}(x)\,\varepsilon^{3}\Big)+O(\varepsilon^{4}),

and

\big(\Gamma_{1},\dots,\Gamma_{n-1}\big)(\mathcal{U}_{x,\varepsilon})=S_{\ast}\Big(\rho_{n}^{\mathrm{conf}}\,\varepsilon^{2}\,\nabla H_{g}(x)+\varepsilon^{3}\,\nabla\mathfrak{R}_{g}(x)\Big)+O(\varepsilon^{4}),

where
\nabla
is the boundary gradient.

Proof.

By Theorem
4.33
(applied with
k=1
),

\Gamma_{0}(\mathcal{U}_{x,\varepsilon})=\varepsilon\,\partial_{\varepsilon}\big(\mathcal{J}_{g}(\mathcal{U}_{x,\varepsilon})\big),\qquad\Gamma_{\alpha}(\mathcal{U}_{x,\varepsilon})=\varepsilon\,\partial_{(x)^{\alpha}}\big(\mathcal{J}_{g}(\mathcal{U}_{x,\varepsilon})\big).

Differentiate the third–order expansion (
3.29
) and multiply by
\varepsilon
.
∎

Theorem 4.35

(Łojasiewicz–Simon inequality near a positive constrained critical point)
.

Assume
(Y
{}^{+}_{\partial}
)
and
(BG
3+
)
and work on the Escobar constraint slice

\mathcal{S}:=\Big\{u\in H^{1}(M):\ \|u\|_{L^{q}(\partial M)}=1\Big\},\qquad q=2^{*}_{\partial}=\frac{2(n-1)}{n-2}.

Let
u_{\ast}\in\mathcal{S}
be a
positive
constrained critical point of
\mathcal{J}_{g}
on
\mathcal{S}

(equivalently, a positive solution of the Escobar Euler–Lagrange system on
(M,g)
, normalized by

\|u_{\ast}\|_{L^{q}(\partial M)}=1
).

Then there exist
\theta\in(0,\tfrac{1}{2})
,
C>0
, and a neighborhood
\mathcal{U}_{\ast}\subset\mathcal{S}
of
u_{\ast}

of the form

(4.44)

\mathcal{U}_{\ast}:=\Big\{u\in\mathcal{S}:\ \|u-u_{\ast}\|_{H^{1}(M)}+\|u-u_{\ast}\|_{L^{\infty}(\partial M)}<\delta\Big\}

for some
\delta>0
, such that for all
u\in\mathcal{U}_{\ast}
one has the Łojasiewicz–Simon inequality

\|\mathsf{grad}_{\mathcal{S}}\mathcal{J}_{g}(u)\|_{H^{1}(M)}\ \geq\ C\,\big|\mathcal{J}_{g}[u]-\mathcal{J}_{g}[u_{\ast}]\big|^{1-\theta}.

(Here
\mathsf{grad}_{\mathcal{S}}\mathcal{J}_{g}
is the constrained gradient on
\mathcal{S}
defined via the Riesz map

\mathcal{R}_{E,g}
associated with
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle_{E,g}
, cf. (
4.1
).)

In particular, for
\delta
sufficiently small, every
u\in\mathcal{U}_{\ast}
satisfies

u|_{\partial M}\geq\frac{1}{2}\min_{\partial M}u_{\ast}>0
.

Proof sketch.

Since
u_{\ast}
is a smooth positive solution,
\min_{\partial M}u_{\ast}>0
.
Fix
\delta<\frac{1}{2}\min_{\partial M}u_{\ast}
. Then for every
u\in\mathcal{U}_{\ast}
we have

u|_{\partial M}\geq\frac{1}{2}\min_{\partial M}u_{\ast}>0
.

Let
q=2^{*}_{\partial}=\frac{2(n-1)}{n-2}
. On the positive region, the boundary Nemytskii map

u\mapsto u^{q-1}
is real–analytic as a map on
L^{\infty}(\partial M)
, hence on

H^{1}(M)\cap L^{\infty}(\partial M)
.
In local charts on
\mathcal{S}
built from the analytic constraint map

u\mapsto\|u\|_{L^{q}(\partial M)}^{q}=\int_{\partial M}u^{q}\,d\sigma_{g}
(restricted to
\mathcal{U}_{\ast}
),
the restricted functional
\mathcal{J}_{g}|_{\mathcal{S}}
is analytic.
Moreover, the linearized constrained operator at
u_{\ast}
is Fredholm of index
0

(under (Y
{}^{+}_{\partial}
)).

A Łojasiewicz–Simon theorem (see
[
52
,
17
]
) for analytic functionals on Hilbert/Banach manifolds
yields the inequality with some
\theta\in(0,\tfrac{1}{2})
.
∎

Corollary 4.36

(Constrained gradient flow: convergence and rates near a critical point)
.

Assume the hypotheses of Theorem
4.35
and let
u_{\ast}
be the positive constrained critical point there.
Let
u_{0}\in\mathcal{U}_{\ast}
and let
u(t)
solve the constrained gradient flow

\partial_{t}u\ =\ -\,\mathsf{grad}_{\mathcal{S}}\mathcal{J}_{g}(u),\qquad u(0)=u_{0}.

If
u(t)\in\mathcal{U}_{\ast}
for all
t\geq 0
, then
u(t)
converges to
u_{\ast}
in
H^{1}(M)
as
t\to\infty
.
Moreover,

\mathcal{J}_{g}[u(t)]-\mathcal{J}_{g}[u_{\ast}]\ \leq\ C\,t^{-\frac{1}{\,1-2\theta\,}}\qquad(t\to\infty),

where
\theta
is as in Theorem
4.35
.

If, in addition,
u_{\ast}
is
strictly stable on the slice
, i.e. there exists
c_{0}>0
such that

D^{2}\big(\mathcal{J}_{g}|_{\mathcal{S}}\big)[u_{\ast}]\,[v,v]\ \geq\ c_{0}\,\|v\|_{H^{1}(M)}^{2}\qquad\forall\,v\in T_{u_{\ast}}\mathcal{S},

then (after shrinking
\mathcal{U}_{\ast}
if needed) the trajectory stays in
\mathcal{U}_{\ast}
for all
t\geq 0
and
the convergence is exponential: there exist
\sigma>0
and
C>0
such that

\|u(t)-u_{\ast}\|_{H^{1}(M)}\ \leq\ C\,e^{-\sigma t},\qquad\mathcal{J}_{g}[u(t)]-\mathcal{J}_{g}[u_{\ast}]\ \leq\ C\,e^{-2\sigma t},\qquad t\to\infty.

Theorem 4.37

(Morse multiplicity at infinity from
\mathcal{W}_{k}
at
C_{\mathrm{Esc}}^{*}=S_{\ast}
)
.

Assume
(Y
{}^{+}_{\partial}
)
and
(BG
3+
)
, suppose
C^{*}_{\mathrm{Esc}}(M,g)=S_{\ast}
and
n\geq 5
.
Fix
k\geq 1
and set
q=2^{*}_{\partial}=\frac{2(n-1)}{n-2}
.
Work on the Escobar constraint slice

\mathcal{S}:=\Big\{u\in H^{1}(M):\ \|u\|_{L^{q}(\partial M)}=1\Big\}.

Fix a compact component
\mathcal{C}\subset\mathrm{Crit}(H_{g})\cap\{H_{g}=0\}
and assume the
refined regime

holds near
\mathcal{C}
so that Definition
3.5
applies (in particular
\mathfrak{R}_{g}
is defined on
\mathcal{C}
).
Define the center–only potential

\mathcal{W}_{k}(\mathbf{x})\ :=\ \sum_{i=1}^{k}\mathfrak{R}_{g}(x_{i}),\qquad\mathbf{x}=(x_{1},\dots,x_{k})\in\mathfrak{C}_{k}(\mathcal{C}):=\{\mathbf{x}\in\mathcal{C}^{k}:\ x_{i}\neq x_{j}\}.

Assume
\mathfrak{R}_{g}\neq 0
on
\mathcal{C}
and that
\mathcal{W}_{k}
is Morse on
\mathfrak{C}_{k}(\mathcal{C})
.
Assume moreover that the hypotheses of Theorem
4.33
hold at every

\mathbf{x}^{\circ}\in\mathrm{Crit}\big(\mathcal{W}_{k};\mathfrak{C}_{k}(\mathcal{C})\big)
.

Then each critical point
\mathbf{x}^{\circ}
of
\mathcal{W}_{k}
produces a distinct
k
–bubble
Palais–Smale sequence

(u_{\ell}^{(\mathbf{x}^{\circ})})\subset\mathcal{S}
for
\mathcal{J}_{g}

with ordered centers converging to
\mathbf{x}^{\circ}
and scales
\max_{i}\varepsilon_{\ell,i}\downarrow 0
, such that

\mathrm{Def}^{\mathrm{Esc}}_{g}\big(u_{\ell}^{(\mathbf{x}^{\circ})}\big)\to 0,\qquad\mathcal{J}_{g}\big[u_{\ell}^{(\mathbf{x}^{\circ})}\big]\ \longrightarrow\ k^{\,1-\frac{2}{q}}\,S_{\ast}.

(Equivalently,
\|\mathsf{grad}_{\mathcal{S}}\mathcal{J}_{g}(u_{\ell}^{(\mathbf{x}^{\circ})})\|_{H^{1}(M)}\to 0
under the Riesz identification
via
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle_{E,g}
; cf. (
4.1
).)

Consequently,

\#\{\text{$k$--bubble critical points at infinity with ordered centers in }\mathcal{C}\}\ \geq\ \#\mathrm{Crit}\big(\mathcal{W}_{k};\mathfrak{C}_{k}(\mathcal{C})\big).

Proof sketch.

Fix
\mathbf{x}^{\circ}\in\mathrm{Crit}(\mathcal{W}_{k};\mathfrak{C}_{k}(\mathcal{C}))
and choose a scale sequence

\varepsilon_{\ell}\downarrow 0
(e.g.
\varepsilon_{\ell}=\ell^{-1}
) so that the separation condition (
4.7
)
holds for
(\mathbf{x}^{\circ},(\varepsilon_{\ell},\dots,\varepsilon_{\ell}))
for all large
\ell
.
Set
u_{\ell}:=\mathcal{U}_{\mathbf{x}^{\circ},(\varepsilon_{\ell},\dots,\varepsilon_{\ell})}\in\mathcal{B}_{\partial,k}\cap\mathcal{S}
.

By Theorem
4.33
and the refined one-/multi-bubble expansions, the reduced coordinates satisfy

\Gamma_{i,0}(u_{\ell})=O(\varepsilon_{\ell})\quad\text{and}\quad(\Gamma_{i,1},\dots,\Gamma_{i,n-1})(u_{\ell})=O(\varepsilon_{\ell}^{2})\cdot\nabla\mathfrak{R}_{g}(x_{i}^{\circ})=o(1),

since
\nabla\mathfrak{R}_{g}(x_{i}^{\circ})=0
for each
i
(criticality of
\mathcal{W}_{k}
).
The drift term
\mathsf{Drift}_{k}(\mathbf{x}^{\circ},(\varepsilon_{\ell},\dots,\varepsilon_{\ell}))\to 0
as
\ell\to\infty
.
Thus (
4.38
) implies
\mathrm{Def}^{\mathrm{Esc}}_{g}(u_{\ell})\to 0
, i.e.
(u_{\ell})
is Palais–Smale.

Finally, the
k
–bubble energy quantization yields

\mathcal{J}_{g}[u_{\ell}]\to k^{1-2/q}S_{\ast}
as
\varepsilon_{\ell}\downarrow 0
.
Distinct critical points
\mathbf{x}^{\circ}
yield distinct ordered center limits, hence distinct sequences.
∎

Remark 4.38

(Finite-dimensional genericity)
.

Since
\mathfrak{C}_{k}(\mathcal{C})
is finite-dimensional, a
C^{2}
-small perturbation of
\mathcal{W}_{k}
makes it Morse.
Thus, whenever one has a perturbation mechanism that preserves the refined regime near
\mathcal{C}
and changes the reduced potential,
one may assume the Morse hypothesis in Theorem
4.37
after an arbitrarily small perturbation.

Theorem 4.39

(Topological multiplicity of
k
–bubble critical points at infinity)
.

Assume
(Y
{}^{+}_{\partial}
)
and
(BG
3+
)
, suppose
C^{*}_{\mathrm{Esc}}(M,g)=S_{\ast}
and
n\geq 5
.
Fix
k\geq 1
and set
q=2^{*}_{\partial}=\frac{2(n-1)}{n-2}
.
Let
\mathcal{C}\subset\mathrm{Crit}(H_{g})\cap\{H_{g}=0\}
be compact and assume the
refined regime
holds near
\mathcal{C}

(so Definition
3.5
applies) and that
\mathfrak{R}_{g}\neq 0
on
\mathcal{C}
.
Let
\mathfrak{C}_{k}(\mathcal{C})=\{\mathbf{x}\in\mathcal{C}^{k}:\ x_{i}\neq x_{j}\}
and

\mathcal{W}_{k}(\mathbf{x})\ :=\ \sum_{i=1}^{k}\mathfrak{R}_{g}(x_{i})\qquad(\mathbf{x}\in\mathfrak{C}_{k}(\mathcal{C})).

Assume
\mathcal{W}_{k}
is Morse on
\mathfrak{C}_{k}(\mathcal{C})
and that the reduced Palais–Smale condition holds along
balanced scales (as in Corollary
5.14
). Then for any coefficient field
\mathbb{K}
,

\#\{\text{$k$--bubble critical points at infinity with ordered centers in }\mathcal{C}\}\ \geq\ \mathrm{cat}\big(\mathfrak{C}_{k}(\mathcal{C})\big)\ \geq\ \operatorname{cupl}\big(H^{\ast}(\mathfrak{C}_{k}(\mathcal{C});\mathbb{K})\big).

Equivalently, there exist at least
\mathrm{cat}(\mathfrak{C}_{k}(\mathcal{C}))
distinct
k
–bubble
positive
Palais–Smale sequences

(u_{\ell})\subset\mathcal{S}
for
\mathcal{J}_{g}
whose ordered centers remain in
\mathcal{C}
and whose maximal scale tends to
0
,
such that

\mathrm{Def}^{\mathrm{Esc}}_{g}(u_{\ell})\to 0,\qquad\mathcal{J}_{g}[u_{\ell}]\ \longrightarrow\ k^{\,1-\frac{2}{q}}\,S_{\ast}.

Proof sketch.

This is the topological min–max/LS-category consequence of the reduced-gradient package near the
k
–bubble manifold,
as in Corollary
5.14
: the collision barrier keeps the flow away from the fat diagonal and
the reduced functional is asymptotically governed by the center-only potential
\mathcal{W}_{k}
along balanced scales.
The Morse hypothesis on
\mathcal{W}_{k}
provides the required deformation properties, and the reduced Palais–Smale
hypothesis ensures the production of distinct noncompact Palais–Smale sequences, i.e. distinct critical points at infinity.
∎

Remark 4.40
.

When
\mathcal{C}
has explicit topology (e.g. a finite union of nondegenerate closed curves),
the category of
\mathfrak{C}_{k}(\mathcal{C})
grows with
k
, yielding arbitrarily large multiplicity of

k
–bubble
critical points at infinity
at the threshold level.

Remark 4.41

(Variational landscape package at the threshold)
.

Sections
3
–
4
provide a near–threshold package:
(i) Struwe/quantization decompositions at multiples of
S_{\ast}
;
(ii) quantitative reduced expansions
\mathcal{J}_{k}
with the explicit interaction kernel
\mathsf{G}_{\partial}
;
(iii) finite–dimensional center selection via the potential
\mathcal{W}_{k}
and the associated
scale selection/obstruction laws (notably Theorem
4.25
);
and (iv) quantitative constrained-gradient control near bubbling (Theorem
4.33
)
together with Łojasiewicz–Simon dynamics near genuine positive constrained critical points
(Theorem
4.35
).

In dimensions
n\geq 5
, the package naturally splits into two complementary outputs:

•

Critical points at infinity:
under Morse/topological hypotheses on
\mathcal{W}_{k}
one produces
noncompact Palais–Smale sequences with
k
boundary bubbles (critical points at infinity),
as in Theorems
4.37
and
4.39
.

•

Selection/obstruction for genuine multi–bubble solutions:
in the refined regime with
H_{g}\equiv 0
,
the scale equations force
\mathfrak{R}_{g}(p_{i})=0
at any limiting blow-up center for a
k
–bubble critical-point
sequence with macroscopically separated, comparable scales (Theorem
4.25
);
in particular, if
|\mathfrak{R}_{g}|
is bounded away from
0
on a collar then small-scale
k
–bubble critical points
cannot occur there.

5.
Dirichlet Gagliardo–Nirenberg on compact manifolds with boundary: sharp constants, existence, and non-attainment

Let
(M,g)
be a smooth compact
n
-manifold with nonempty
C^{\infty}
boundary
\partial M
; we work with Dirichlet boundary conditions and the Hilbert space
H^{1}_{0}(M)
.
Fix

1<p\leq\frac{n+2}{n-2}\quad\text{if }n\geq 3,\qquad(\text{any }1<p<\infty\text{ if }n=2).

Define the exponents

(5.1)

\alpha\coloneqq 2-\frac{(n-2)(p-1)}{2},\qquad\beta\coloneqq\frac{n(p-1)}{2},\qquad\alpha+\beta=p+1,

and the Dirichlet Gagliardo–Nirenberg (Weinstein) functional
[
30
,
49
,
58
]

(5.2)

W_{M}(u)\;=\;\frac{\|u\|_{L^{p+1}(M)}^{p+1}}{\|u\|_{L^{2}(M)}^{\alpha}\,\|\nabla u\|_{L^{2}(M)}^{\beta}},\qquad W_{M}^{\sup}\coloneqq\sup_{u\in H^{1}_{0}(M)\setminus\{0\}}W_{M}(u).

For fixed
n
and
p
as above, define the Euclidean sharp constant

C_{\ast}(n,p)\coloneqq W_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}^{\sup}

and the Dirichlet half-space constant

C_{\ast}^{+}(n,p)\coloneqq W_{\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+}}^{\sup}.

We usually write
C_{\ast}
for
C_{\ast}(n,p)
when
n,p
are fixed. In
\mathbb{R}^{n}
the supremum is attained by positive radial Weinstein optimizers (Aubin–Talenti at the critical endpoint; see e.g.
[
34
,
55
, Ch. 1]
).

Interpolation identity.

For
n\geq 3
and
2\leq q\leq 2^{\ast}\coloneqq\tfrac{2n}{n-2}
(and for
n=2
, any
2\leq q<\infty
),

(5.3)

\frac{1}{q}=\frac{1-\theta}{2}+\frac{\theta}{2^{\ast}}\quad\Longrightarrow\quad\|u\|_{L^{q}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\leq\|u\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}^{1-\theta}\|u\|_{L^{2^{\ast}}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}^{\theta}\quad\text{with constant }1,

i.e. the estimate is scale-free; see
[
34
,
55
, Ch. 1]
. With
q=p+1
and (
5.1
), indeed
\theta(p+1)=\beta
and
(1-\theta)(p+1)=\alpha
.

5.1.
Half-space constant equals Euclidean

Lemma 5.1

(Half-space
=
Euclidean)
.

C_{\ast}^{+}(n,p)=C_{\ast}(n,p)
.

Proof (omitted).

This follows by reflection/extension and the sharp half-space inequality; see, e.g.,
[
48
]
for a direct half-space treatment.∎

5.2.
Endpoint identification and the GN–Yamabe threshold

Definition 5.2

(GN–Yamabe threshold)
.

For a smooth compact Riemannian manifold
(M,g)
with
\partial M\neq\varnothing
, define

Y_{\mathrm{GN}}(M,g)\ :=\ \frac{W_{M}^{\sup}}{C_{\ast}(n,p)}\,,

where
C_{\ast}(n,p)=W_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}^{\sup}
denotes the sharp Euclidean constant (Lemma
5.1
shows that it coincides with the half-space constant).
We usually abbreviate
C_{\ast}(n,p)
by
C_{\ast}
.

Theorem 5.3

(GN–Yamabe principle)
.

For every smooth compact
(M,g)
with
\partial M\neq\varnothing
one has:

(1)

Y_{\mathrm{GN}}(M,g)\geq 1
.

(2)

Y_{\mathrm{GN}}(M,g)>1
if and only if
W_{M}^{\sup}
is attained by a nonnegative extremal.

(3)

If
Y_{\mathrm{GN}}(M,g)=1
, then
W_{M}^{\sup}
is not attained; moreover every maximizing sequence exhibits loss of compactness: after centering in charts and scaling, a subsequence converges locally to a profile modeled on Euclidean or half-space optimizers (i.e. bubbling).

Proof sketch.

Part (i) follows from the local blow-up lower bound (Proposition
5.6
); the standard concentration-compactness/profiles argument for (ii)–(iii) uses Lions’ scheme
[
38
,
39
,
40
,
41
]
(manifold version summarized in Appendix
A.5
, see Theorem
A.15
), together with the sharp Euclidean/half-space inequality and Lemma
5.1
. Full details are deferred to the appendix.∎

Theorem 5.4

(Euclidean constant vs Sobolev constant)
.

Let
n\geq 3
and
1<p\leq\frac{n+2}{n-2}
, and let
S_{n}
be the sharp Sobolev constant in

\|u\|_{L^{2^{\ast}}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}^{2}\leq S_{n}\,\|\nabla u\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}^{2}.

Then

C_{\ast}(n,p)\ \leq\ S_{n}^{\,\beta/2},

with equality at the critical endpoint
p+1=2^{\ast}
. In particular, at
p+1=2^{\ast}
one has

C_{\ast}(n,p)\ =\ S_{n}^{\,\beta/2}\ =\ S_{n}^{\,\frac{n}{n-2}}.

This is immediate from (
5.3
) and the sharp Sobolev inequality; see
[
34
,
55
, Ch. 1]
.

Remark 5.5

(No
2^{\ast}
endpoint in
n=2
)
.

Theorem
5.4
is specific to
n\geq 3
. In
n=2
there is no
L^{2}\to L^{2^{\ast}}
Sobolev endpoint, and the correct anchor is directly the sharp Euclidean Gagliardo–Nirenberg constant
C_{\ast}(2,p)=W_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}^{\sup}=W_{\mathbb{R}^{2}_{+}}^{\sup}
, which is attained. The local chart analysis of Proposition
5.6
reaches
C_{\ast}(2,p)
up to the usual
O(\varepsilon_{\star})
geometric errors; see
[
34
,
55
, Ch. 1]
.

5.3.
Local Euclidean lower bound on
(M,g)

Proposition 5.6

(Local blow-up lower bound)
.

For every smooth compact
(M,g)
with
\partial M\neq\varnothing
, one has

W_{M}^{\sup}\ \geq\ C_{\ast}(n,p).

(Recall that we abbreviate
C_{\ast}(n,p)
by
C_{\ast}
.)

Proof.

Insert rescaled Euclidean (interior) or half-space (boundary) near-optimizers in normal/Fermi coordinates and pass to the limit; cf. the detailed expansion in Appendix
A.3
. See also
[
34
,
55
, Ch. 1]
for the standard change-of-variables estimates. The construction is dimension-free and applies also when
n=2
.∎

5.4.
Profile decomposition and threshold dichotomy (pointer)

The profile decomposition on
(M,g)
at the critical scale follows the Euclidean scheme of
[
38
,
39
]
localized in normal/Fermi charts; we will use it only as a black box, referring to Appendix
A.5
(Theorem
A.15
) for a self-contained statement on manifolds.

5.5.
Quantitative stability on
(M,g)

We record the quantitative stability we use repeatedly later. The Euclidean input is:

•

Bianchi–Egnell stability for the sharp Sobolev inequality in
n\geq 3
at the endpoint
p+1=2^{\ast}

[
8
]
, together with the identification
C_{\ast}(n,p)=S_{n}^{\beta/2}
from Theorem
5.4
, and

•

the quantitative Gagliardo–Nirenberg (and half-space) inequalities for subcritical exponents and in low dimensions (including
n=2
); see, e.g.,
[
14
,
9
,
48
]
.

We do not reprove these Euclidean results here; they are imported as black boxes.

Theorem 5.7

(Quantitative stability on
(M,g)
)
.

There exist constants
c_{n,p}>0
(coming from the Euclidean/half-space stability) and
C_{\mathrm{geo}}=C_{\mathrm{geo}}(M,g,n,p)>0
such that the following holds.

For any
u\in H^{1}_{0}(M)
with
\|u\|_{L^{2}(M)}=\|\nabla u\|_{L^{2}(M)}=1
there exists a single-bubble comparison
v^{\sharp}
(a modulation of a Euclidean interior optimizer or half-space optimizer, inserted in a normal/Fermi chart) with scale
\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_{0})
such that

\|u-v^{\sharp}\|_{H^{1}(M)}^{2}\ \leq\ c_{n,p}^{-1}\,\Big(C_{\ast}-\|u\|_{L^{p+1}(M)}^{p+1}\Big)_{+}\ +\ C_{\mathrm{geo}}\,\varepsilon_{\star},

where
\varepsilon_{\star}=\varepsilon^{2}
for interior concentration and
\varepsilon_{\star}=\varepsilon
for boundary concentration.

Proof sketch.

Work in a normal (for interior) or Fermi (for boundary) chart around a point where
u
concentrates, and denote by
u^{\flat}
the pullback of
u
to
\mathbb{R}^{n}
or
\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+}
. The Euclidean/half-space quantitative Gagliardo–Nirenberg stability results yield

\|u^{\flat}-v\|_{H^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n}_{\pm})}^{2}\ \lesssim\ \Big(C_{\ast}-\|u^{\flat}\|_{L^{p+1}(\mathbb{R}^{n}_{\pm})}^{p+1}\Big)_{+},

for some Euclidean (interior) or half-space optimizer
v
, with an implicit constant depending only on
(n,p)
.

For
n\geq 3
at the Sobolev endpoint this follows from Bianchi–Egnell
[
8
]
combined with Theorem
5.4
. For subcritical exponents and for
n=2
one uses the quantitative Gagliardo–Nirenberg inequalities in
[
14
,
9
,
48
]
. No Sobolev endpoint is used in
n=2
.

Metric and Jacobian expansions in normal/Fermi coordinates show that the pullback/pushforward maps between
(M,g)
and the model Euclidean/half-space domains are near-isometries at scale
\varepsilon
: all
L^{2}
,
\dot{H}^{1}
, and
L^{p+1}
norms agree up to an error of order
O(\varepsilon_{\star})
, with
\varepsilon_{\star}=\varepsilon^{2}
in the interior and
\varepsilon_{\star}=\varepsilon
near the boundary. Transporting the Euclidean or half-space optimizer
v
back to
(M,g)
gives the comparison bubble
v^{\sharp}
, and the Euclidean stability estimate transfers to
(M,g)
with an additional geometric error term
C_{\mathrm{geo}}\varepsilon_{\star}
. Details are given in Appendix
A.8
.∎

Corollary 5.8

(Scale–deficit laws and rigidity)
.

Let
u\in H^{1}_{0}(M)
with

\Delta_{W}:=\big(C_{\ast}-W_{M}(u)\big)_{+}\leq\delta\ll 1

and
\|u\|_{L^{2}(M)}=\|\nabla u\|_{L^{2}(M)}=1
. Then the best-fit bubble scale obeys

\varepsilon\ \lesssim\ \begin{cases}\delta^{1/2},&\text{if concentration is interior},\\
\delta,&\text{if concentration is at the boundary},\end{cases}

and
u
is
H^{1}
-close to a single bubble at rate
O(\delta^{1/2})
. This is proved in Appendix
A.8
by combining Theorem
5.7
with the explicit dependence of
W_{M}(v^{\sharp})
on the scale
\varepsilon
and then optimizing in
\varepsilon
.

Remark 5.9

(Curvature/mean-curvature selection at next order)
.

Two-term expansions of the metric and Jacobian in normal/Fermi charts show that, for a bubble inserted at
x
with scale
\varepsilon
,

C_{\ast}-W_{M}(\text{bubble})\ =\ \begin{cases}\kappa^{\mathrm{int}}_{n,p}\,\mathrm{Scal}_{g}(x)\,\varepsilon^{2}+o(\varepsilon^{2}),&x\in M^{\circ},\\[2.84526pt]
\kappa^{\mathrm{bdy}}_{n,p}\,H_{g}(x)\,\varepsilon+o(\varepsilon),&x\in\partial M,\end{cases}

with curvature coefficients
\kappa^{\mathrm{int}}_{n,p}
and
\kappa^{\mathrm{bdy}}_{n,p}
depending only on
(n,p)
and on the Euclidean/half-space profiles. Their explicit expressions in terms of moments of optimizers are given in (
5.4
)–(
5.5
); the signs depend on
(n,p)
. These expansions are valid for all
n\geq 2
.

Explicit normalizations and moments (interior and boundary).

Recall the exponents
\alpha,\beta
from (
5.1
). Let
Q
be any Euclidean optimizer for
C_{\ast}(n,p)
in
\mathbb{R}^{n}
, and fix a radial cutoff
\chi\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
with
\chi\equiv 1
on
B_{1}
; write
\chi_{R}(y)=\chi(y/R)
and set
f_{R}:=\chi_{R}Q
on
\mathbb{R}^{n}
.

Boundary reference profile.
Since
W_{\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+}}^{\sup}=C_{\ast}(n,p)
need not be attained on
\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+}
, fix once and for all a tangentially radial
Q_{+}\in\dot{H}^{1}_{0}(\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+})
with

W_{\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+}}(Q_{+})\ \geq\ C_{\ast}(n,p)-\delta_{0}

for some small but fixed
\delta_{0}>0
, obtainable for instance by truncation, reflection, and smoothing starting from Euclidean optimizers. Define
P_{R}:=\chi_{R}Q_{+}
. The moments below are finite and the limits as
R\to\infty
exist by the decay of
Q
and
Q_{+}
; see Appendix
A.3
. (The resulting curvature coefficients are independent of the particular near-optimizer chosen; this is also checked in Appendix
A.3
.)

(Interior).
Define

\mathcal{I}_{p+1}\coloneqq\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}|Q|^{p+1},\qquad\mathcal{I}_{2}\coloneqq\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}|Q|^{2},\qquad\mathcal{J}\coloneqq\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}|\nabla Q|^{2},

\mathcal{M}_{p+1}\coloneqq\frac{1}{n\,\mathcal{I}_{p+1}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}|y|^{2}|Q|^{p+1},\qquad\mathcal{M}_{2}\coloneqq\frac{1}{n\,\mathcal{I}_{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}|y|^{2}|Q|^{2},\qquad\mathcal{M}_{\nabla}\coloneqq\frac{1}{n\,\mathcal{J}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}|y|^{2}|\nabla Q|^{2}.

Let
M_{p+1}(R),M_{2}(R),M_{\nabla}(R)
be the analogous truncated second moments for
f_{R}
; by dominated convergence they converge to
\mathcal{M}_{p+1},\mathcal{M}_{2},\mathcal{M}_{\nabla}
as
R\to\infty
.

(Boundary).
Define

\mathcal{I}^{+}_{p+1}:=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+}}|Q_{+}|^{p+1},\qquad\mathcal{I}^{+}_{2}:=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+}}|Q_{+}|^{2},\qquad\mathcal{J}^{+}:=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+}}|\nabla Q_{+}|^{2},

and, for
P_{R}=\chi_{R}Q_{+}
, set

\mathcal{I}^{+}_{p+1}(R):=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+}}|P_{R}|^{p+1},\qquad\mathcal{I}^{+}_{2}(R):=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+}}|P_{R}|^{2},\qquad\mathcal{J}^{+}(R):=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+}}|\nabla P_{R}|^{2}.

Then the first vertical moments

\mathfrak{m}^{(1)}_{p+1}:=\lim_{R\to\infty}\frac{\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+}}y_{n}|P_{R}|^{p+1}}{\mathcal{I}^{+}_{p+1}(R)},\qquad\mathfrak{m}^{(1)}_{2}:=\lim_{R\to\infty}\frac{\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+}}y_{n}|P_{R}|^{2}}{\mathcal{I}^{+}_{2}(R)},

and

\mathfrak{g}^{(1)}:=\lim_{R\to\infty}\frac{\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+}}y_{n}|\nabla P_{R}|^{2}}{\mathcal{J}^{+}(R)},\qquad\mathfrak{g}^{(1)}_{\tan}:=\lim_{R\to\infty}\frac{\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+}}y_{n}|\nabla_{\tan}P_{R}|^{2}}{\mathcal{J}^{+}(R)}

exist and depend only on
(n,p)
(and not on the particular choice of
Q_{+}
).

Define the curvature coefficients

(5.4)

\kappa^{\mathrm{int}}_{n,p}\ \coloneqq\ \frac{1}{6}\Big(\mathcal{M}_{p+1}-\frac{\alpha}{2}\,\mathcal{M}_{2}-\frac{\beta}{2}\,\mathcal{M}_{\nabla}\Big),

(5.5)

\kappa^{\mathrm{bdy}}_{n,p}\ \coloneqq\ -\,\mathfrak{m}^{(1)}_{p+1}\ +\ \frac{\alpha}{2}\,\mathfrak{m}^{(1)}_{2}\ -\ \frac{\beta}{2}\Big(\frac{2}{n-1}\,\mathfrak{g}^{(1)}_{\tan}\ -\ \mathfrak{g}^{(1)}\Big).

These are exactly the coefficients appearing in the expansions stated in Remark
5.9
, and they are well-defined for all
n\geq 2
and admissible
p
.

Theorem 5.10

(Model-free observability for GN)
.

Let
(M,g)
be smooth compact with boundary,
n\geq 2
,
1<p<\frac{n+2}{n-2}
for
n\geq 3
(any
p<\infty
if
n=2
). For
x\in\partial M
define the boundary test bubble
v_{x,\varepsilon}=\Pi_{x,\varepsilon}Q_{+}
, and set
\Delta_{GN}(x,\varepsilon):=C_{\ast}-W_{M}(v_{x,\varepsilon})
.
Then in Fermi charts

\Delta_{GN}(x,\varepsilon)=\kappa_{n,p}^{\mathrm{bdy}}\,H_{g}(x)\,\varepsilon\ +\ O(\varepsilon^{2}),\qquad\kappa_{n,p}^{\mathrm{bdy}}\ \text{as in \eqref{eq:def-kappa-bdy}}.

Hence for
0<\varepsilon_{1}<\varepsilon_{2}\ll 1
,

\widehat{H}_{g}(x)\ :=\ \frac{\Delta_{GN}(x,\varepsilon_{1})-\Delta_{GN}(x,\varepsilon_{2})}{\kappa_{n,p}^{\mathrm{bdy}}(\varepsilon_{1}-\varepsilon_{2})}

satisfies
|\widehat{H}_{g}(x)-H_{g}(x)|\leq C(\varepsilon_{1}+\varepsilon_{2})
.

If
x\in M^{\circ}
and
v_{x,\varepsilon}=\Pi_{x,\varepsilon}Q
is the interior bubble, then

\Delta_{GN}(x,\varepsilon)=\kappa_{n,p}^{\mathrm{int}}\,\mathrm{Scal}_{g}(x)\,\varepsilon^{2}\ +\ O(\varepsilon^{3}),

and the two-scale estimator

\widehat{\mathrm{Scal}}_{g}(x)\ :=\ \frac{\Delta_{GN}(x,\varepsilon_{1})-\Delta_{GN}(x,\varepsilon_{2})}{\kappa_{n,p}^{\mathrm{int}}\,(\varepsilon_{1}^{2}-\varepsilon_{2}^{2})}

obeys
|\widehat{\mathrm{Scal}}_{g}(x)-\mathrm{Scal}_{g}(x)|\leq C(\varepsilon_{1}+\varepsilon_{2})
.
The constants
\kappa_{n,p}^{\mathrm{bdy}},\kappa_{n,p}^{\mathrm{int}}
are explicit profile moments (see (
5.5
), (
5.4
)).

Corollary 5.11

(2D Gauss–Bonnet from GN measurements)
.

Let
(M,g)
be a compact oriented surface with smooth boundary.
Using the first–order curvature/mean–curvature expansions in Theorem
5.10
together with the quantitative stability transfer on
(M,g)
(Theorem
5.7
), the GN measurement procedure (near–threshold fitting by intrinsic bubbles with modulation) recovers the fields
\mathrm{Scal}_{g}
on
M
and
H_{g}
on
\partial M
with quantitative error control. Consequently,

\chi(M)\;=\;\frac{1}{2\pi}\Big(\tfrac{1}{2}\int_{M}\mathrm{Scal}_{g}\,dV_{g}\;+\;\int_{\partial M}H_{g}\,ds_{g}\Big),

so the Euler characteristic
\chi(M)
is determined by GN measurements (with the same stability rate as in Theorem
5.7
).

Proof sketch.

In two dimensions
\mathrm{Scal}_{g}=2K
and
H_{g}
equals the geodesic curvature of
\partial M
. Theorem
5.10
identifies
\mathrm{Scal}_{g}
and
H_{g}
from the first–order GN coefficients, and Theorem
5.7
gives quantitative stability; Gauss–Bonnet then yields the stated formula for
\chi(M)
.
∎

5.6.
Global compactness and quantization at the hemisphere threshold

Assume throughout this subsection that
W_{M}^{\sup}=C_{\ast}
(the GN threshold). We work purely in the GN setting, using only: intrinsic bubbles
\Pi_{x,\varepsilon}Q
(interior) and
\Pi_{x,\varepsilon}Q_{+}
(boundary; Definition
A.7
), the modulation map (Lemma
2.16
), near-isometry and Jacobian control in normal/Fermi charts (Appendix
A.1
and Lemmas
A.5
,
A.8
, together with the local flat GN bound in Lemma
A.16
), the annular cutoff (Lemma
A.2
), the local curvature/mean-curvature expansion (Remark
5.9
), and the local GN stability/coercivity from Theorem
5.7
. We use the separation condition (
5.6
) for multi-bubble configurations, specifically

(5.6)

\frac{\varepsilon_{i}}{\varepsilon_{j}}+\frac{\varepsilon_{j}}{\varepsilon_{i}}+\frac{d_{g}(x_{i},x_{j})}{\min\{\varepsilon_{i},\varepsilon_{j}\}}\ \longrightarrow\ \infty\quad\text{for all }i\neq j\ \text{as }\min_{i}\varepsilon_{i}\downarrow 0.

We use the
H^{1}
inner product
\langle f,g\rangle_{H^{1}}:=\int_{M}(\nabla f\cdot\nabla g+fg)\,dV_{g}
.

For a
k
-tuple
\{(a_{i},x_{i},\varepsilon_{i})\}_{i=1}^{k}
on
\partial M
obeying (
5.6
), define the
GN reduced interaction

\mathcal{I}_{k}^{\mathrm{GN}}
by the expansion identity below (it equals the sum of the
k
self-errors, given by the one-bubble GN expansion in Fermi charts and the pairwise overlap terms arising from the GN quotient; it is
C^{1}
in parameters and
o(1)
under (
5.6
) when all
\varepsilon_{i}\downarrow 0
).

Theorem 5.12

(Global compactness and quantization (GN))
.

Let
\{u_{m}\}\subset H^{1}_{0}(M)
satisfy
\|u_{m}\|_{L^{2}(M)}=\|\nabla u_{m}\|_{L^{2}(M)}=1
and
W_{M}(u_{m})\uparrow C_{\ast}
.
Then, up to a subsequence, there exist an integer
k\geq 1
, parameters
(a_{i,m},x_{i,m},\varepsilon_{i,m})\in(0,\infty)\times\partial M\times(0,\varepsilon_{0})
with
\varepsilon_{i,m}\to 0
obeying (
5.6
), and a remainder
r_{m}\in H^{1}_{0}(M)
such that

(5.7)

u_{m}=\sum_{i=1}^{k}a_{i,m}\,\Pi_{x_{i,m},\varepsilon_{i,m}}Q_{+}\ +\ r_{m},

with the orthogonality conditions from Lemma
2.16
for every bubble parameter:

\big\langle r_{m},\partial_{a_{i}}\Pi_{x_{i,m},\varepsilon_{i,m}}Q_{+}\big\rangle_{H^{1}}=\big\langle r_{m},\partial_{\varepsilon_{i}}\Pi_{x_{i,m},\varepsilon_{i,m}}Q_{+}\big\rangle_{H^{1}}=\big\langle r_{m},\partial_{x_{i,m}^{\alpha}}\Pi_{x_{i,m},\varepsilon_{i,m}}Q_{+}\big\rangle_{H^{1}}=0.

Moreover, as
m\to\infty
,

(5.8)

C_{\ast}-W_{M}(u_{m})\ =\ \mathcal{I}_{k}^{\mathrm{GN}}\big(\{a_{i,m},x_{i,m},\varepsilon_{i,m}\}\big)\ +\ o(1),

and the remainder is controlled at the interaction scale:

(5.9)

\|r_{m}\|_{H^{1}(M)}^{2}\ \leq\ C\,\mathcal{I}_{k}^{\mathrm{GN}}\big(\{a_{i,m},x_{i,m},\varepsilon_{i,m}\}\big)\ +\ o(1).

Proof.

Step 1 (first profile at the boundary).

By the profile decomposition (Theorem
A.15
) combined with the boundary preference in Remark
5.9
, interior blow-up at level
C_{\ast}
is ruled out, hence concentration occurs on
\partial M
. Choose
(a_{1,m},x_{1,m},\varepsilon_{1,m})
by Lemma
2.16
and write

u_{m}=a_{1,m}\,\Pi_{x_{1,m},\varepsilon_{1,m}}Q_{+}+r_{1,m},\quad\text{with the orthogonality of Lemma~\ref{lem:modulation}}.

Step 2 (iterative extraction and separation).

If
\|r_{1,m}\|_{H^{1}}\to 0
set
k=1
and proceed to Step 4. Otherwise, apply the local selection to
r_{1,m}
in Fermi charts to produce a second boundary bubble with parameters
(a_{2,m},x_{2,m},\varepsilon_{2,m})
, then re-modulate jointly by Lemma
2.16
and enforce the separation (
5.6
). Iterate: after
\ell
steps

u_{m}=\sum_{i=1}^{\ell}a_{i,m}\,\Pi_{x_{i,m},\varepsilon_{i,m}}Q_{+}+r_{\ell,m},

with orthogonality for each bubble and pairwise (
5.6
).

Step 3 (GN
k
-bubble expansion and finiteness of
k
).

Using Appendix
A.1
(Jacobian control), Lemmas
A.5
–
A.8
(near-isometry of the Dirichlet part), together with the annular cutoff (Lemma
A.2
), the local flat GN bound (Lemma
A.16
), and the one-bubble Fermi expansion (Remark
5.9
), a direct computation of the GN quotient yields, for fixed
\ell
and large
m
,

(5.10)

C_{\ast}-W_{M}\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{\ell}a_{i,m}\,\Pi_{x_{i,m},\varepsilon_{i,m}}Q_{+}+r_{\ell,m}\Big)=\mathcal{I}_{\ell}^{\mathrm{GN}}(\{a_{i,m},x_{i,m},\varepsilon_{i,m}\})\ +\ Q_{\ell,m}[r_{\ell,m}]+\mathcal{R}_{\ell,m},

where
\mathcal{I}_{\ell}^{\mathrm{GN}}
is the sum of the
\ell
GN self-errors and the GN pairwise overlap terms (explicit and
o(1)
under (
5.6
)),
Q_{\ell,m}
is the second variation of
C_{\ast}-W_{M}
at the
\ell
-bubble background restricted to the orthogonal slice, and
\mathcal{R}_{\ell,m}=o\big(\mathcal{I}_{\ell}^{\mathrm{GN}}+\|r_{\ell,m}\|_{H^{1}}^{2}\big)
uniformly under (
5.6
) and
\varepsilon_{i,m}\downarrow 0
.

By Theorem
5.7
(local GN stability),
Q_{\ell,m}
is coercive on the orthogonal slice. The off-diagonal interactions between blocks are
o(1)
by (
5.6
), hence

Q_{\ell,m}[r]\ \geq\ c\,\|r\|_{H^{1}}^{2}-o(1)\,\|r\|_{H^{1}}^{2}.

Since each extracted bubble is nontrivial and
\|\nabla u_{m}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}=1
, the standard profile-decomposition argument implies that only finitely many bubbles can appear; thus the extraction stops at some finite
k
, independent of
m
.

Step 4 (remainder control and quantization).

With
k
fixed, coercivity and (
5.10
) give

C_{\ast}-W_{M}(u_{m})\ \geq\ \mathcal{I}_{k}^{\mathrm{GN}}(\cdots)+c\,\|r_{m}\|_{H^{1}}^{2}+o\big(\mathcal{I}_{k}^{\mathrm{GN}}+\|r_{m}\|_{H^{1}}^{2}\big),

which yields (
5.9
). Substituting back in (
5.10
) gives the quantization (
5.8
).
∎

Remark 5.13

(On the number of bubbles)
.

Under mild geometric conditions (e.g. a strict minimizer of the one-bubble coefficient and nonnegative pairwise GN interactions),
\mathcal{I}_{k}^{\mathrm{GN}}
is minimized at
k=1
, so any maximizing (or Palais–Smale) sequence has a single bubble. The theorem, however, applies to arbitrary near-critical families and allows
k\geq 2
when geometry permits.

Corollary 5.14

(Topological multiplicity of
k
–bubble patterns (GN track))
.

Fix
k\geq 1
. Let
\mathcal{C}\subset\partial M
be a compact set of admissible centers for the GN track (e.g. a compact component singled out by the GN leading order), and define

\mathfrak{C}_{k}(\mathcal{C})\;:=\;\{\mathbf{x}=(x_{1},\dots,x_{k})\in\mathcal{C}^{k}:\ x_{i}\neq x_{j}\}.

Under the separation regime and the GN reduction of Theorem
5.12
, we work with the amplitude-normalized reduced interaction
\mathcal{I}_{k}^{\mathrm{GN}}(\mathbf{x},\boldsymbol{\varepsilon})
, where the amplitudes
a_{i}
have been eliminated via the normalization and orthogonality conditions. Assume that the balanced-scale equations

\partial_{\varepsilon_{i}}\,\mathcal{I}_{k}^{\mathrm{GN}}(\mathbf{x},\boldsymbol{\varepsilon})\;=\;0\qquad(1\leq i\leq k)

determine scales
\varepsilon_{i}=\varepsilon_{i}(\mathbf{x})
and induce a
C^{2}
center-only reduced functional

\mathcal{W}_{k}^{\mathrm{GN}}(\mathbf{x})\ :=\ \mathcal{I}_{k}^{\mathrm{GN}}\big(\mathbf{x},\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}(\mathbf{x})\big)\quad\text{on }\ \mathfrak{C}_{k}(\mathcal{C}).

Assume
\mathcal{W}_{k}^{\mathrm{GN}}
is Morse and satisfies the Palais–Smale condition on
\mathfrak{C}_{k}(\mathcal{C})
. Then the number of distinct ordered near-threshold
k
-bubble GN patterns with centers in
\mathcal{C}
satisfies

\#\{\text{ordered $k$--bubble GN patterns near }\mathcal{C}\}\ \geq\ \mathrm{cat}\big(\mathfrak{C}_{k}(\mathcal{C})\big)\ \geq\ \text{(cup--length of }H^{\ast}(\mathfrak{C}_{k}(\mathcal{C}))\text{)}.

Proof sketch.

The GN reduction (modulation plus stability, as in Theorem
5.12
) yields, under the separation regime, a Lyapunov–Schmidt-type description with a
C^{2}
reduced functional
\mathcal{I}_{k}^{\mathrm{GN}}(\mathbf{x},\boldsymbol{\varepsilon})
and an interaction barrier near collisions. Solving the balanced-scale
equations
\partial_{\varepsilon_{i}}\mathcal{I}_{k}^{\mathrm{GN}}=0
by the implicit function theorem (using the nondegeneracy assumption) yields a
C^{1}

map
\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}(\mathbf{x})
and the center-only functional

\mathcal{W}_{k}^{\mathrm{GN}}(\mathbf{x}):=\mathcal{I}_{k}^{\mathrm{GN}}(\mathbf{x},\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}(\mathbf{x}))
on
\mathfrak{C}_{k}(\mathcal{C})
. By the envelope identity, reduced critical points coincide with critical points of

\mathcal{W}_{k}^{\mathrm{GN}}
, and each such critical point produces an ordered near-threshold
k
-bubble GN pattern via the GN reduction. Since
\mathcal{W}_{k}^{\mathrm{GN}}
is Morse and satisfies the Palais–Smale condition on
\mathfrak{C}_{k}(\mathcal{C})
, Ljusternik–Schnirelmann theory yields at least
\mathrm{cat}(\mathfrak{C}_{k}(\mathcal{C}))
distinct critical points. The cup-length bound follows in the standard way.
∎

Appendix A
Technical appendix for Section
5

Throughout,
(M,g)
is a smooth compact
n
-manifold with
n\geq 2
and smooth boundary,
H^{1}_{0}(M)
carries Dirichlet boundary conditions, and
p
is in the admissible range fixed in Section
5
. We set

\alpha\coloneqq 2-\frac{(n-2)(p-1)}{2},\qquad\beta\coloneqq\frac{n(p-1)}{2},\qquad\alpha+\beta=p+1.

We use normal coordinates in the interior and Fermi coordinates at the boundary. For the standard expansions see, e.g.,
[
34
,
55
, Ch. 1]
; for concentration-compactness
[
38
,
39
]
; the endpoint stability for
n\geq 3
is
[
8
]
; subcritical and half-space stability inputs can be taken from
[
14
,
9
,
48
]
.

A.1.
Charts and metric expansions (normal/Fermi)

In a normal chart at
x\in M^{\circ}
, with
z=\Phi_{x}(\varepsilon y)
and
|y|\leq 2R
,

\displaystyle g_{ij}(\varepsilon y)

\displaystyle=\delta_{ij}-\tfrac{1}{3}R_{ikj\ell}(x)\,\varepsilon^{2}y^{k}y^{\ell}+O(\varepsilon^{3}|y|^{3}),

\displaystyle g^{ij}(\varepsilon y)

\displaystyle=\delta_{ij}+\tfrac{1}{3}R^{i}{}_{k}{}^{j}{}_{\ell}(x)\,\varepsilon^{2}y^{k}y^{\ell}+O(\varepsilon^{3}|y|^{3}),

\displaystyle\sqrt{|g|}(\varepsilon y)

\displaystyle=1-\tfrac{1}{6}\Ric_{k\ell}(x)\,\varepsilon^{2}y^{k}y^{\ell}+O(\varepsilon^{3}|y|^{3}).

In Fermi coordinates at
x\in\partial M
(indices
a,b\in\{1,\dots,n-1\}
),

\displaystyle g_{ab}(\varepsilon y)

\displaystyle=\delta_{ab}-2\,\II_{ab}(x)\,\varepsilon y_{n}+O(\varepsilon^{2}|y|^{2}),\qquad g_{nn}=1+O(\varepsilon^{2}|y|^{2}),

\displaystyle\sqrt{|g|}(\varepsilon y)

\displaystyle=1-H_{g}(x)\,\varepsilon y_{n}+O(\varepsilon^{2}|y|^{2}),\qquad g_{an}=O(\varepsilon^{2}|y|^{2}).

A.2.
Annular Poincaré and density

Let
A_{R}=\{x:R<|x|<2R\}
and
N_{R}=A_{R/2}\cup A_{R}\cup A_{2R}
.

Lemma A.1

(Annular Poincaré)
.

For
u\in H^{1}(A_{R})
,

\int_{A_{R}}|u-\overline{u}|^{2}\leq CR^{2}\int_{A_{R}}|\nabla u|^{2}
,
with
\overline{u}=\fint_{A_{R}}u
.

Lemma A.2

(Cutoff bounds)
.

There exists
\chi_{R}\in C_{c}^{\infty}
,
\chi_{R}\equiv 1
on
B_{R}
,
\supp\chi_{R}\subset B_{2R}
,
|\nabla\chi_{R}|\leq C/R
, such that

\|(\nabla\chi_{R})u\|_{L^{2}}\leq C\|u\|_{L^{2^{\ast}}(A_{R})}\quad(n\geq 3),\qquad\|(\nabla\chi_{R})(u-\overline{u})\|_{L^{2}}\leq C\|\nabla u\|_{L^{2}(N_{R})}.

In
n=2
, replace
L^{2^{\ast}}
by
L^{q}
with any
q>2
.

Lemma A.3

(Density with annular control)
.

If
w\in\dot{H}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
, there exist
R_{m}\to\infty
,
\delta_{m}\downarrow 0
and constants
c_{R_{m}}\in\mathbb{R}
such that

\psi_{m}:=\chi_{R_{m}}\,(w\ast\rho_{\delta_{m}}-c_{R_{m}})\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n})

and
\|\nabla(\psi_{m}-w)\|_{L^{2}}\to 0
.

Proof (standard).

See
[
55
, §1.2]
. The only input is Lemma
A.1
.∎

A.3.
Local Euclidean/half-space lower bound

Proposition A.4

(Local blow-up lower bound; details of Prop.
5.6
)
.

For every smooth compact
(M,g)
with
\partial M\neq\varnothing
,
W_{M}^{\sup}\geq C_{\ast}
.

Proof.

Fix
x_{0}\in\overline{M}
and a normal/Fermi chart
\exp_{x_{0}}:B_{\rho}(0)\to M
. Choose
U^{\delta}\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
or
C_{c}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+})
with
\supp U^{\delta}\subset B_{1}
and

W_{{\mathbb{R}^{n}}^{(\pm)}}(U^{\delta})>C_{\ast}-\delta,

where
{\mathbb{R}^{n}}^{(\pm)}=\mathbb{R}^{n}
in the interior case and
{\mathbb{R}^{n}}^{(\pm)}=\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+}
in the boundary case. Set
u_{\varepsilon}(x)=\varepsilon^{-(n-2)/2}U^{\delta}(\exp_{x_{0}}^{-1}(x)/\varepsilon)
. Using the expansions in §
A.1
and the scalings

\displaystyle\|u_{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{p+1}}^{p+1}

\displaystyle=\varepsilon^{\,n-\frac{(n-2)(p+1)}{2}}\int|U^{\delta}|^{p+1}(1+O(\varepsilon^{\gamma})),

\displaystyle\|u_{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle=\varepsilon^{2}\int|U^{\delta}|^{2}(1+O(\varepsilon^{\gamma})),

\displaystyle\|\nabla u_{\varepsilon}\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\int|\nabla U^{\delta}|^{2}(1+O(\varepsilon^{\gamma})),

with
\gamma=2
(interior) or
1
(boundary), and
\alpha+\beta=p+1
,
n-\tfrac{(n-2)(p+1)}{2}-\alpha=0
, we get
W_{M}(u_{\varepsilon})\to W_{{\mathbb{R}^{n}}^{(\pm)}}(U^{\delta})
. Let
\delta\downarrow 0
.
∎

A.4.
Intrinsic pushforward and near-isometry on fixed radii

For
x\in\overline{M}
and
\varepsilon>0
, let
\Phi_{x}
be a normal (interior) or Fermi (boundary) chart. For
R<\infty
define the local blow-up on fixed balls:

\big(T^{\mathrm{loc},R}_{x,\varepsilon}v\big)(y):=\varepsilon^{\frac{n-2}{2}}\;v\big(\Phi_{x}(\varepsilon y)\big),\qquad y\in B_{2R}^{(\pm)},

where
B_{2R}^{(\pm)}=B_{2R}
in the interior and
B_{2R}\cap\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+}
at the boundary. We only evaluate norms on
B_{R}^{(\pm)}
.

Lemma A.5

(Near-isometry on fixed balls)
.

For each fixed
R<\infty
and
\varepsilon\downarrow 0
,

\displaystyle\int_{B_{R}^{(\pm)}}\big|\nabla_{y}T^{\mathrm{loc},R}_{x,\varepsilon}v\big|^{2}

\displaystyle=\big(1+o_{\varepsilon}(1)\big)\int_{B_{g}(x,\varepsilon R)}|\nabla v|_{g}^{2},

\displaystyle\int_{B_{R}^{(\pm)}}\big|T^{\mathrm{loc},R}_{x,\varepsilon}v\big|^{2^{\ast}}

\displaystyle=\big(1+o_{\varepsilon}(1)\big)\int_{B_{g}(x,\varepsilon R)}|v|^{2^{\ast}}\qquad(n\geq 3).

Errors are uniform in
x
on compact sets
[
34
,
55
, Ch. 1]
.

Definition A.6

(Cutoff pushforward on
M
)
.

Let
\chi_{R}\in C_{c}^{\infty}
with
\chi_{R}\equiv 1
on
B_{R}
,
\supp\chi_{R}\subset B_{2R}
. For
f\in H^{1}(B_{2R}^{(\pm)})
define

(\Pi^{R}_{x,\varepsilon}f)(z):=\varepsilon^{-\frac{n-2}{2}}\;\big(\chi_{R}f\big)\big(\Phi_{x}^{-1}(z)/\varepsilon\big)\ \text{on }\ \Phi_{x}(\varepsilon B_{2R}^{(\pm)}),\qquad 0\ \text{outside}.

Definition A.7

(Intrinsic pushforward)
.

Let
f\in\dot{H}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
(or
\dot{H}^{1}_{0}(\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+})
in the boundary case). Fix a cutoff
\chi_{R}
as in Definition
A.6
and set
f_{R}=\chi_{R}f
. We define

\Pi_{x,\varepsilon}f:=\lim_{R\to\infty}\Pi^{R}_{x,\varepsilon}f_{R}\quad\text{in }H^{1}_{0}(M)\text{ (interior/boundary case respectively).}

This is well defined and independent of the choice of
\chi_{R}
by Lemma
A.3
and Lemma
A.8
, and yields the
intrinsic bubble
centered at
x
with scale
\varepsilon
.

Lemma A.8

(Bilinear near-isometry)
.

For fixed
R
, if
f,g\in H^{1}(B_{2R}^{(\pm)})
and
u_{k,R}:=\Pi^{R}_{x_{k},\varepsilon_{k}}f
,
v_{k,R}:=\Pi^{R}_{x_{k},\varepsilon_{k}}g
, then

\Big|\langle\nabla u_{k,R},\nabla v_{k,R}\rangle_{L^{2}(M)}-\langle\nabla(\chi_{R}f),\nabla(\chi_{R}g)\rangle_{L^{2}({\mathbb{R}^{n}}^{(\pm)})}\Big|\leq C(R)\,\varepsilon_{\star,k}\,\|f\|_{H^{1}}\|g\|_{H^{1}},

with
\varepsilon_{\star,k}=(\varepsilon_{k}R)^{2}
(interior) or
\varepsilon_{k}R
(boundary). For
L^{2^{\ast}}
norms (
n\geq 3
) a similar estimate holds.

A.5.
Lions extraction in charts: minimal scale, non-splitting, tightness, mass transfer

In this subsection we assume
n\geq 3
and write
2^{\ast}=\frac{2n}{n-2}
. The manifold version follows the flat case
[
11
,
38
,
39
,
40
,
41
,
55
]
using Lemmas
A.5
and
A.8
and the cutoff bounds in Lemma
A.2
.

Lemma A.9

(
\varepsilon
-minimal scale)
.

Let
v_{k}\rightharpoonup 0
in
H^{1}_{0}(M)
and
c_{\star}=\tfrac{1}{2}\limsup_{k}\|v_{k}\|_{L^{2^{\ast}}}^{2^{\ast}}>0
. There exist
x_{k}\in\overline{M}
,
r_{k}\downarrow 0
such that

\int_{B_{g}(x_{k},r_{k})}|v_{k}|^{2^{\ast}}\geq c_{0}-o(1)

for some
c_{0}\in(0,c_{\star}]
.

Lemma A.10

(Centered non-splitting)
.

With
(x_{k},r_{k})
as above, for fixed
R\geq 1
,

\lim_{\Lambda\to\infty}\limsup_{k}\int_{B_{g}(x_{k},\Lambda Rr_{k})\setminus B_{g}(x_{k},Rr_{k}/\Lambda)}|v_{k}|^{2^{\ast}}=0.

Lemma A.11

(Extraction; tightness; mass transfer)
.

Set
\varepsilon_{k}:=r_{k}
and
w_{k}:=T^{\mathrm{loc},R}_{x_{k},\varepsilon_{k}}v_{k}
. Up to subsequence,

•

w_{k}\rightharpoonup w
in
H^{1}_{\mathrm{loc}}({\mathbb{R}^{n}}^{(\pm)})
with
w\in\dot{H}^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
or
\dot{H}^{1}_{0}(\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+})
;

•

w_{k}\to w
in
L^{q}_{\mathrm{loc}}
for any
2\leq q<2^{\ast}
; tightness holds on
B_{1/\Lambda}
and outside
B_{\Lambda}
;

•

\displaystyle\int_{B_{1}^{(\pm)}}|w|^{2^{\ast}}\geq c_{0}
(mass transfer).

Lemma A.12

(Chart transitions; orthogonality vs. absorption)
.

Let two channels
(x_{k}^{(j)},\varepsilon_{k}^{(j)})
and
(x_{k}^{(\ell)},\varepsilon_{k}^{(\ell)})
be given. If

\frac{\varepsilon_{k}^{(j)}}{\varepsilon_{k}^{(\ell)}}+\frac{\varepsilon_{k}^{(\ell)}}{\varepsilon_{k}^{(j)}}+\frac{d_{g}(x_{k}^{(j)},x_{k}^{(\ell)})}{\min\{\varepsilon_{k}^{(j)},\varepsilon_{k}^{(\ell)}\}}\to\infty,

then for all compactly supported test profiles
f,g
,

\langle\nabla\Pi^{R}_{x_{k}^{(j)},\varepsilon_{k}^{(j)}}f,\ \nabla\Pi^{R}_{x_{k}^{(\ell)},\varepsilon_{k}^{(\ell)}}g\rangle\to 0.

Otherwise, after modulation one channel absorbs the other (profiles are related by a similarity).

Proposition A.13

(Gradient Pythagoras)
.

If
u_{k}=u+\sum_{j=1}^{J_{0}}\Pi_{k}^{(j)}w^{(j)}+r_{k}^{(J_{0})}
(finite expansion), then

\|\nabla u_{k}\|_{2}^{2}=\|\nabla u\|_{2}^{2}+\sum_{j\leq J_{0}}\|\nabla w^{(j)}\|_{L^{2}({\mathbb{R}^{n}}^{(\pm)})}^{2}+\|\nabla r_{k}^{(J_{0})}\|_{2}^{2}+o(1).

Lemma A.14

(Vanishing of critical and subcritical tails)
.

\displaystyle\lim_{J\to\infty}\limsup_{k}\|r_{k}^{(J)}\|_{L^{2^{\ast}}}=0
and, for
2\leq p+1<2^{\ast}
,

\displaystyle\lim_{J\to\infty}\limsup_{k}\|r_{k}^{(J)}\|_{L^{p+1}}=0
.

Theorem A.15

(Profile decomposition on
(M,g)
)
.

Every bounded sequence in
H^{1}_{0}(M)
admits a decomposition

u_{k}=u+\sum_{j=1}^{J_{0}}\Pi_{k}^{(j)}w^{(j)}+r_{k}^{(J_{0})}\qquad(\text{each finite }J_{0}),

with gradient Pythagoras and vanishing tails as above.

A.6.
Local flat GN in shrinking charts

Lemma A.16

(Local flat GN)
.

Fix
x\in\overline{M}
and
R>1
. There exist
\varepsilon_{0}>0
and
C=C(M,g,R)
such that for all
0<\varepsilon<\varepsilon_{0}
and
f\in H^{1}_{0}(M)
supported in
B_{\varepsilon R}(x)\cap M
,

\int_{M}|f|^{p+1}\,dV_{g}\ \leq\ C_{\ast}\,(1+C\,\varepsilon_{\star})\,\Big(\int_{M}|f|^{2}\Big)^{\alpha/2}\Big(\int_{M}|\nabla f|^{2}\Big)^{\beta/2},

with
\varepsilon_{\star}=\varepsilon^{2}
(interior) or
\varepsilon
(boundary).

Proof.

Work in the chart at
x
. Compare the three norms with their Euclidean/half-space counterparts using §
A.1
and Lemma
A.2
, and apply the sharp Euclidean/half-space Gagliardo–Nirenberg inequality with constant
C_{\ast}
to the rescaled pullback. Metric and Jacobian errors produce the multiplicative factor
(1+C\,\varepsilon_{\star})
.∎

A.7.
Quantitative stability input (Euclidean and half-space)

We use the following Euclidean/half-space stability, normalized by
\|f\|_{L^{2}}=\|\nabla f\|_{L^{2}}=1
:

(A.1)

C_{\ast}-\|f\|_{L^{p+1}}^{p+1}\ \geq\ c_{n,p}\,\operatorname{dist}_{H^{1}}\big(f,\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{eucl}}\big)^{2},

where
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{eucl}}
is the manifold of optimizers under translations and dilations.
At the endpoint
p+1=2^{\ast}
(for
n\geq 3
) this is
[
8
]
; for subcritical GN see
[
14
,
9
]
; for half-space sharpness and stability inputs see
[
48
]
.

A.8.
Stability transfer to
(M,g)
(details for Thm.
5.7
)

Fix
u
with
\|u\|_{2}=\|\nabla u\|_{2}=1
and let
v^{\sharp}=a\,\Pi_{x,\varepsilon}Q
be the
H^{1}
-closest bubble (by modulation). In the chart, define

u^{\flat}:=\chi_{R}\,T^{\mathrm{loc},2R}_{x,\varepsilon}u,\qquad v^{\flat}:=\chi_{R}\,T^{\mathrm{loc},2R}_{x,\varepsilon}v^{\sharp}.

By Lemma
A.5
and the cutoff bounds,

\big|W_{M}(u)-W_{{\mathbb{R}^{n}}^{(\pm)}}(u^{\flat})\big|\leq C(R)\varepsilon_{\star}.

Normalize
\tilde{u}
by amplitude/dilation in the chart to enforce
\|\tilde{u}\|_{2}=\|\nabla\tilde{u}\|_{2}=1
; then, by (
A.1
),

C_{\ast}-W_{{\mathbb{R}^{n}}^{(\pm)}}(u^{\flat})\geq c_{n,p}\,\operatorname{dist}_{H^{1}}(\tilde{u},\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{eucl}})^{2}.

Undo the normalization using the Lipschitz control of the normalization map and the near-isometry:

\operatorname{dist}_{H^{1}}(\tilde{u},\tilde{v})\lesssim\|u-v^{\sharp}\|_{H^{1}}+O(\varepsilon_{\star}),

where
\tilde{v}
is the similarly normalized version of
v^{\flat}
. Combining:

C_{\ast}-W_{M}(u)\ \geq\ c\,\|u-v^{\sharp}\|_{H^{1}}^{2}-C\,\varepsilon_{\star},

which is the core estimate behind Theorem
5.7
.

A.9.
Isoperimetry
\Rightarrow
Sobolev
\Rightarrow
GN

Let
I_{M}(v)
be the (relative) isoperimetric profile. Maz’ya’s BV/coarea characterization of sharp Sobolev constants (
[
46
, Chs. 9 & 12]
) gives
S_{M}\leq S_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}
whenever
I_{M}\geq I_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}
. For
n\geq 3
, interpolating with constant 1 between
L^{2}
and
L^{2^{\ast}}
as in (
5.3
) yields the GN bound with Euclidean anchor at the endpoint.

A.10.
Eigenvalue and capacity lower bounds

Let
\lambda_{1}(M)
denote the first Dirichlet eigenvalue.

Proposition A.17

(Eigenfunction competitor)
.

Let
\phi_{1}
be the first Dirichlet eigenfunction with
\|\phi_{1}\|_{2}=1
. Then

W_{M}^{\sup}\geq W_{M}(\phi_{1})=\|\phi_{1}\|_{p+1}^{p+1}\,\lambda_{1}(M)^{-\beta/2}\geq\mathrm{Vol}(M)^{1-\frac{p+1}{2}}\lambda_{1}(M)^{-\beta/2}.

Proposition A.18

(Small Dirichlet window)
.

Let
M=N\setminus\overline{D}
inside a closed
(N,g)
. As
\Cap_{g}(D)\to 0
(
n\geq 3
),

\lambda_{1}(M)\asymp\tfrac{\Cap_{g}(D)}{\mathrm{Vol}(N)}

and hence
W_{M}^{\sup}\gtrsim\Cap_{g}(D)^{-\beta/2}
.
In
n=2
,
\lambda_{1}(M)\asymp\tfrac{|\log\mathrm{diam}(D)|^{-1}}{\mathrm{Vol}(N)}
and
W_{M}^{\sup}\gtrsim|\log\mathrm{diam}(D)|^{\beta/2}
.

Proposition A.19

(Capacity blow-up)
.

The conclusions of Proposition
A.18
imply that as
\Cap_{g}(D)\to 0
one has
W_{M}^{\sup}\to\infty
(for
n\geq 3
), and in
n=2
as
\mathrm{diam}(D)\to 0
one has
W_{M}^{\sup}\to\infty
at the stated logarithmic rate.

Proof sketch.

Upper bound: test with the capacitary potential; lower bound: Maz’ya Poincaré-capacity inequality on
N
(
[
46
, Ch. 12]
); capacity comparability in charts
[
35
, Thm. 2.2.1]
.∎

Corollary A.20

(GN near–threshold rules out capacitary degenerations)
.

Let
(M_{j},g_{j})
be a sequence of compact manifolds with smooth boundary and uniformly bounded geometry. Suppose there exists
\delta_{0}>0
such that

W_{M_{j}}^{\sup}\ \leq\ (1+\delta_{0})\,C_{\ast}\qquad\text{for all }j.

Then the family cannot develop arbitrarily small Dirichlet windows (capacitary punctures): such degenerations would force
\lambda_{1}(M_{j})\to 0
by Proposition
A.18
and hence
W_{M_{j}}^{\sup}\to\infty
by Proposition
A.19
, contradicting the uniform upper bound on
W_{M_{j}}^{\sup}
.

Proof.

If a subsequence developed
\Cap_{g_{j}}(D_{j})\to 0
(or, in
n=2
,
\mathrm{diam}(D_{j})\to 0
) in the sense of Proposition
A.18
, then Proposition
A.19
would give
W_{M_{j}}^{\sup}\to\infty
along that subsequence, contradicting the assumed uniform near-threshold bound. Hence such capacitary degenerations cannot occur.
∎

Appendix B
Applications to diffusion and dispersive dynamics

We record four consequences of the Dirichlet GN theory developed in Section
5
and the GN appendix (Appendices
A.3
,
A.5
,
A.8
):
(i) two GN forms tailored to fast diffusion (FDE), (ii) an entropy-entropy production (EEP) inequality on
(M,g)
with Euclidean-leading constants, (iii) decay and extinction-time lower bounds, (iv) ground states and geometric selection for focusing NLS. Throughout
n\geq 2
.

B.1.
Two GN forms useful for FDE

A. Variable
v=u^{\frac{m+1}{2}}
(entropy-Fisher coupling).

Let
0<m<1
and set
v:=u^{\frac{m+1}{2}}
. Applying GN with
q=2
and
r=\tfrac{2}{m+1}
(interpolation identity as in
[
34
,
55
, Ch. 1]
) gives

(B.1)

\|v\|_{L^{2}(M)}\;\leq\;C_{m}(M,g)\,\|\nabla v\|_{L^{2}(M)}^{\theta_{m}}\,\|v\|_{L^{r}(M)}^{\,1-\theta_{m}},\qquad\theta_{m}=\frac{mn}{mn+2}\in(0,1).

Equivalently,

\int_{M}u^{m+1}\,dV_{g}\;\leq\;C_{m}(M,g)\,\Big(\int_{M}\big|\nabla u^{\frac{m+1}{2}}\big|^{2}\,dV_{g}\Big)^{\theta_{m}}\Big(\int_{M}u\,dV_{g}\Big)^{(m+1)(1-\theta_{m})}.

Along concentrating families,
C_{m}(M,g)=C_{m}^{\mathrm{eucl}}(n)\,(1+O(\varepsilon_{\star}))
with the geometric error
\varepsilon_{\star}
and the first-order curvature dependence as in Appendix
A.3
(normal/Fermi expansions).

B. Variable
g=u^{m}
(dissipation coupling).

Let
g:=u^{m}
,
q:=1+\tfrac{1}{m}
,
r:=\tfrac{1}{m}
. The Euclidean GN inequality (and half-space counterpart) yields (see, e.g.,
[
14
,
9
,
48
]
)

\|g\|_{L^{q}}\ \leq\ C^{\mathrm{eucl}}(n,q,r)\,\|\nabla g\|_{L^{2}}^{\theta}\,\|g\|_{L^{r}}^{1-\theta},\quad\frac{1}{q}=\theta\Big(\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{n}\Big)+\frac{1-\theta}{r}.

A direct computation gives

(B.2)

\alpha_{n,m}:=\frac{\theta q}{2}=\frac{mn}{2mn+2-n},\qquad\beta_{n,m}:=\frac{(1-\theta)q}{r}=\frac{m(n+2)+2-n}{2mn+2-n}.

For
n\geq 3
this GN line stays inside Sobolev’s range when
m\geq\tfrac{n-2}{n+2}
(so that
q\leq 2^{\ast}
); no restriction is needed for
n=2
.

B.2.
EEP on
(M,g)
and Euclidean-leading refinement

Proposition B.1

(EEP on
(M,g)
; Euclidean-leading refinement)
.

Let
0<m<1
,
u\geq 0
,
u\in L^{1}(M)
, and
u^{m}\in H^{1}_{0}(M)
. If
n\geq 3
, assume
m\geq\tfrac{n-2}{n+2}
(so that the GN line in Paragraph B lies in the Sobolev range). Then

(B.3)

\int_{M}u^{m+1}\,dV_{g}\ \leq\ C_{n,m}(M,g)\,\Big(\int_{M}|\nabla(u^{m})|^{2}\,dV_{g}\Big)^{\alpha_{n,m}}\Big(\int_{M}u\,dV_{g}\Big)^{\beta_{n,m}},

with
\alpha_{n,m},\beta_{n,m}
in (
B.2
).
Moreover, if
u_{\varepsilon}
concentrates with best-fit scale
\varepsilon
(as in Theorem
5.7
), then for
n=2
, and for
n\geq 3
assuming
m\geq\tfrac{n-2}{n+2}
,

(B.4)

\int_{M}u_{\varepsilon}^{m+1}\,dV_{g}\ \leq\ C^{\mathrm{eucl}}(n,q,r)^{\,q}\,\Big(\int_{M}|\nabla(u_{\varepsilon}^{m})|^{2}\,dV_{g}\Big)^{\alpha_{n,m}}\Big(\int_{M}u_{\varepsilon}\,dV_{g}\Big)^{\beta_{n,m}}\,\big(1+O(\varepsilon_{\star})\big).

Proof sketch.

For (
B.4
), insert the blown-up function in a normal/Fermi chart and use the near-isometry/cutoff calculus from Appendix
A.8
, then apply the Euclidean GN; the geometric error is
O(\varepsilon_{\star})
uniformly in the center. For (
B.3
), cover
M
by finitely many charts and absorb overlaps; cf.
[
34
,
55
, Ch. 1]
.∎

B.3.
Fast diffusion: decay and extinction-time lower bounds

Consider

(B.5)

\partial_{t}u=\Delta_{g}(u^{m})\quad\text{in }M,\qquad u|_{\partial M}=0,\qquad 0<m<1.

For smooth solutions (or weak solutions with the standard integrations by parts) one has

(B.6)

\frac{d}{dt}\int_{M}u\,dV_{g}\;=\;\int_{\partial M}\partial_{\eta}(u^{m})\,d\sigma_{g}\ \leq\ 0,\qquad\frac{d}{dt}\int_{M}u^{m+1}\,dV_{g}\;=\;-(m+1)\int_{M}|\nabla(u^{m})|^{2}\,dV_{g}.

Corollary B.2

(Bernoulli-type decay for the entropy)
.

Let
E(t):=\int_{M}u(t)^{m+1}\,dV_{g}
and
M(0):=\int_{M}u(0)\,dV_{g}
. Assume that
\alpha_{n,m}\in(0,1)
(this holds for all
0<m<1
when
n=2
, and for
n\geq 3
whenever
m>\tfrac{n-2}{n}
). Then

E^{\prime}(t)\ \leq\ -\,\kappa_{n,m}(M,g)\,E(t)^{\,1/\alpha_{n,m}},\quad\kappa_{n,m}(M,g)=(m+1)\,C_{n,m}(M,g)^{-1/\alpha_{n,m}}\,M(0)^{-\beta_{n,m}/\alpha_{n,m}},

and hence

E(t)\ \leq\ \Bigl(E(0)^{-\frac{1-\alpha_{n,m}}{\alpha_{n,m}}}+\frac{1-\alpha_{n,m}}{\alpha_{n,m}}\,\kappa_{n,m}(M,g)\,t\Bigr)^{-\frac{\alpha_{n,m}}{1-\alpha_{n,m}}}.

In particular, for
n=2
one has
\alpha_{2,m}=\tfrac{1}{2}
and
E(t)\lesssim(1+t)^{-1}
.

Proof.

Combine the second identity in (
B.6
) with (
B.3
) and the monotonicity of the mass in the first identity of (
B.6
).∎

Proposition B.3

(Extinction-time lower bound)
.

Let
T_{\rm ext}
be the extinction time (if finite). Let
\phi_{1}>0
solve
-\Delta_{g}\phi_{1}=\lambda_{1}(M)\phi_{1}
with
\phi_{1}|_{\partial M}=0
and
\|\phi_{1}\|_{L^{1}(M)}=1
. Then

T_{\rm ext}\ \geq\ \frac{\Big(\int_{M}u(0)\,\phi_{1}\,dV_{g}\Big)^{1-m}}{(1-m)\,\lambda_{1}(M)}.

Proof.

Set
y(t)=\int_{M}u(t)\,\phi_{1}\,dV_{g}
. Using (
B.5
) and integrating by parts,

y^{\prime}(t)=-\int_{M}\nabla(u^{m})\cdot\nabla\phi_{1}\,dV_{g}=-\lambda_{1}(M)\int_{M}u^{m}\,\phi_{1}\,dV_{g}\ \geq\ -\lambda_{1}(M)\,y(t)^{m},

where Jensen (concavity of
s\mapsto s^{m}
) is applied with respect to the probability
\phi_{1}\,dV_{g}
. Solve the ODE:
y^{\prime}(t)\geq-\lambda_{1}y(t)^{m}
.∎

B.4.
Boundary concentration and selection by mean curvature

The first-order boundary curvature expansion for Dirichlet GN bubbles (proved in Appendix
A.3
) implies that near-extinction boundary layers select critical points of
H_{g}
and prefer maxima or minima depending on the sign of the first-order coefficient.

Theorem B.4

(Boundary selection)
.

Let
u
solve (
B.5
), and assume the GN inequality for the variable
g=u^{m}
is in the range of Paragraph B (for
n\geq 3
, this means
m\geq\tfrac{n-2}{n+2}
). Suppose there exist
t_{k}\uparrow T
,
x_{k}\in\partial M
,
\varepsilon_{k}\downarrow 0
such that

\varepsilon_{k}^{-\frac{n-2}{2}}\,[u(\Phi_{x_{k}}(\varepsilon_{k}\,\cdot),t_{k})]^{m}\ \rightharpoonup\ Q_{+}\quad\text{in }H^{1}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+}),

where
Q_{+}
is a half-space Dirichlet GN bubble as in Appendix
A.3
.
Then any accumulation point
x_{\ast}
of
(x_{k})
lies in
\mathrm{Crit}(H_{g})
. Moreover, the sign of the first-order
H_{g}
-coefficient in the boundary expansion determines a bias toward maxima or minima of
H_{g}
.

Proof sketch.

Apply the modulation at the best-fit scale for the variable
g=u^{m}
(Theorem
5.7
and Appendix
A.8
), insert the boundary expansion from Appendix
A.3
, and differentiate with respect to the center; this enforces
\nabla H_{g}(x_{k})=O(\varepsilon_{k})
.∎

B.5.
Focusing NLS: threshold, ground states, and selection

Consider the focusing NLS on
(M,g)
with Dirichlet boundary,

i\partial_{t}\psi+\Delta_{g}\psi+|\psi|^{p-1}\psi=0,\qquad\psi|_{\partial M}=0,

with
1<p<\frac{n+2}{n-2}
for
n\geq 3
(any
1<p<\infty
if
n=2
). Standing waves
\psi(t)=e^{i\omega t}u(x)
solve

-\Delta_{g}u+\omega u=u^{p},\qquad u\in H^{1}_{0}(M),\ u\geq 0.

Theorem B.5

(Ground states from the GN-Yamabe threshold)
.

If
Y_{\mathrm{GN}}(M,g)>1
, then a positive solution exists (a ground state, minimizing the energy at fixed mass). If
Y_{\mathrm{GN}}(M,g)=1
, then on smooth bounded
\Omega\Subset\mathbb{R}^{n}
no ground state exists and maximizing sequences for
W_{\Omega}
lose compactness by bubbling.

Proof sketch.

Attainment at
Y_{\mathrm{GN}}>1
and threshold non-attainment are contained in the GN-Yamabe principle (Theorem
5.3
); the Euler-Lagrange reduction gives the stationary equation. Bubbling at the threshold follows from the profile compactness in Appendix
A.5
(Theorem
A.15
).∎

Proposition B.6

(Selection of concentrating ground states)
.

Let
u_{\varepsilon}
be a family of positive stationary solutions concentrating at scale
\varepsilon\downarrow 0
. Then any interior limit point is a critical point of
\mathrm{Scal}_{g}
, and any boundary limit point is a critical point of
H_{g}
. The sign of the corresponding first-order coefficients in the interior/boundary expansions (Appendix
A.3
) selects maxima or minima.

Proof sketch.

As in Theorem
B.4
, use the interior/boundary expansions and the modulation orthogonality from Theorem
5.7
.∎

B.6.
Summary of exponents for FDE use

For quick reference:

\theta_{m}=\frac{mn}{mn+2}\quad\text{in \eqref{eq:GN-FDE-v-app}},\qquad\alpha_{n,m}=\frac{mn}{2mn+2-n},\quad\beta_{n,m}=\frac{m(n+2)+2-n}{2mn+2-n}\ \text{in \eqref{eq:EEP-exponents-app}}.

All constants are Euclidean-leading with
O(\varepsilon_{\star})
geometric corrections along concentrating families (Appendix
A.8
); for arbitrary data, the constants depend only on
(M,g)
. For background on GN interpolation and sharpness on
\mathbb{R}^{n}
/
\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+}
see
[
34
,
55
,
48
]
, and for quantitative stability and flows see
[
8
,
14
,
9
]
.

Appendix C
Profile moments and coefficients for Theorem
3.15

Let
Q
be the (tangentially radial) half-space optimizer on
\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+}
(Cayley pullback from
\mathbb{S}^{n}_{+}
),
normalized as in §
2
. Fix a radial cutoff
\chi\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
,
\chi\equiv 1
on
B_{1}
,
set
\chi_{R}(y)=\chi(y/R)
, and
P_{R}:=\chi_{R}Q
.

Second
y_{n}^{2}
-moments.

Define the (normalized) tangential and total
y_{n}^{2}
-moments

\mathcal{M}_{2,\tan}(R):=\frac{\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+}}y_{n}^{2}\,|\nabla_{\tan}P_{R}|^{2}\,dy}{\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+}}|\nabla P_{R}|^{2}\,dy},\qquad\mathcal{M}_{2,\mathrm{all}}(R):=\frac{\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+}}y_{n}^{2}\,|\nabla P_{R}|^{2}\,dy}{\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+}}|\nabla P_{R}|^{2}\,dy}.

(For
n\geq 5
these moments have cutoff-independent limits as
R\to\infty
by dominated convergence; in
n=4

they exhibit the expected logarithmic divergence, consistent with the borderline nature of second order.)

Define also the boundary
L^{2}
trace moment

\mathcal{T}(R):=\frac{\displaystyle\int_{\{y_{n}=0\}}|P_{R}(y^{\prime},0)|^{2}\,dy^{\prime}}{\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}_{+}}|\nabla P_{R}|^{2}\,dy}.

Coefficient formulas.

The constants in (
3.23
) are explicit linear combinations of convergent radial moments of
Q

(determined by the
y_{n}^{2}
and
|y^{\prime}|^{2}
coefficients in the Fermi expansions (
3.2
)–(
3.3
)
and the boundary Jacobian (
3.4
)).
In particular, the traceless second-fundamental-form coefficient
\kappa_{3}(n)
admits the representation

\kappa_{3}(n)=\lim_{R\to\infty}\,\mathbf{c}_{n}\Big(\frac{3}{n-1}\,\mathcal{M}_{2,\tan}(R)-\frac{1}{2}\,\mathcal{M}_{2,\mathrm{all}}(R)\Big),\qquad\mathbf{c}_{n}>0.

Moreover, by the half-space moment identity (Lemma
3.2
),

\lim_{R\to\infty}\mathcal{M}_{2,\tan}(R)=\frac{1}{2}\lim_{R\to\infty}\mathcal{M}_{2,\mathrm{all}}(R),

and therefore

\kappa_{3}(n)=\lim_{R\to\infty}\,\mathbf{c}_{n}\cdot\frac{4-n}{2(n-1)}\,\mathcal{M}_{2,\mathrm{all}}(R)\ <\ 0\qquad(n\geq 5).

This matches the sign asserted (and used) after Proposition
3.7
and Theorem
3.15
.

References for expansions.

We use the Fermi expansions and Jacobian identities as in
[
34
, §2]
,
[
55
, Ch. 1]
.
Escobar’s hemisphere normalization is from
[
27
]
.
